# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file. Entries carry a `(YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM)` timestamp; see CONTRIBUTING.md for the convention.

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **The POPULATED disassembly panel is rendered, and it found a defect on the first pass** — `src/components/__tests__/DisassemblyPanel.dom.test.tsx`, **30 tests, 24 negative controls**. CLAUDE.md's "Not verified" list named the virtualized rows, `DisassemblyRows`, `DisassemblyToolbar`, `InsnContextMenu`, `JumpArrows`, `DisassemblyMinimap` and `InstructionDetail` as never rendered by anything; the sibling suite reached only `DisassemblyView`'s four early-return branches. **THE DEFECT: the arrow keys cannot cross a separator row, in either direction, permanently** — `rowAddress` answers null for a `{ kind: "separator" }` row and the ArrowDown/ArrowUp branches dispatch only when it does not, with no skip and no retry, so nothing about the state changes and the next press does the same thing. Measured against the real view: fourteen consecutive ArrowDowns from the `jmp` leave the cursor where it started; five consecutive ArrowUps from the row below it likewise. A **label** row is not a wall (it carries its function's address), so this is separators alone — inserted after every ret/retn/jmp/int3 not immediately followed by a label, which in a real listing walls off everything below the first function tail. Left in place and filed as **`peek-a-bin-a5sw`**, with two `it.fails` cases stating what the view should do and a third pinning today's behaviour so the pair cannot be satisfied by a handler that has merely stopped responding. What else is now covered: rows carry their address (padded to the image's pointer width), bytes and mnemonic; labels are named and honour a rename; the separator rule's *both* arms, positionally; the `×N` xref affordance; the cursor highlight; **`JumpArrows`' forward/backward classification** (arithmetic over estimated row offsets, never pixels); the toolbar's section/span/count, its Bytes toggle, its filter *dimming* rows rather than hiding them, and Graph/Decompile gated on a detected function; the non-executable `.rdata` path, which renders `DataRow`s and **asks the worker for nothing and builds none**; the keyboard — repeated ArrowDown, clamping, PageDown, `b`, `i` — with an unfocused control, since `handleKeyDown` is on the pane and not on window; the context menu opening on the row right-clicked, its four conditional items, and a dispatch and a clipboard write attributed to that row; and **the render step of `peek-a-bin-56q`**, which had never been executed — `InstructionDetail` names a calling convention for an x86 image and says nothing whatever for an ARM64 one, the two mounts differing in the COFF machine word and nothing else. **Two controls came back inert and were sharpened rather than dropped**: freezing `showBytes` hit the toolbar's prop and not the rows', and — the interesting one — **removing `currentIndex` from `handleKeyDown`'s 37-entry dependency array changes nothing observable**, because `currentAddress`, `search` and `addressHistory` all co-vary with the cursor; five entries must go together before the handler goes stale, at which point exactly one test reddens. So the AST guard in `disasmHandlerDeps.test.ts` and these are complementary and neither subsumes the other. `src/test/domSetup.ts` gains a stub `ResizeObserver` (jsdom has none, and three components construct one from an effect where a throw tears the tree down) and an **opt-in** `stubLayoutRect()`, because `virtual-core` renders **zero** rows unless the scroll element's `offsetHeight` is non-zero. **Virtualization is a stand-in, not the real thing**: every element reports one size, `scrollTop` is permanently 0 and the stub observer never fires, so the range is computed once from offset 0 — which rows are windowed in, whether `overscan` is right, whether `scrollToIndex` works and whether anything is *visible* stay unanswered, and the minimap mounts and never paints. No application code changed (2026-08-24 16:35)
- **`peek-a-bin-qvv`'s render count re-taken against the REAL `DisassemblyView`, and the harness was right** — `contextRenderCount.dom.test.tsx` measured **two full-tree renders per arrow key** against a transcription, saying so in its own docstring because "nothing has yet driven the populated panel a render count would have to be counted over". Now something does: bystander consumers mounted beside the real view under the same providers, with the real component doing the dispatching, and the number is **exactly 2** — for consumers that read neither cursor field as well as for the status-bar stand-in. The dispatch sequence matches the transcription too (`SET_ADDRESS`, then `SET_CURRENT_INSTRUCTION` and `SET_CURRENT_BLOCK` batched into the second pass), and disabling the cursor effect in the real component takes it to 1, so the second render is provably that effect's. **The harness stays and is still worth its ~0 cost** — it isolates the reducer and the contexts from everything else in a 1600-line component and runs in milliseconds — but **the real view is now the authority** for the number, and the harness's own "this is a transcription" caveat is what the two together are for. **Count is still not cost**: what a render of a virtualized list and a dagre graph costs needs the DevTools Profiler in a real browser, and that is unchanged. No application code changed (2026-08-24 16:35)
- **The five never-rendered views are rendered now — `AddressBar`, `CFGView`, `Sidebar`, `HexView`, `FileLoader`** — five suites, **93 tests and 29 negative controls, 28 of which discriminate**. The one that does not is a finding in itself: removing `FileLoader`'s `getStepStatus` arm for `analysisPhase === "failed"` changes no output at all, here or in principle, because no `ANALYSIS_STEPS` entry lists that phase so `activeStepIndex` is -1 and the very next line already returns `"pending"` — the arm is redundant and its comment describes a job the line below it was doing. The control was re-aimed at the `"ready"` arm, which is load-bearing. None of these was ever *blocked* the way `DisassemblyView` was; they simply had no test. What only a renderer can see: **the tab bar's digits are checked against the tab bar itself** — `VIEW_TABS` feeds two separate `.map`s, one writing `(i + 1)` into each button's `title` and one building `TAB_KEYS`, so the test reads the digit out of each button and presses it rather than repeating a list, which is the duplication `peek-a-bin-t40b` removed; a reversed map, an off-by-one, a `slice(0, 8)` and a locally re-spelled label each redden it. **`peek-a-bin-b3jn`'s third surface** — the tab bar must stop saying "Loading engine..." once `disasmFailed` is set, an *exclusivity* between two sibling branches that no source scrape can check; the control reproducing the bead reddens exactly one test. **`peek-a-bin-bo3b` at its third site** — the sidebar's skeleton is driven from `ANALYSIS_IN_PROGRESS`, and reverting to the old hand-written phase chain reddens exactly the test that iterates the record's terminal phases. **The CFG is asserted as a graph** — dagre never measures the DOM, taking node sizes from the font size, so the block set, the edge set, each edge's colour and dagre's coordinates are exactly what a browser gets; each edge is attributed to the block it leaves by matching its `M x,y` against the blocks' own bottoms, **because a bare colour count could not see two edge types swapped in `EDGE_COLORS`** — the first version of that test was green under the swap and was sharpened until it was not. **`HexView`'s byte search** runs `parseBytePattern` and `findBytePatternMatches` over a real parsed PE end to end, wildcards included, and distinguishes "no matches" from "not a pattern". **`FileLoader` renders `state.error`**, which is the premise `peek-a-bin-b3jn` turns on and which `App.tsx` asserts twice in prose, plus the `File` third argument of `onFile` that `peek-a-bin-ex2`/`peek-a-bin-736` rest on — supplied on the drop and browse paths, absent on the recents path, "by construction rather than by accident" and never checked. `src/test/browserApiStubs.ts` is new: jsdom implements neither `ResizeObserver` nor `matchMedia`, both of which `HexView`'s entropy effect constructs, and the stub is documented as buying **nothing whatever** about measured widths. **Two virtualized lists are out of reach and each file says so as an assertion** — the sidebar's function rows and the hex grid render *zero* rows under jsdom, so nothing here is evidence about either, and neither is the entropy strip, which gates on a width that is a constant 0. No application code changed (2026-08-24 16:25)
- **All six modal dialogs' own contents are rendered now, closing the largest remaining item on CLAUDE.md's "Not verified" list** — the first component pass reached `Modal`, `ModalBackdrop`, `useDismissOnOutsideClick` and `StatusBar` and left "five of the six modals' own contents, the command-palette listbox" unrendered. Five suites now cover `CommandPalette`, `GoToAddressModal`, `SettingsModal`, `BatchRenameModal`, `AIReportPanel` and `KeyboardShortcuts` — **87 tests, 49 negative controls, 48 discriminating** — sharing `src/components/__tests__/appStateHarness.tsx` (a context provider pair plus a real `buildMinimalPE64` + `parsePE` fixture; named outside vitest's include glob so it is imported, not collected). What only a renderer can see: **the palette's listbox is wired, not merely computed** — `listboxIds.test.ts` pins the id arithmetic over strings, while a dangling `aria-activedescendant` is silent, so the live attribute is resolved against the document and checked to land on the row carrying `aria-selected="true"` (the control, ids minted from a different list name, reddens five tests); **`peek-a-bin-v2u` section 3's "Tab once must not walk 60 result rows"**, a claim about three decisions at once that no one of them proves; **which booleans each dialog passes to `accidentalDismissAllowed` at which moment** — Settings, a running batch rename and a streaming report refuse Escape and render no backdrop, a *failed* batch rename and a *finished* report accept both, and all six keep a visible Cancel/Close, which is what makes the refusal WCAG-legitimate; **the `key` on each of `BatchRenameModal`'s three Modals**, whose comment says that without it a progress→review transition drops focus to `<body>`, and deleting the keys reddens exactly that test; **`GoToAddressModal`'s reverse section mapping**, the only one in the app and previously untested by anything; **the Ghidra "Test Connection" round trip** over a stubbed `fetch` (version string, the server's own `detail` on a refusal, transport failure, disabled in flight — nothing reaches a real server); and **that `SHORTCUT_GROUPS` reaches the screen**, where the existing scrape only reads the exported data. **One control came back inert and is recorded in the test rather than tuned away** — `initialFocusRef` is inert on the palette, whose search field is also the dialog's first focusable control, and load-bearing on `GoToAddressModal`, where three mode buttons come first. **One test was found overclaiming and reworded**: `activeDescendantId`'s past-the-end clause is reachable only in a render `user.type` flushes past, so it stays the pure test's. `src/test/domSetup.ts` gains a no-op `scrollIntoView` (jsdom has none, and the palette calls it from an effect where a throw tears the tree down) — it buys the effect running, and **nothing about scrolling**. Two stale docstrings saying "there is no React renderer in this repo" corrected. **jsdom is not a browser and this closes none of `peek-a-bin-v2u`**, which a browser pass drove on 2026-08-24; what these add is that those findings cannot silently regress. Not reached: `SettingsModal`'s Theme-tab tab *order* (Tailwind is out of the test config, so `className="hidden"` carries no `display: none` and the hidden file input is visible to `focusableWithin` here and not in a browser), and rendering any dialog in its real context. No application code changed (2026-08-24 15:30)
- **`DisassemblyView` is mounted by a test — the largest component in the tree, and until now not mountable at all** — `src/components/__tests__/DisassemblyView.dom.test.tsx`. Seven cases over the four early-return branches: nothing rendered with no file open, the unsupported-architecture notice (amber heading, and the parser-derived tabs on screen as *buttons* rather than prose, so `PARSER_DERIVED_TABS` and `availableTabs` are checked against each other in the DOM), the engine-unavailable notice in red, and — the assertion the file exists for — **the order between the notice and the `!disasmReady` spinner**, which `analysisNotice.test.ts` could only reach by asserting that two regexes appear in a given order *in this file's source*. `StatusBar`'s test closed one half of that pair on 2026-08-24; this closes the other, and the source-order guards stay, since they also cover a branch no state here reaches. The losing branch is asserted to still render on its own (an order test whose loser can never appear is not testing an order), and no `Worker` is built by any of them. **Two negative controls, both decisive**: dropping `engine-unavailable` from the notice arm fails exactly the two engine tests and nothing else, and restoring eager `Worker` construction fails the file at **collection** — `ReferenceError: Worker is not defined`, "no tests" — so it provably measures the change below. **Mounting is not testing**: the populated panel is untouched, so the virtualized rows, the toolbar, the context menu, the jump arrows, the minimap, the CFG and the decompile panel have still never rendered (peek-a-bin-z8h1) (2026-08-24 15:10)
- **The `cs_insn` ABI has an oracle** — `src/disasm/capstoneReader.ts` hard-codes the bundled capstone build's struct layout (`address` @8, `size` @16, `bytes` @18, `mnemonic` @42, `opStr` @74, stride 240), and a capstone-wasm bump can change it **silently** into plausible garbage on every instruction. `src/disasm/__tests__/capstoneReader.test.ts` decodes hand-written sequences and deterministic pseudo-random bytes — swept the way production sweeps, since one `cs_disasm` over random bytes stops at the first bad byte — through both readers and compares every field. It cannot read vacuously (non-zero counts asserted) and it is **negative-controlled at nine offsets**: `fastCapstoneHandle` takes its `CsInsnAbi` as an argument for that reason alone, and each perturbation is detected by 1092-1911 differing rows rather than by escaping through a throw. A *runtime* self-check was refused: it would be a new way for startup to fail, on the decoder the whole tool is downstream of, to catch a change that only ever arrives with a `package.json` edit. The drift guard in `capstoneWindow.test.ts` is widened to match — no `"cs_disasm"`/`_cs_disasm` and no `loadCapstone(` under `src/` outside `capstoneReader.ts`, each checked in both directions so a rename cannot make it read clean by matching nothing (peek-a-bin-fdi8) (2026-08-24 14:15)
- **A React renderer, and the first component tests this project has ever had** — jsdom 28, `@testing-library/react` 16 and `@testing-library/user-event` 14 are installed, and five `*.dom.test.tsx` suites render React: **`Modal`** (Tab and Shift+Tab cycling in both directions and never reaching the page behind, Escape honoured *and* refused for a non-dismissible dialog, `stopPropagation` so it does not also mean "navigate back" underneath, focus returned to the trigger, the backdrop kept out of the tab order, `aria-modal` and the `label`/`labelledBy` split, and the nested body scroll lock releasing only when the *last* dialog closes), **`useDismissOnOutsideClick`** (attach, detach — pointer and Escape separately — inside-vs-outside, the `mousedown`-before-`click` ordering that a toggle outside the ref depends on, and the ref indirection that stops an inline arrow re-attaching the listener), and **`StatusBar`** (the analysis notice's *render step*, which `analysisNotice.test.ts` could previously only reach by asserting the order of two regex matches in the source). This closes the largest item on CLAUDE.md's "Not verified" list, which opened "Nothing has rendered a component". Every assertion is negative-controlled by perturbing the component and confirming the right test — and only that test — fails; **two controls came back inert and the tests were sharpened rather than kept**, one of which was passing for the wrong reason (a hook whose listener is never removed still declines to fire, because React nulls the ref on unmount). **jsdom is not a browser**: no layout, so `HTMLElement.offsetParent` is a constant `null` there and is supplied by a documented stand-in in `src/test/domSetup.ts` that the focus trap depends on; no browser focus algorithm, no screen reader. `peek-a-bin-v2u` is untouched (2026-08-24 13:52)
- **`peek-a-bin-qvv`'s render count is measured — exactly TWO full-tree renders per arrow key** — that bead says MEASURE FIRST and recorded the measurement as unobtainable without a renderer. `src/hooks/__tests__/contextRenderCount.dom.test.tsx` settles the count half against the real `appReducer` and the real contexts: every context consumer renders twice per cursor move, including the four of five that read neither field the second render exists to deliver. Negative-controlled — disable the cursor effect and it reads **1**, so the second render is provably `DisassemblyView`'s status-bar effect. Also measured: rebuilding `rows` without moving the cursor costs a full-tree render, because neither reducer branch has the no-op check `SET_OMITTED_PASSES` has. Three drift guards keep the harness tied to the real source (the effect and its dependency array, the single reader of `currentInstruction`/`currentBlock`, the absence of any `React.memo`), each negative-controlled. **It is a harness, not `DisassemblyView`** — that component imports a module constructing a `Worker` singleton at load, and jsdom has none — and **count is not cost**, which still wants the DevTools Profiler on a real binary. No application code changed (2026-08-24 13:52)
- **`build/domTestNaming.test.ts`** — component tests opt into jsdom per file, with an environment docblock and an import of the shared setup, and forgetting either fails quietly: without the setup, `offsetParent` stays jsdom's constant `null`, `focusableWithin` returns `[]` for every dialog and a focus-trap test goes **vacuously green**. The guard checks both, plus the reverse direction (a DOM-rendering test hiding under an ordinary name) and that the setup has not been promoted back into `setupFiles`. Five negative controls (2026-08-24 13:52)
- **`npm run corpus:replycost -- <pe>` — what the worker's REPLY costs, and the four-row table `peek-a-bin-rjt` measured with a bench that was then LOST** — `corpus/replyCloneCost.ts`. That bead records a measured decision not to pack the reply's instruction bytes and ends "Bench: scratchpad bench-reply.mjs"; two staleness audits then confirmed no such file existed anywhere outside `node_modules`, so its table was a claim nobody could re-take — CLAUDE.md's own `peek-a-bin-02fa` hazard, and its standing lesson "when you build an oracle to verify a change, land the oracle". **The decision is untouched; what changed is that it now has a base.** Six rows over the same objects: clone as-is (A), transfer every per-insn buffer (B), pack into one buffer and transfer (C), the same plus the re-slice the return trip forces (C+), the no-`bytes` floor (D), and the return trip itself — with `A − D` printed because it is the entire budget any scheme here competes for, and most of a reply is object and string overhead rather than bytes. **The recorded table survives**: measured idle at `488ddde` at the bead's own 500k instructions, A 1640 → **1794 ms**, B 80568 → **80128 ms**, C 1085 → **1219 ms**, D 789 → **892 ms** — row B agreeing to within **0.5%** on a figure of 80 seconds. Three things the recorded table did not have. **Row B is strongly superlinear**, per-buffer cost rising 5.7 → 164 µs over N = 5000 → 500000, an exponent of about **1.7** — a stronger claim than the single 80568 ms, because it means a large transfer list's harm grows with the image rather than being a fixed tax, which is what makes `prepareBinaryArgs` being top-level only structural. **C+ and not C is the row to read**, confirming the bead's own caveat with a number: on a 669 KiB-`.text` `go` image C saves 103.6 ms of a 463.5 ms reply and C+ saves 92.7 ms, and on the four corpus binaries C+ saves 4.9–8.6 ms of a ~45 ms reply. And **the bead's premise about provenance has moved without mattering** — since `peek-a-bin-iqzu`, `gridScan` serves almost every instruction with an exact-size `.slice()` rather than capstone-wasm's padded 24-byte buffer (155531 of 155531 on the `go` image; only 214 of t32's 18045 still from Capstone), and control 2 measures that this changed the reply's cost not at all, its *sign* being unstable between runs. Four negative controls, **two of which came back inert and were rebuilt** — control 3 twice, the second time because a ratio threshold made its verdict depend on machine load (17x idle, 5x beside the test suites); it is now judged on the claim being controlled, that the tax tracks the section rather than N. A stopwatch and a census, never a gate; writes no artifacts. **NOT VERIFIED**: nothing spawns a real `Worker`, so `structuredClone` in one process stands in for `postMessage` across two — it serialises *and* deserialises on one thread, over-stating what the main thread blocks for, so every row is an upper bound (`peek-a-bin-rjt`) (2026-08-24 13:45)
- **A worker RPC timeout is no longer reported as a failed analysis** — `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` is one 5-minute budget for every method (correctly: the worker is serial, so a cheap call legitimately queues behind a multi-minute `hybridDisassemble`), and every rejection in App's analysis chain reached the user as `analysisPhase: "failed"` — exactly what a truncated or corrupt file produces. The two call for opposite responses, so the watchdog now mints a **`WorkerTimeoutError`** (`src/workers/requestTimeout.ts`, a leaf so the budget and the type have readers outside the module that constructs the `Worker` singleton), the new pure **`analysisRejection()`** routes it to a third terminal phase **`"timed-out"`** with its message kept verbatim, and `analysisNotice()` gains a sixth kind **`"analysis-timed-out"`** — `isFault: true`, ranked below `"engine-unavailable"` (an `init()` that never answers is itself watchdogged, and its remedy is a page reload) and above `"analysis-failed"`, and the only fault kind whose `unavailableTabs` is empty, because `buildAllXrefs` is the chain's last stage so a timeout there leaves a complete disassembly on screen. A phase rather than a flag beside `"failed"`: a phase is single-valued and `RESET` returns it to `"idle"`, where a parallel boolean would need clearing twice over and a stale one would report the next file's genuine parse failure as a timeout. **Never fired on any binary here** — `detectFunctions` reaches 300 s at roughly 225 MiB of code and nothing that large exists on this machine — so it is verified by fixtures, ten negative controls and reading, and has never been rendered; corpus artifacts byte-identical (peek-a-bin-meai) (2026-08-24 04:20)

### Fixed
- **The graph view's "this address is not code, hand back to linear" rule existed TWICE, byte for byte** — comment included — in `DisassemblyView.tsx` and in `hooks/useDisassemblyKeyboard.ts`, which was extracted out of it. Both re-rolled the VA-to-section lookup and both spelled the predicate as a bare `0x20000000` rather than `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE`, which is precisely what `pe/sections.ts`'s own header says it exists to end: *"the 'which section holds the code?' predicate was written out by hand at seven call sites, none of which referenced the named flag."* These were the eighth and ninth. Now `sectionAtVirtualAddress`, `isAddressInCodeSection` and `isAddressOutsideCode` in that module. **The last two are deliberately not negations of one another**: an address in NO section is neither, and answering `true` there would switch the view on an address nothing is known about — an asymmetry both call sites already had, spelled `sec && !(sec.characteristics & 0x20000000)`. The predicate also **widens** to `isCodeSection`, i.e. `.text` OR the exec flag, which is what `findCodeSection` uses to decide where the disassembler works — so a `.text` section lacking the flag now keeps the graph view instead of being handed back as non-code, which is the reading the rest of the tool already takes. Six tests, two negative controls, each failing exactly its own test: restoring the plain negation fails only the asymmetry test, and restoring the bare flag fails only the `.text`-without-exec test. `npm run corpus` byte-identical over four binaries — the two new functions have no caller outside the UI (2026-08-24 15:55)
- **One notice rendered amber and red at the same time, and the renderer found it on its first pass** — `AnalysisNotice.isFault` exists so red-versus-amber is decided in one place, and `App.tsx`'s four render sites were converted to read it precisely because `kind === "analysis-failed"` is a hand-written predicate a new kind joins on the wrong side of silently. **A fifth site was missed.** `StatusBar.tsx:269` still spelled it by hand, and two kinds had since joined on the wrong side: `"engine-unavailable"` and `"analysis-timed-out"` are both `isFault: true`, so a dead engine or a timed-out run rendered **amber** in the status bar while the same notice rendered **red** in App's banner — one notice, two colours, on screen at once. Now `notice.isFault`, which is the last hand-written spelling of that predicate under `src/`. Pinned by three cases in `src/components/__tests__/StatusBar.dom.test.tsx` with an `isFault: false` control beside them, the third stated as the **invariant** rather than as three cases so a seventh kind is covered the day it is added; all three were written as `it.fails` against the defect and turn green on the one-line fix. **No static instrument here could have seen this** — both spellings compile and type-check, and the two sites disagree only once rendered (`peek-a-bin-n7q1`) (2026-08-24 14:05)

### Changed
- **`CLAUDE.md` split into a summary and a long-form record — 643 KB to 195 KB, ~25% of a session's context window to ~7%** — the file is loaded into every agent session, and 81% of it was the Gotchas and Verification sections, whose entries had grown into full post-mortems: the rule, then the measurement transcript, the per-binary four-tuples, the negative-control enumeration and the history of every superseded figure. The evidence is **preserved verbatim** in three new documents — `docs/gotchas.md` (369 KB), `docs/verification.md` (159 KB) and `docs/decompiler-ir.md` (56 KB) — each keeping the same entries in the same order as the summary section it belongs to, so an entry lines up with its full record. Nothing was deleted: the split was made by extraction and diffed against the original before the summaries were written. What stays in `CLAUDE.md` is the rule, the file and symbol names that make it navigable, one clause on what breaks if it is undone, and every "measured and refused, do not re-attempt" verdict — the three things a session needs before it reads code. What moved out is everything a session needs only when it is about to re-measure something. Also audited for currency: the Commands, Source Layout, Architecture and Conventions sections were rewritten against the tree rather than trimmed, and Conventions no longer hard-codes the test counts its own rule forbids. The Beads block is untouched, byte for byte, hash included. Gates on the result: `npm run check` exit 0 at the documented 70 warnings / 3 infos, `typecheck` clean, 4129 + 230 tests passing, `build` clean. (2026-08-24 18:13)
- **The disasm worker is built on first use, not on import** — `src/workers/disasmClient.ts` exports a singleton whose *constructor* ran `new Worker(new URL("./disasm.worker.ts", …))`, so merely importing the module spawned a thread and started its Capstone WASM load. Two costs, and the second was the one that mattered: a session that opens no file paid for both anyway, and because jsdom has no `Worker` the import **threw**, which is why CLAUDE.md listed `DisassemblyView` as "not mountable at all here". Construction moves into a private `ensureWorker()`, converging on `metricsClient.ts`, which has always been lazy — two clients, one lifecycle rule. Every path that posts goes through it: the only reads of the field were the constructor and `send`'s `postMessage`, and there is no `terminate`/`dispose`. The four methods that are pure client-side bookkeeping — `setImage`, `registerSourceBlob`, `invalidateCache`, `invalidateDecompileCache` — deliberately do **not** construct, and `App` calls the first two on every parse, so making any of them post would put the thread back at load time by another door. The field is assigned last, so a throw leaves it null and the next request retries rather than inheriting a half-built worker; and because `send` builds inside its existing `try`, an environment with no `Worker` rejects the *request* instead of throwing at the caller — which is exactly what lets a component test mount a view that posts. **Timing cost is one render pass**: `App`'s first effect calls `init()`, so construction moves from module evaluation to the first effect flush, before anything can be asked of the worker; no warm-up call is offered, since that would be production API existing only to restore an import-time side effect. Five new tests pin the laziness (nothing built at import, exactly one across many requests, none for the four state-only methods, a clean rejection with no `Worker` at all, and retry after a failed construction). **Corpus byte-identical** — it drives `FileSession`, which never touches this client, so it is the control: `diff -r` over the whole artifact tree finds only the label line and the artifact path, `compare.mjs` "byte-identical throughout" with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` and verdict "no regression". Stale docstrings in `requestTimeout.ts`, `analysisNotice.ts`, `llm/decompileTarget.ts` and two DOM tests corrected — each said the client constructs at module scope, and `requestTimeout.ts`'s leaf rule now rests on import weight rather than on impossibility (peek-a-bin-z8h1) (2026-08-24 15:10)
- **Capstone instructions are marshalled by hand, for ~3x off the load's dominant term** — capstone-wasm's per-instruction cost is **83-86% JS marshalling and ~16% decode** (measured at `488ddde` over eight images: a full `Capstone.prototype.disasm` is 3.09-3.81 µs/instruction against 0.51-0.60 µs for `cs_disasm` plus every `ccall` and zero per-instruction JS), and the single dominant component is `readStruct`'s `fields.reduce((obj, f) => ({ ...obj, [f.name]: v }), {})` — a computed key defeats V8's fast object path and costs ~1.5-1.8 µs/instruction, about three times the decoder. New **`src/disasm/capstoneReader.ts`** reads each `cs_insn` straight out of the emscripten heap and is a drop-in because `CapstoneHandle` is structural: it slots in *under* `createScan`, whose windowing and engine-death probe are untouched. It also owns the bootstrap — `loadCapstoneModule` is the one place in `src/` allowed to call `loadCapstone` (a singleton: a second caller populates nothing), and `capstoneHandle()` is the single point choosing between the fast reader and the dependency's, so retention is optional and a lost race costs speed and never correctness. The `transfer.ts` rule that refused `peek-a-bin-9a8` is satisfied by a wide margin here: the check is one integer comparison against a whole-file memcpy, and the handle is re-read per call rather than standing in for a remembered answer, so that bead's stale-hit class cannot arise. `npm run corpus` byte-identical (control: it drives `FileSession`, which has no worker), `npm run corpus:arm64` 51/51 green (`peek-a-bin-736`) (2026-08-24 04:20)

### Changed
- **The SEH32 prologue head is read lazily, and 76–85% of that pass's Capstone calls stop happening** — `headInstructions` decoded `MAX_SEH32_HEAD_INSNS` (8) instructions at every candidate function start before `seh32PrologImmediates` looked at any of them, and that rule stops at the first instruction that is neither padding nor a pushed immediate — nearly every function's second. It is a pull now (`HeadReader` in `src/disasm/seh32.ts`), so the rule sees the same instructions in the same order and the ones it never asks for are simply not decoded — output-neutral by construction, which is what matters because this feeds `interiorBranchedOverStarts`' fifth withdrawal admission and therefore function boundaries. `seh32-relation` **15.30/14.17/97.29/262.30 → 3.29/3.51/16.33/39.94 ms** on w32/t32 and two Windows/x86 `go` builds, i.e. 8.3–17.1% of PE32 detection down to 1.4–4.3%; x64 untouched (the `is64` arm builds an empty map and never reads a head). `npm run corpus` byte-identical on all four binaries, `corpus:arm64` 51/51 (`peek-a-bin-6dv3`) (2026-08-24 04:55)

It was the second-largest member of a decompile request after `peek-a-bin-9gc9` removed the two big arrays: 1.369 ms for 1641 rows on a 669 KiB-`.text` `go` image, 19% of a request, against 0.001 ms for the one row that applies (measured at 755ea94). - **A hybrid image has two exception tables and `pe/pdata.ts` reads one of them — settled against documentation, and the two hybrid machine constants are removed because one of them was WRONG rather than merely unreachable** — `peek-a-bin-c71x`, following `peek-a-bin-3ucw`'s finding that neither 0xA641 nor 0xA64E reaches a linked image's machine field. `3ucw` left open whether the x64 path is the right reading of an ARM64EC image's `.pdata`; it is, and the reason is that an ARM64EC image holds native A64 code following x64 conventions *and* genuine x64 code, with each half in its own table: `IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_EXCEPTION` carries **x64** 12-byte entries and the CHPE `ExtraRFETable` carries **ARM64** 8-byte ones, while an ARM64X image (marked 0xAA64) has them the other way round and the **ARM64X dynamic-value relocations swap both** when it is loaded into an x64/EC process. So the exception directory always holds the table of the architecture the image presents itself as, `ExtraRFETable` always holds the other, and `parsePdata`'s machine-keyed schema is **right for every hybrid case and incomplete for all of them**. Three independent sources, read directly rather than summarised: lld's COFF writer (`// ARM64EC (but not ARM64X) contains x86_64 exception table in data directory`, the `pdata`/`hybridPdata` split by `chunk->getMachine() == AMD64`, and the `IMAGE_DVRT_ARM64X_FIXUP_TYPE_VALUE` relocations over `EXCEPTION_TABLE` and `offsetof(chpe_metadata, ExtraRFETable)`, [Writer.cpp](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/blob/main/lld/COFF/Writer.cpp)); Wine's loader, which routes by *code range* rather than by image — `RtlLookupFunctionTable` answers `ExtraRFETable` when `RtlIsEcCode(pc)` and the data directory otherwise, and the x64 `RtlLookupFunctionEntry` delegates an EC pc to the ARM64 one searching `ARM64_RUNTIME_FUNCTION` ([unwind.c](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/dlls/ntdll/unwind.c)); and the [Arm64EC ABI page](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/arm/arm64ec-abi), which requires dynamically added EC unwind entries to "be in Arm64 format" and whose worked entry-thunk listing is ARM64 unwind codes. `isArm64Machine` is gone: 0xA641 would have meant an ARM64EC image, whose directory is the *x64* table, so that arm would have read 12-byte entries at an 8-byte stride — the `peek-a-bin-kwc` desynchronisation `parsePdata` opens by warning about, reached from the other direction — while 0xA64E's intention was right and is already served by 0xAA64, the word an ARM64X image actually carries. **Reading the second table is deliberately not attempted**: `chpeMetadataPointer` has never been observed non-zero on any file here (at `11408ac`, t64/w64 have no load-config directory, the ARM64 pair read 0, the PE32 pair declare a structure too short), so a consumer would be unverifiable in both directions; the offsets a future implementer needs (`ExtraRFETable` 0x40, `ExtraRFETableSize` 0x44) and the schema rule (the **complement** of the machine word) are recorded instead. Output-neutral and pinned by test rather than by gate, since neither constant reaches an image: `npm run corpus` byte-identical (`diff -r` finds only the label and artifact-path lines; `compare.mjs` "no regression", `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, emitted C byte-identical over 1072 functions), `corpus:arm64` 51/51 with the differential still 4083→1361 and 3393→1131, src 3736 → 3739 tests over 100 files, build 193/193. The three new cases use a well-formed **x64** fixture so they show what the withdrawn arm would have done — at an 8-byte stride those 24 bytes read as one packed entry claiming a 0x1054-byte function at 0x1000 — and the negative control (restore the arm) fails exactly those two `it.each` cases and nothing else. **NOT VERIFIED, as plainly as `3ucw` said it: this rests entirely on documentation.** There is no ARM64EC or ARM64X binary on this machine, so neither table has been seen, no `ExtraRFETable` value has been read, and the ARM64X swap has never been observed. (2026-08-24 03:25)

- **ARM64EC images are marked x64, not ARM64 — the claim this project had recorded for months was wrong, and it is settled here against Microsoft's documentation rather than against a file** — no behaviour change to `archForMachine`, which turns out to have been right all along; what was wrong was every comment explaining it. Settled because nothing on this machine can adjudicate it: a **final ARM64EC image carries `IMAGE_FILE_MACHINE_AMD64` (0x8664)**, per [Microsoft's Arm64EC overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/arm/arm64ec), which shows `link /dump /headers` reporting `8664 machine (x64) (ARM64X)` for one and states that only the linked EXE or DLL carries "the `8664 (x64) (ARM64X)` or `AA64 (ARM64) (ARM64X)` machine value", and per lld's own writer (`case ARM64EC: coff->Machine = AMD64`, [D149086](https://reviews.llvm.org/D149086)); an **ARM64X image carries 0xAA64 by default** — "By default, Arm64X binaries appear to be Arm64 binaries", so a Windows 10 on Arm system that does not know the format can still load it ([Arm64X PE Files](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/arm/arm64x-pe)) — and may instead be marked 0x8664, which is what makes it launch as emulated x64; and **0xA641 / 0xA64E never appear in a linked image's machine field**, 0xA641 being an object/lib marker Microsoft calls "not a valid final PE machine type", with `dumpbin`'s `(ARM64X)` annotation coming from the hybrid metadata rather than from this word. Three consequences, all of them now written down where they belong (`disasm/arch.ts`, `disasm/arm64.ts`, `pe/types.ts`, `pe/pdata.ts`, `docs/architecture.md`, CLAUDE.md). **The decode-rate refusal's real population is an ARM64X image marked 0xAA64**, plus an image whose machine word is not describing its bytes — it never sees an ARM64EC one, a narrower claim than was recorded, and `Arm64DecodeRateError`'s message no longer names ARM64EC as a cause it cannot have. **An ARM64EC image, and an ARM64X marked 0x8664, take the `"x86"` arm and are disassembled as x64 with nothing said**, which is a real misclassification and not a benign one — Arm64EC code is *native A64* following x64 software conventions, so the x64 decoder reads it as fiction and an x86 linear sweep decodes essentially any byte string, the same silent failure `arch.ts` records for ARMNT. That is **knowingly left alone rather than fixed**: routing on `chpeMetadataPointer` there would be a *decision* taken on a field that has never once been read on an x64 image here — measured at `11408ac`, `t64.exe` and `w64.exe` have **no load-config data directory at all** and read `undefined`, while the only two images on this machine that read the field are the genuine ARM64 pair, which read **0** — so a wrong reading would begin refusing ordinary x64 images, which is strictly worse than today. And `pe/pdata.ts`'s acceptance of 0xA641/0xA64E is a deliberate **superset no real image reaches**, so an ARM64EC image's `.pdata` is read with the **x64** schema; whether that is right is not established here and is now stated rather than assumed. **Three tests pinned the wrong claim and are replaced**: one asserted the refusal message contains `"ARM64EC"` (it now asserts `ARM64X`, and that an ARM64EC image can never be the cause of a refusal thrown from an A64 sweep), and two new `archForMachine` cases pin the documentation finding itself — that a hybrid image goes wherever its machine word points, and that 0xA641/0xA64E are `"unsupported"` because no linked image carries them. **Not verified against a real file, and this cannot be fixed here**: there is no ARM64EC or ARM64X binary on this machine, so the misclassification above has never been observed and the whole finding rests on documentation (`peek-a-bin-3ucw`) (2026-08-24 02:45)
- **The ARM64 decode-rate refusal states a fact about the image where the image declares one** — `PEFile.loadConfig.chpeMetadataPointer` (landed unconsumed by `peek-a-bin-7p5t`) now reaches `Arm64DecodeRateError`, so instead of inferring hybrid-ness from a low rate the message can say the image declares CHPE metadata, name the pointer so the claim can be checked against the file, and — following the machine-type finding above — name **ARM64X** specifically, since this sweep is only reached for an image marked ARM64 and a final ARM64EC image is marked x64. Threaded `App` → `disasmClient.configure` → `WorkerState` → `Arm64Context` → `sweepArm64`. Six things. **It is consumed for PROSE ONLY, and that is what makes it safe rather than what makes it cheap**: `peek-a-bin-7p5t` deliberately left the field with no consumer because "a wrong CHPE reading that starts rejecting genuine ARM64 is worse than the current heuristic, and it cannot be tested here", and that reason does not apply to a message — the throw condition, the `decoded`/`words` fields and every caller's behaviour are untouched, so the failure mode it declined to risk is **structurally impossible** here, pinned by a test that refuses and accepts the identical section under all three states of the field. **It is optional, not a dependency**: an image with no load-config directory, a structure too short to reach the field, or a caller that was never threaded produces exactly the previous message, and only a **non-zero** value is evidence — `0` means present-and-not-hybrid, `undefined` means unreadable, and collapsing those two would make the field useless. **Session state on the worker rather than a per-request field**, the opposite of `machine` and for a stated reason: `peek-a-bin-x4o2`'s hazard is that session state cannot decide the architecture of a message posted before its `configure`, and this decides nothing — but it is keyed on **`machine` being declared** rather than on its own presence, because a declared machine type is the load handshake and guarding on its own presence leaves one file's CHPE pointer attached to the next file's refusal. **Not in any cache key**: `Arm64SweepCache`'s answer is a function of the bytes, the load address and the decoder, so a fourth argument for a message must not be able to cause a miss, which would make one file's prose cost another file's sweep. **`mcp/disasm.ts` is deliberately not threaded** — its three entry points are 7-to-10-parameter functions and the consumer there is a tool client rather than a person, so that path keeps the rate-only wording, which is inside the field's contract rather than a gap in it. Verified through the real `disasmClient` answered by the real `dispatch`, and negative-controlled six ways, each failing 1–3 tests and only its own: the throw dropping the argument, the cache keying on it, `0` treated as evidence, `armCtx` not forwarding it, `configure` guarding on its own presence, and the client dropping it from the message. **NOT VERIFIED against a real file**: `chpeMetadataPointer` is non-zero for no file here — at `11408ac` the two real ARM64 images read 0, the two x64 images have no load-config directory, and the two PE32 images declare a structure too short to reach the field — so the branch that says "this image declares CHPE metadata" is fixture-and-control-only, and nothing rendered the notice it ends up in. `npm run corpus` byte-identical, `npm run corpus:arm64` 51/51, `npm run corpus:comments` md5s unmoved (`peek-a-bin-3ucw`) (2026-08-24 02:45)
- **A decompile request carries one function's instructions, not the whole section's** — `disasmClient.decompileFunction` shipped the whole section's `Instruction[]` and the whole section's typed xref map on every request; measured at `11408ac` by `npm run corpus:decompilecost`, that payload was **92-99% of a request** (476-652 ms and 52-65 ms of clone on a 669 KiB-`.text` `go` image, against 5-6 ms of decompiling). Neither array was read in full: `decompileFunction` hands the instructions to `buildCFG`, which narrows them with `getFuncInsns`, and hands it the map, which is read only at those instructions' addresses. The client now sends this function's slice of each (`collectFuncInsns` and the new `funcXrefEntries`, one declaration of "belongs to this function" in `funcInsns.ts`), and the section crosses only when the worker asks for it — `{ needInstructions: true }` — which happens once per file on x64 and never on PE32, where no callee-clobber summary is built. **A request is 16.6-22.9x cheaper on the four corpus binaries and 56-68x on the `go` image**, whose 1973 functions go from 1242 s of clone to 22.7 s. **Retention is unchanged at zero**, which is why this is not the worker-side residency the bead proposed: that would hold a second 24 MB copy of an array the main thread already has. The worker asks rather than the client predicting, because `CallSummaryCache` holds one entry and `configure` clears it — a client-side mirror would be an eviction rule in two files, and a stale one would serve summary-less C for the whole session out of the address-keyed decompile cache (`peek-a-bin-s2ws`'s hazard). **Equivalence is a census: 0 of 3059 functions across five real images emit different C**, in each of two runs. `npm run corpus` is byte-identical (`diff -r` finds only the label line; `compare.mjs` "byte-identical throughout" on all four with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0"), and `npm run corpus:arm64` is 51/51 green. Ten negative controls, two of which came back inert and had their fixtures sharpened until they bit. NOT verified: nothing spawns a real `Worker`, so `structuredClone` in one process stands in for `postMessage` across two and every figure is an upper bound on what a user feels (`peek-a-bin-9gc9`) (2026-08-24 02:41)
- **A loop header's struct-generation phi now covers what the body changes, not what it assigns** — `baseGenerations` (`src/disasm/decompile/structs.ts`) minted a loop header over `assign` destinations alone, which missed two things: a `call_stmt`'s `resultDest`, so a loop whose body calls anything failed to mint the accumulator at its own header (`peek-a-bin-9fp5`'s fact, at a loop), and every key a `label` in the body re-values. Closing it was filed as needing a per-loop fixpoint nested inside the label one (2^depth walks); it needs neither, because the keys `cur` can hold inside the body are bounded by the state in flight at the header plus the body's own definitions. Census at `1198f4a`: **50/53/47/38 of 187/188/180/174 loop headers** (t32/t64/w64/w32) under-minted, and the residue after adding call results is 20/20/16/16 headers of which **100% contain a label**. Keys a body changes that its header did not mint go **328/681/448/215 → 0**, over header key sets growing 635→958, 785→1460, 685→1127 and 578→788 across 49/52/46/37 headers. The label half is `atLabel`'s own rule read forwards rather than "every key in flight": the blunt reading moves emitted C in 50/10/8/47 functions and costs `t32!sub_4041D0` — the single-definition ESI `peek-a-bin-slkh` adjudicated against `objdump` — a member access, with every gate still green, so only reading the C separates the two. Emitted C **byte-identical on all four binaries** (`report.txt`, `cc/` and `offsetof/` trees identical, `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, `structOverlaps` 0, `offsetof` ratio 1.00), so this is a bound pinned by unit test and not a measured saving. Three negative controls, each failing exactly one test (`peek-a-bin-mvc2`) (2026-08-23 19:27)
- **`hybridDisassemble` decodes through the sweep the load already paid for** — it was the last x86 RPC still running Capstone over `.text` for itself, and the largest (765 ms of a 1666 ms load on a 669 KiB `.text`). It cannot take the linear sweep's array, since recursive descent plus a gap fill produces a different, annotated, smaller stream; it takes the decoder underneath instead (`gridScan`), which answers from the held grid where the grid has an instruction at that address and delegates to Capstone where it does not. The coincidence rate `peek-a-bin-x40u` deferred, measured over the four corpus binaries and a `go` image: **99.9–100.0% of 222137 instructions, 100.0% of the hits agreeing in mnemonic, operands and size**. Served against decoded, the returned `Instruction[]` is identical element for element and field for field. Hybrid 770 → 203 ms on the `go` image and 133 → 22 on t32; whole loads 1654 → 1018 and 241 → 128, a further 38–47% on top of x40u. - **An indirect-dispatch census that takes a path (`corpus/jumpTableReach.ts`, `npm run corpus:jumptables -- <pe>`)** — the instrument `peek-a-bin-2q5` and `peek-a-bin-6rge` have both been blocked on. Neither is closed by writing code: both are "no x64 PE with a table-dispatched switch is available here", which is closed by *pointing at* a binary, so this takes a path and reads the file where it lies rather than requiring it in the corpus directory that CLAUDE.md forbids adding to. It reports the indirect dispatches by shape — `literal-indexed` is `readAbsoluteTable`'s, `register` is `recoverX64RvaChain` → `readRvaTable` → `readDenseRvaTable`'s, `register-base-indexed` is the shape **no reader takes**, `unscaled-memory` is an import thunk — so the shape census *is* the structural answer to "was this reader reached", with no counters bolted into `functionDetect.ts`; then how many the tool recovered, whether a rip-relative `lea` base and a `cmp`/`and` bound were standing there unread, whether the table bases lie inside the code section (which is what decides whether an unread table is *harmful*, since the gap fill reaches nowhere else), and how many of the unread tables' case targets are not already decoded as instruction starts. Everything is **derived from the image** — census from the decoded stream, bounds from the PE header — which is what makes it landable where `peek-a-bin-ayhj`'s probe was not: that one hard-coded 20 dispatch addresses off one `go build` and would have been stale on arrival. It is a census and never a gate, so it cannot over-recover — it never decides a table exists. Both stopped being true when `findArm64JumpTables` landed, and the first inverts the situation: the extents *are* known and this drops them. Corrected in place, with the measurement (`npm run corpus:arm64` at `87a8499`: 9 of 255 words on t64-arm.exe and 9 of 139 on w64-arm.exe render as instructions) and the bead (`peek-a-bin-gb40`) named at the site. `dispatch.ts`'s real reason for not forwarding `dataWindows` — the A64 reader takes its table off an `adr` in the code section, so `.rdata` windows would go unread — is now what the comment says. **`corpus/README.md`'s "`tsx` does not work on this machine (Node 18, `ERR_REQUIRE_ESM`)" is corrected too**: the machine is on Node 22 and all three separately-invoked harnesses are `tsx` scripts that run (`peek-a-bin-56q`) (2026-08-23 02:58)
- **`StackFrame.frameDelta` can no longer be negative zero** — `inlineFrameGeometry` computes `D = E - V` by negating the frame register's entry-relative value, so a frame established with `V == E` (`mov ebp, esp` as the first instruction, nothing pushed ahead of it) yielded `-0`. Inert in every consumer, all of which use `D` arithmetically or with a relational operator where `-0` behaves as `0` — and the emitted C is **byte-identical on all four corpus binaries** at `baa7f61`, which is the claim rather than an assumption. What it removes is a trap: `Object.is(-0, 0)` is false, so `expect(frame.frameDelta).toBe(0)` FAILS on that shape, and `peek-a-bin-s7hl`'s `promote.test.ts` fixture hand-builds `frameDelta: 0` rather than asserting what `stack.ts` produces for exactly this reason. Normalised at the one site that can produce it. The new `stack.test.ts` case IS the negative control — reverting the one-line fix fails it with `expected -0 to be +0 // Object.is equality` — and it also asserts `arg_0` at `[ebp+4]`, pinning that `D` is right and not merely well-signed. Zero occurrences in the corpus (the `D` histogram at `cc45263` is `D=-8:1` and `D=4:203` on t32), so nothing was measurable to move (peek-a-bin-f28y) (2026-08-23 00:52)

- **`t32!sub_40BEBA` / `w32!0x4050F4` was recorded as a real function and is a `__finally` funclet** — CLAUDE.md and `functionDetect.test.ts` both held it up as a counterexample against the "predecessor is unreached" withdrawal rule, describing it as "the six-byte thunk (`call 0x406196; ret`)" whose withdrawal "deletes real code with nothing said". ### Added
- **A struct member whose name and whose brackets disagree is a gate** — `memberNameAgreement` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts`, the naming half of the claim `offsetofCheck` makes about the layout half, over the same `defsIn` member list so a definition cannot be in one denominator and out of the other. The field names record what the recovery *found* (`candidateFields` spells an indexed access whose stride is the width read there `array_0x8` and everything else `field_0x8`) while `declareField` spells the `[...]` off `isArray` alone, so `uint64_t field_0x8[];` is one declaration whose identifier says scalar member and whose extent says array — provably wrong from the emitted text alone with no reference to the machine, which is `polarity inverted`'s character. **GATED at 0**, from **10/1/1/10 disagreeing over 4/1/1/4 functions** (t32/t64/w64/w32) at `51264fe` to **0 over 597/599/587/579 members across 84/71/67/81 definitions**. **The two nearest gates are blind and one of them looks like the instrument**: `gcc -fsyntax-only` compiles a flexible array member without comment, and `offsetofCheck` passes at **1.00** because it takes the expected offset from the name's hex suffix and `offsetof(struct_20, field_0x8)` really is 8 — the layout is right and the member's *kind* is misstated. Both directions are counted (`field_` with `[...]`, `array_` without) although only the first has ever occurred, so a rule that renamed every member instead of promoting would be caught rather than reading green; `members` and `defs` are the liveness halves. Negative-controlled over the **real** corpus rather than a fixture — run over `51264fe`'s own emitted C it reports exactly 10/1/1/10 and names all 22 rows, agreeing figure for figure with an independently written census — and `build/memberNameAudit.test.ts` pins the rule directly, because with the fix the corpus population is 0 and a run can no longer demonstrate the scan discriminates (`peek-a-bin-tm29`) (2026-08-22 23:20)

Measured at `d8d2d02`: **25/0/0/23 internal and 7/3/3/7 external** (t32/t64/w64/w32) over 1274/1161/1092/1203 call sites. All 48 read against `objdump -d -M intel`: every one an MSVC x86 `__finally` funclet the detector folded into its parent (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`, `peek-a-bin-d827`), each with exactly one direct caller and that caller inside the function it is now part of. External is detection's or the IAT's business — a tail `jmp` to a function detection never produced (4 per 32-bit binary) or an indirect `call dword ptr ds:0x…` through a data pointer with no IAT entry, where the minted name is the **pointer's** address (3 per binary, all four). **The bead's split was wrong and the correction matters**: it read the pre-existing 20/18 as unrecovered callees, so it put this class at 12 per binary; it is 25 and 23, and the 20/18 was 13/11 internal plus 7/7 external. **REPORT-ONLY in both directions and not gateable at 0** — `sub_4038F7();` is an incompleteness, not a falsehood, since the machine really does call that address and the name is derived from it, which is `offsetNamedArgs`' character rather than `unencodableNames`'. **Nothing else here sees the class and gcc is blind structurally**: `ccSyntaxCheck` compiles one function per file with no prototypes, so *every* callee in the corpus is an implicit declaration and `-std=gnu89` accepts one, with `-w` suppressing the warning before `preludeFor` is consulted — so this leaves no invented prelude declaration either and `peek-a-bin-k8i`'s instrument is blind too (the bead had recorded `preludeFor` as the mechanism; measured, it is not). `distinct callees lost` asks only whether the name is on the page. **Negative-controlled by the change that created the population**: reverting `peek-a-bin-d827`'s fourth admission takes `internal` 25 → 13 on t32 and 23 → 11 on w32 while `functions` rises 268 → 280 and 266 → 278 — exactly the twelve funclet starts it withdraws per binary — with `external` unmoved at 7 and `internalLabelled` unmoved at 8 and 6; run the other way `compare.mjs` flags the rise and exits 1, so the row would have been red at `21ea66e`. Emitted C **byte-identical on all four binaries** (md5 of every `funcs_*.jsonl`, `diff -r` over the `cc/` and `offsetof/` trees), `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, verdict "no regression". ### Fixed

- **A funclet has two entries, and the one MSVC's parent calls is the one that got detected** — `interiorBranchedOverStarts` gains a **fourth admission**: the instruction that *runs into* a candidate start reads memory through the frame register, so nothing there established a frame. MSVC x86 emits the register reloads only the unwinder needs and then the funclet body; the parent's own `call` names the **body**, past the reloads, so the body is detected as a function and the reload falls to the previous function as a dead assignment whose only consumer is now somewhere else. `t32!sub_40388B` ended `loc_4038F4: esi = arg_0;` and nothing else, while `sub_4038F7` called `sub_4023CD()` with **no argument at all**; it now reads `eax = sub_4023CD(arg_0); return eax;`. t32 0x405f1a's trailing `loc_405F23: esi = *(int32_t*)(ebp + 8)` — a frame read in a function that never assigns `ebp`, the exact defect the third admission exists to fix — is gone. The evidence is the second and third admissions composed and asked one instruction earlier; reachability is deliberately not tested (all 12 predecessors per binary are unreached, so it would contribute nothing, and the case it would exclude is *stronger* — the previous function's own execution crossing the boundary). Measured at `cc45263`, both sides pinned: 12 starts per 32-bit binary and **all 24 are funclet bodies, no false positive over 558 detected starts**; `functions` 280 → **268** and 278 → **266** with **t64/w64 byte-identical** and instructions unmoved at 18025/16601; every gate figure identical bar the denominators that move with `functions` — gcc 268/268 and 266/266 clean, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, polarity anchor-A 517/517 and 441/441 with 0 inverted, `guards audited` flat at 919/824/821/729 and `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, **arity completely unmoved**, unrecovered values 36/12/11/33, and 0 on every zero-gate. **The frame test is the whole restriction and only the emitted C can see it**: relaxed to "the predecessor is not a terminator" the rule also takes `t32!0x4037b2` / `w32!0x403a06`, whose predecessor is `call _invalid_parameter_noinfo`, and the merge appends `eax = sub_406CC0(7); return eax;` to the arm containing it — stating that control flows out of a `noreturn` call — with `npm run corpus` reporting that **clean, exit 0** (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`). ### Fixed
- **A function start whose first instruction reads a frame it did not establish is withdrawn into the function that established it** — `peek-a-bin-qe8z`. `[ebp + 8]` in the *first* instruction at an address is a read of some other function's frame: nothing has run there yet to set one up, and no x86 calling convention passes EBP, so an entry point cannot mean anything by it. Beside `interiorBranchedOverStarts`' existing funclet condition ("the previous function is the only thing that calls it") the candidate is that function's MSVC `__finally` body — emitted inside the parent, `call`ed from it, running on its frame with no prologue of its own, which is exactly what CLAUDE.md's `peek-a-bin-sysf` note already says such a funclet *is*. **8 starts on t32 and 6 on w32** (measured at base `99203fb`): seven ten-byte `push dword ptr [ebp + N]; call; pop ecx; ret` funclets per binary plus the 46-byte pair at t32 0x40a631 / w32 0x40921c. In every case the base emitted a standalone function reading `*(int32_t*)(ebp + 8)` with **nothing in it assigning `ebp`**, and the withdrawal turns the same read into `arg_0` — the parent's own argument slot, nameable now that the frame is the frame of the function the read sits in. `t32!sub_401DB3` is the worked example: it overwrites `[ebp+8]` at 0x401e05, so the funclet reads the *updated* argument and the base gave the reader no way to know which frame, let alone which value. All 14 diffs read by hand against `objdump`; the 46-byte pair merges as a real `if` nest over the parent's `var_1C`/`var_20` and sharpens `var_1C` from `int32_t` to `uint32_t` off the new comparison. Functions **288 → 280 (t32)** and **285 → 278 (w32)**, instructions unmoved at 18025/16601, **t64 and w64 byte-identical**; every gate flat — gcc 280/280 and 278/278 clean, `offsetof` 346/346 and 354/354 at ratio 1.00 with definitions unmoved, polarity 517/517 and 441/441 correct with 0 inverted and 0 mismatch, `guards: 919 → 919` and `821 → 821` with **CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0**, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/wildBranches/unencodableNames/crossEdgeGuards/selfAssigns all 0 — and **loop shapes identical on all four** (`for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759).
