Two forms the bead proposed are **refused, on evidence**: `and <mem>, <all ones>` because `and`'s identity element is width-dependent and a text-level reading of it misfires on real instructions here (`and <reg>, 0xff`/`0xffff` on PE32, `and <reg>, 0xfff` on x64 are truncations, and a misfire deletes a real store), and `xchg` with itself because `lock` requires a memory operand while `xchg` requires a register one, so that encoding cannot exist. Measured at base `41113c9`, both sides pinned: self-assignments **3 → 0** on each x64 binary, `var_0` declarations **2 → 0** (not 3 — the two fences in `sub_14000C440`/`sub_14000A690` share one slot), `var_0` mentions 8 → 0, −2 emitted lines, **2 of 279 and 2 of 275 functions changed**, and t32/w32 **byte-identical**. Every gate flat and `compare.mjs` verdict "no regression": gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` 935/935 across 161 at ratio 1.00, polarity 1554/1554 with 0 inverted and `CHANGED 0`/`only-base 0`/`only-change 0` on all four, unrecovered values unmoved, loop shapes unmoved, statements dropped 0, callees lost 0, arity/staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/wildBranches/unencodableNames all 0. The only moved rows are `lostDefs`' own liveness counters, −6 per x64 binary, which is exactly the two `rsp` reads per fence leaving the examined set (`peek-a-bin-qbk3`) (2026-08-21 18:57)
- **`detectForLoop` no longer gives up on a loop because the first increment it finds is not the induction variable** — MSVC's newline count, `for (p = a; p < b; p++) if (*p == '\n') n++;`, has two self-increments, and `n++`'s block is numbered below the latch while `n` has no initialiser in the function at all; the search committed to it, failed the init test and returned null, so 26 loops corpus-wide (8/8/5/5 on t32/t64/w64/w32 at `bd73798`) were emitted as `while`. Every candidate is now tried and the first with an init wins, which is additive by construction. A predecessor *inside the loop body* is also no longer a source of inits: the back-edge test was `pred.id < header.id`, and a latch is routinely numbered below its header, so in 19/18/15/16 of these loops one "pre-loop" predecessor was a body block. `for` 6/5/5/6 → **6/6/6/6**, top-tested `while` 111/107/100/98 → 111/106/99/98, `do/while` and `if` unmoved, t32/w32 byte-identical, one function changed per x64 binary, every gate flat (`peek-a-bin-9q2`) (2026-08-21 18:05)
- **A matched `push <reg>` / `pop <reg>` is lifted as a stack slot, so a restored register reads the value the machine saved** — a `pop` that is not lifted is no definition in SSA, so every later read bound to the value the register held *before* it. `t32!sub_40D99A` is MSVC's SSE memset: `push edx` saved the remaining byte count, the block reused EDX as a scratch and a loop decremented it to zero, `pop edx` restored the count, and the emitted C read the scratch — 0 at loop exit. The same function's `push ecx` / … / `pop eax` / `ret` is memset **returning its destination pointer**, and `return eax` returned the loop counter. `matchedStackSlots` (`decompile/lifter.ts`) runs a balanced-depth model over the whole CFG and `liftBlock` spells the pair as `stk_<pushaddr> = <reg>` at the push and `<reg> = stk_<pushaddr>` at the pop, so no read of RSP is moved and no RSP-relative expression is built — the slot is identified by the pairing, not by an address. Measured at base `bd73798`, both sides pinned: `corpus/popReads.ts` **4 → 0 wrong** and **2 → 0 implicit-`ret`-wrong** on both 32-bit binaries, `popsLifted` 73 → 151 and 68 → 146, 5 pops paired on each x64 binary with the emitted C **byte-identical**, 6 of 288 and 8 of 285 functions changed, `unrecovered values` unmoved at 91/21/17/72, loop shapes identical, and every gate flat except `offsetof` w32 418 → 421 fields at ratio 1.00. `peek-a-bin-6f3v` (2026-08-21 18:10)
- **A trivial phi's operand no longer reaches the emitted C under the register's canonical 64-bit name** — `insertPhis` mints every phi as `irReg(canonReg(...))` and the operand fill copies that name and width verbatim, so a phi operand is `rsi` at eight bytes even in 32-bit code. `destroySSA` corrects that for a lowered phi copy; `simplifyPhis` substitutes the operand into every reader of the phi's destination, where it becomes an ordinary read with nothing left to correct it. It now spells the operand the way the value's own mentions spell it, which is `registerSpeller`'s `perVersion` rule one pass earlier. Latent until the stack-slot pairing above made an RSI phi trivial, which took `corpus/emitAudits.ts`'s `unencodableNames` gate from 0 to 34 mentions over 2 PE32 functions. Output-neutral on its own: byte-identical emitted C on all 1127 functions of all four binaries at `bd73798` (2026-08-21 18:10)
- **A `for`'s initialiser no longer has to be the statement immediately before the loop** — `structureFrom` demoted a recognised `for` back to a `while` unless the init was, by object identity, the *last* statement the walk had emitted, which was costing 3 for-loops per 32-bit corpus binary. `initHoistable` answers the general case, and states the question properly: hoisting moves the init later, past everything between it and the loop, so it is not enough that nothing in between touches the induction variable — the value has to be the same after the move. Four refusals, each measured rather than hypothesised: a whitelist of what may be in between (`assign`/`store`/`comment`, so a `label` — a jump target that today *skips* the init and after the move would *run* it — and a `raw` are both refused); a side-effecting init; any mention in between of the induction variable or of anything the init's right-hand side reads, nested bodies included; and a memory write in between when the init reads memory, with no attempt to prove non-aliasing. `for` 3/5/5/3 → **6/5/5/6** with top-tested `while` 114/107/100/101 → 111/107/100/98, `do/while` unmoved, both x64 binaries byte-identical (the x64 population is out of reach by construction — there the init is not in the walk at all), −6 emitted lines per 32-bit binary, and every gate flat. `compare.mjs`'s one flagged row per 32-bit binary is the documented benign anchor shift: a byte-identical `if (al != 0)` guard moving from the strict anchor-A tier to A2 because the hoist removed the statement carrying its block's start address, with A+A2 flat at 879 and 789 and every verdict OK. A latent bug in the first draft is worth recording: an equality-only index test fires for an empty `result` (both sides are -1) and copies in an init the walk never emitted, which measured as +3 `for` on each x64 binary where the census predicted 0 — the predicted-versus-measured gap is the only thing that caught it (peek-a-bin-9q2) (2026-08-21 17:05)

### Added
- **`emptyCaseBodies` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts` — a `case` label whose whole body is `break;`** — the quantity `peek-a-bin-37az` was filed on, hand-counted with a throwaway script at the time because nothing in the run reported it. **The defect it was filed for no longer exists**: measured at `d8d2d02`, **0 bare bodies of 72 case labels on t32 and 0 of 54 on w32** (0 of 0 on both x64 binaries, which recover no jump table), against **10 of 72 on t32** as filed on 2026-08-12 — 8 of the 27 labels `peek-a-bin-mk42` had just added, plus 2 pre-existing in `sub_40887C`. `peek-a-bin-dp6` and `peek-a-bin-pqs5` closed it between them: 29/29 labels are now a lone `goto` to the label the block *was* emitted under and 43/25 carry a body of their own. **REPORT-ONLY, not a gate**, matching `armExits.ts`' own refusal to gate its `unnameable` population — where the short-circuit fold consumed the target block without a label there is no name for a `goto` to use — and because `unnameable` is 0 here too, so a gate would rest on an empty population. **The nearest existing gate is provably blind to the class**: under the negative control (`armBody`'s reclaimed-label branch returning `break`) this row goes 0 → **29 on each PE32 binary** and `compare.mjs` flags it, while `armExits`' `falseBreaks` stays at **0** and its `unnameable` rises only 0 → 1 — 28 of the 29 blocks end in `ret`, so the audit correctly calls them truthful — and **`npm run corpus` exits 0 over that whole control**. The two stop rules in `caseBody` are the instrument and each was wrong in a first draft (the switch's closing brace sits at the case label's own indent, so a strictly-shallower stop absorbs the code after the switch into the last arm; a `loc_` label is emitted at column 0 whatever its nesting), both pinned with nine rule tests in `build/caseBodyAudit.test.ts`. It judges the body's emptiness and never its contents. Emitted C **byte-identical on all four binaries** and every row in `compare.mjs` unchanged, this being the only new field in any artifact (`peek-a-bin-37az`) (2026-08-22 22:50)
- **`paramClobberedAtEntry` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts` — a declared parameter a callee-saved register overwrites at entry** — a gate at **0 rows over 430/578/414/565 declared parameters** (t32/t64/w64/w32 at `99203fb`). It exists because the `offset-named argument slots` row **provably cannot tell a right change from a wrong one**: the Microsoft x64 home space is four slots the caller reserves and the callee may use for anything, so `peek-a-bin-g186` drove that row to 0 by declaring no parameter for an unfilled home slot, and the variant `peek-a-bin-sx57` measured and refused drives it to the same 0 by *naming* all 35 slots — moving nothing else in the whole report — while printing 11 declared parameters per x64 binary that a callee-saved register overwrites before any read. Asked over the name the two are identical; asked over the declared parameter list they differ, so that row is now documented as a **target** and this is the **gate**. `first appearance, not any appearance` is the precision that makes it sound (a callee may reuse an argument slot as scratch once it has read the argument, and MSVC does), and the callee-saved restriction is what makes it a defect rather than a shape — the same corpus carries `arg_3 = rax` under that variant and it is correctly not counted. Negative-controlled: forcing `inUnfilledHomeSpace` false takes it 0 → 11 on each x64 binary with `offsetNamedArgs` still 0 on all four, and the row it names first is `sub_1400027C8:arg_0 = rbx`. Emitted C byte-identical on all 1127 functions; five rule tests in `build/paramClobberAudit.test.ts`. `offsetNamedArgs`' docstring, which described a defect fixed three commits ago, now says what a non-zero reading would mean (`peek-a-bin-15q7`) (2026-08-22 03:40)
- **A guard that is wrong on one incoming edge is gated** — `corpus/crossEdgeGuards.ts`, the first oracle here that can see a Jcc *alone in its basic block* being answered from a predecessor that disagrees with the others. Such a block sets no flags of its own, so its test was made in the block before it, and where several predecessors make different tests no block-local `if` states the machine on every path in. **0 answered and 0 named of 12 disagreeing** cross-edge blocks (2/5/4/1 on t32/t64/w64/w32) of 14 multi-edge, 35/18/13/24 cross-edge, over 2285/2219/1966/2019 jcc blocks at `16f1633`. The blindness was **demonstrated by execution, not argued**: drop only the agreement test in `flagModel.ts`'s `unanimousCompare` and all **19 pre-existing gates pass** with `compare.mjs` reporting "VERDICT: no regression" — polarity 519/577/499/442 audited all correct (it judges the *operator*, which is right; the *operands* belong to one edge), `staleGuards` 0 named with `shapes` unmoved because its scan is block-local, gcc 1127/1127 — while the recovery baseline scores it as an **improvement**, unrecovered values falling by exactly 12. **Two counts gate**, because the stronger claim has the weaker coverage: `named` (a guard anchored on the page) sees 8 of 12 and misses the witness, `admitted` (the predecessor `flagPredecessor` chose, which is necessary for either route to a condition) sees 12 of 12 with no anchoring. Witness `t64!sub_140002A2C` at `0x140002afd` — one `je`, entered from `test rbx, rbx` and from `test rbp, rbp` — emits `if (rbx_2 == 0)` under the control against `if (!!__unrecovered_1 /* je */)` on the shipping tree. Liveness is `soleAdmitted === sole` (32/13/9/22) and `soleNamed` (6/4/2/3); disagreement is text inequality after folding `test`'s two exact equivalences. Emitted C **byte-identical on all four binaries** and every other audited number unchanged, the new field being the only difference in any artifact (`peek-a-bin-0xe2`) (2026-08-22 02:30)
- **A self-assignment in the emitted C is audited rather than suppressed** — `corpus/selfAssigns.ts` resolves every `X = X;` through the emitted line map to the instruction it carries the address of and classifies it from that instruction's *operands*. `eax = eax;` is noise where the machine really does nothing (`lea ecx,[ecx+0x0]`, `mov edi,edi`, `or al,al`, `add eax,0x0`) and the **one visible trace a lost operand leaves** where it does not — `peek-a-bin-3axd`, 97 wrong reads over 28 t32 functions, was found through exactly two such lines, and every other gate is blind to the class (gcc compiles it; `staleReads`/`lostDefs` see a definition reaching every later read *because a self-assignment is one*; the statement-drop audit snapshots after `foldBlock`). Measured at `97249dc`, t32/t64/w64/w32: **0/0/0/0 `wrong`, 0/0/0/0 `unresolved`, 1/0/0/1 `openOperand` (0 uncorroborated), 30/0/0/30 machine identities**. The bead's recommended gate could **not** be taken literally and that is recorded: the non-idiom subset is not empty — t32 0x403034 `sub ecx, ebx` with EBX zeroed above it is correct output — so `openOperand` is reported and judged in `compare.mjs`, while `wrong` and `unresolved` gate the instrument's integrity at 0. Negative-controlled by disabling `peek-a-bin-3axd`: `openOperand` 1 → 4 on both PE32 binaries, uncorroborated 0 → 3, naming t32 0x40d23f `add edi, esi` exactly. Emitted C **byte-identical on all four binaries** (`peek-a-bin-o7pj`) (2026-08-21 20:20)
- **`offsetNamedArgs` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts` — how much of the argument area the frame recovery is still missing** — reported at **16/147/141/14 occurrences over 8/33/30/7 functions** (t32/t64/w64/w32) at `41113c9` + `peek-a-bin-emlv`, from 156/147/141/140 over 32/33/30/29. `stack.ts` spells a parameter slot `arg_<index>` only once it has verified the frame pointer and `arg_0x<offset>` otherwise, so an `arg_0x<N>` whose `(N - firstOffset) % slotSize === 0` is a slot the naming **would** have indexed had the prologue been recognised — the offset spelling is down to the recognition and to nothing about the file. The sub-slot half (7/5 on the x64 pair, e.g. the third byte of argument 0) is reported apart, because those are correctly offset-named at any level of recovery. **Report-only in both directions and not gateable at 0**: the PE32 residue is detection over-production, where the prologue is outside the detected range and the frame register belongs to the enclosing function, and the x64 population is a different defect again — phantom parameters rather than unnamed ones — which makes this the standing instrument for `peek-a-bin-ikd` as well. Nothing else in the repo sees the class: an offset-named argument is a well-typed identifier gcc compiles, it states nothing false so polarity, `staleGuards` and `staleReads` are all indifferent, it is not an admission so the unrecovered count does not move, and `offsetof` only checks layouts it was given — the whole point being that such a slot never reaches struct synthesis as a parameter. The geometry is written independently of `stack.ts`'s `ARG_AREA` so the audit cannot agree with the code under test by construction. Reported per binary in the summary and in `compare.mjs`, with `funcs` asserted non-zero as the liveness half; negative-controlled by running it over the base's own emitted C, where it reports exactly 156/147/141/140, agreeing figure for figure with an independently written census (peek-a-bin-emlv) (2026-08-21 19:35)
- **`offsetNamedArgs` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts` — how much of the argument area the frame recovery is still missing** — reported at **16/147/141/14 occurrences over 8/33/30/7 functions** (t32/t64/w64/w32) at `41113c9` + `peek-a-bin-emlv`, from 156/147/141/140 over 32/33/30/29. `stack.ts` spells a parameter slot `arg_<index>` only once it has verified the frame pointer and `arg_0x<offset>` otherwise, so an `arg_0x<N>` whose `(N - firstOffset) % slotSize === 0` is a slot the naming **would** have indexed had the prologue been recognised — the offset spelling is down to the recognition and to nothing about the file. The sub-slot half (7/5 on the x64 pair, e.g. the third byte of argument 0) is reported apart, because those are correctly offset-named at any level of recovery. **Report-only in both directions and not gateable at 0**: the PE32 residue is detection over-production, where the prologue is outside the detected range and the frame register belongs to the enclosing function, and the x64 population is a different defect again — phantom parameters rather than unnamed ones — which makes this the standing instrument for `peek-a-bin-ikd` as well. Nothing else in the repo sees the class: an offset-named argument is a well-typed identifier gcc compiles, it states nothing false so polarity, `staleGuards` and `staleReads` are all indifferent, it is not an admission so the unrecovered count does not move, and `offsetof` only checks layouts it was given — the whole point being that such a slot never reaches struct synthesis as a parameter. The geometry is written independently of `stack.ts`'s `ARG_AREA` so the audit cannot agree with the code under test by construction. Reported per binary in the summary and in `compare.mjs`, with `funcs` asserted non-zero as the liveness half; negative-controlled by running it over the base's own emitted C, where it reports exactly 156/147/141/140, agreeing figure for figure with an independently written census (peek-a-bin-emlv). Those figures are the audit's own baseline; `peek-a-bin-ikd` has since taken them to 0/83/83/0 (2026-08-21 20:15)
- **`fold.test.ts` guards the direction an arithmetic fold must NOT take** — `+`, `-` and `*` do not wrap to int32, and until now the only test that failed when they were made to was the 2^53 refusal one, catching it incidentally for a different stated reason, while `npm run corpus` stayed byte-identical on all four binaries so no gate could see it either. Censused first (peek-a-bin-ivj5): these fold 33/42/40/30 `+`, 0/29/24/0 `-` and 0/0/0/0 `*` over the corpus (t32/t64/w64/w32) with **0 results outside the int32 range** — the largest anywhere is 4271612, an image address plus a field offset, about 500× below the boundary. No behaviour changed, and the reason is recorded rather than left to be rediscovered: `knownWidth` returns null for an `IRConst` deliberately and `IRConst.size` is the CPU mode rather than the operand width, so the const-const branch these three fold at has no width evidence to key a wraparound guard on; and the tempting fix — `(l + r) | 0` / `Math.imul`, matching what the bitwise arms of the same switch do by JavaScript's own semantics — would turn a correct 64-bit `add` over the boundary into a negative constant. Negative-controlled: the new test fails on the wrapping change and passes on the tree (2026-08-21 18:10)
- **Loop shape is reported, and the `while` figure it replaces was two constructs added together** — `auditClobbered` counted `while (` by regex over each function's emitted text, which matches *both* a top-tested `while (c) {` and the `} while (c);` back edge of a do/while, so the audit's one `while` number was their sum and do/while was not counted anywhere. `clobbered.doWhiles` splits them, read per line with the same anchored regex pair `siteOf` uses, and the audit and `compare.mjs` now print `if`, top-tested `while`, `do/while` and `for` side by side: at `6d5ae92`, `for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1934/1717/1759 (t32/t64/w64/w32). The split sums to the raw match count exactly on all four binaries, which is the corroboration that the two readings CLAUDE.md carried — 192/184/176/178 and 114/107/100/101 — were ever one quantity. `clobbered.whiles` keeps its raw meaning deliberately, because artifact directories from earlier commits carry it as the sum and narrowing it would make every old-vs-new comparison report a fall of exactly `doWhiles` as though output had been lost; `compare.mjs` prints the raw row unchanged, derives the top-tested figure and notes `NOT MEASURED` when either side predates the split. **Report-only in both directions and not a gate**: a `for` that becomes a `while` is a fidelity loss rather than a false statement about the machine, and the absolutes move whenever function detection does — what this buys is that a shape change between two pinned runs is a row rather than something the next agent has to think to count, which is the whole of peek-a-bin-9q2, whose 4× fall in for-loop recognition happened with nothing recording it. Negative-controlled, and the control shows the blind spot precisely: perturbing one summary's `doWhiles` from 78 to 60 — a do/while silently becoming a top-tested while — moves both new rows while `while emitted (raw)` stays at 192 → 192, exactly the change the old instrument could not see (peek-a-bin-9q2) (2026-08-21 16:25)
- **`corpus/wildBranches.ts` — a direct branch in the filed instruction stream whose target the image does not contain** — a gate at **0 on all four binaries**, from **1/0/0/1 over 4241/4073/3644/3792 direct branches** at `6d5ae92` (t32 0x40b824 `jmp 0x288402b`, w32 0x40964c `jmp 0x2301953`, both `source: "gap-fill"`). A literal branch displacement is resolved by the linker inside the image it is producing, so a filed `jmp 0x288402b` in a 0x400000–0x40e000 image is not an instruction the file contains — it is a byte string the disassembler walked into, and every row is provably fiction decided against `optionalHeader.sizeOfImage` alone. Deliberately *not* "this branch leaves its function", which is ordinary (a tail call, a funclet, an ICF-shared epilogue) and is the weaker question `peek-a-bin-y1di`'s instrument asked, and not "outside the code section" either, since a thunk table in a data section would make the claim arguable. **Independent of what it caught**: it is phrased in terms of the emitted stream and the PE header and mentions neither jump tables nor `unboundedTableExtent`, which matters because the change it was built for works by marking table bytes as data — an audit phrased in those terms would be measuring its own input. Honestly a **lower bound and a loose one**: fiction registers only where it happens to decode as a direct branch aimed outside the image, which was 1 of the 2 jump-table sites per 32-bit binary that `peek-a-bin-xqxy` fixed. Every other standing instrument was blind — gcc compiles fiction, polarity judges guards that exist, the statement-drop audit counts drops rather than inventions, and `peek-a-bin-7lb9`'s spurious-run census needs a run of four code-pointer words containing its own dispatch base. Reported per binary with `checked` as the liveness half, gated in the run, judged in `compare.mjs`, and negative-controlled: revert the `overlappedTableExtent` change and `npm run corpus` exits 1 naming exactly those two rows (peek-a-bin-xqxy) (2026-08-21 16:45)
- **`unencodableNames` in `corpus/emitAudits.ts` — a register name the image has no encoding for** — a gate at **0 mentions over 573 PE32 functions**, from **124 mentions of 18 distinct names over 19 functions** at `d514274` (71/18/11 on t32, 53/13/8 on w32). `canonReg` maps every alias to the 64-bit parent because that is the register's identity and SSA keys on it, so any path that lets a canonical name reach the page prints `rcx` in a function whose every other line says `ecx`. Asked of **PE32 only**, and that restriction is what makes it an oracle rather than a heuristic: a 32-bit image has no RCX, so every occurrence is provably a name no instruction wrote and no reader can mean — `polarity inverted`'s character rather than a count awaiting a threshold. On x64 `rcx` is an ordinary correct spelling and separating a canonical name from a real 64-bit read needs the live range's own width, which `registerSpeller` answers and the emitted text does not record, so those two binaries contribute a structural 0 and `funcs` beside the gate is the liveness half. This is the only oracle in the repo that can see a wrong register *name*: `gcc` cannot, because `preludeFor` declares every undeclared identifier as its own `long` and `rcx`/`ecx` compile as two unrelated variables, and `corpus/staleReads.ts` cannot either, since it compares the name a read uses rather than the canonical register — deliberately, because a correct live-range split emits two names for one register — so a canonical name reads to it as a legitimate second live range. Reported per binary in the summary and in `compare.mjs`, gated in the run, and negative-controlled: restore the phi operand's spelling evidence in `splitStaleReads` and `npm run corpus` exits 1 naming exactly 124 (peek-a-bin-0s6e) (2026-08-21 15:05)
- **`corpus/armExits.ts` — a switch arm claiming the switch is over while its own block goes on** — a gate at **0 on all four binaries**, from **35/0/0/17** at `91085f3` (25/12 of those ending in a conditional jump, 10/5 in a `jmp`). `structureSwitch`'s `armBody` claims exactly one block per arm and closed it with `break` however the block ends, and `break` is a claim about control flow rather than an appendable terminator — so every row is a provably false statement the output makes, which is `polarity inverted`'s character rather than a baseline's. The instrument that found the defect (peek-a-bin-pqs5) was a throwaway probe inside `structureCFG` and lived nowhere; it now hangs off `pipeline.ts`'s existing `StructuringTap`, which grew an `armExits` field fed by a new optional observer on `structureCFG` that only a caller with a tap ever gets. Reported split by whether the block ends in a conditional jump, because the halves are different mechanisms — the conditional one loses the recovered *test* as well as the transfer, step 4b having already hoisted the `IRBranch` out. `arms`/`truthfulExits` are the denominator beside the gate and are **0 on both x64 binaries**, which recover no jump table at all, so the liveness check is tied to the recovered-table count rather than asserted blind (72 arms over 5 functions on t32, 54 over 3 on w32, all truthful, at `5c5dab9`). Emitted C is byte-identical on all four with the audit running; negative-controlled by replacing `armExit`'s body with `[{ kind: "break" }]`, which makes `npm run corpus` exit 1 naming exactly the 35/0/0/17 the throwaway probe reported (peek-a-bin-64gp) (2026-08-21 04:55)
- **`corpus/comments.ts` — the first audit of `Instruction.comment` in this repo** — every gate in `corpus.audit.ts` reads emitted C or the IR behind it, and a comment reaches neither, so a wrong comment is invisible to `npm run corpus`, to `gcc` and to every suite under `src/` while being one of the few things a reader of the disassembly view takes purely on trust. It asks a different question per architecture, deliberately. On **ARM64** it splits today's comments by whether `findArm64AddressRefs` — the real A64 reference reader, already `buildArm64Xrefs`' oracle — agrees the instruction refers to a string or an import; every non-agreeing row is a comment naming something the instruction does not reference, which gives it a gate's character, and it reads **0 coincidence on both binaries** at HEAD from 248/253 at `91085f3`. On **x86/x64** the same operand scan is *sound* (an x86 operand really can carry an absolute address), so there is nothing to judge and it prints an md5 over every commented instruction instead — which is the byte-identity proof for any change to the shared comment path, and the one that established `mapInsn` was untouched here (`ebeee5898bcc`/`dc41107568eb`/`92a1c5b0f848`/`3f4e9eb11bdc` on t32/t64/w64/w32, identical before and after). It now lands in `AppState.omittedPasses` via a new `SET_OMITTED_PASSES` action, and `analysisNotice()` has a third kind, `"partial-detection"`: "Function detection ran without call targets, jump tables, thunk names and tail calls…" in the App banner and in the status bar, with the count itself marked `(partial)`. The case it exists for is a dead Capstone under a *supported* architecture — detection deliberately keeps answering from `.pdata`, the exports, the entry point and the unwind handlers, so the list is *short* rather than empty and is shaped exactly like a complete one, and the status bar's green "Ready" over it was the same lie the failed phase used to tell. The notice says what is still trustworthy as well as what is missing, ranks below both existing kinds (an unsupported architecture already implies every decoder-fed pass, so it is not told twice) and extends a failure's message rather than replacing it. Wire values never reach the screen: `DETECT_PASS_LABELS` is `Record<DetectPass, string>`, so a fifth pass fails the build. **Unverified**: nothing renders a component here, so the reducer branch, the pure decision and the fact that App and StatusBar feed it their state are tested, and the appearance of the banner is not (peek-a-bin-ipzf, peek-a-bin-ybv2) (2026-08-12 04:25)
- **CHPE metadata pointer (load config directory)** — `parsePE` now reads `IMAGE_LOAD_CONFIG_DIRECTORY` (data directory 10) far enough to expose `CHPEMetadataPointer` as `PEFile.loadConfig`, the format's only *declaration* that an image is ARM64EC or ARM64X — where the existing refusal reasons from the A64 decode rate, which is evidence about the bytes rather than an answer about the image. The field is three-state on purpose: a number means it was read (`0` = present and not hybrid), `undefined` means it could not be, because collapsing "not declared" into "not readable" would make it useless as evidence. Offsets are a table rather than a computed widening — the 32-bit layout swaps `ProcessHeapFlags` and `ProcessAffinityMask`, so PE32 reads 4 bytes at 0x7c and PE32+ 8 at 0xc8. Three independent claims must all cover the field before it is read: the data directory's `Size`, the structure's own `Size`, and the section table's mapping; the last needed `rvaRangeToFileOffset()`, since resolving one byte lets a structure that starts 8 bytes before the end of `.rdata` read its remaining fields out of the next section. That bound is not theoretical — both PE32 binaries on this machine declare 0x48 bytes where the 32-bit field needs 0x80, and an unbounded read would report `.rdata`'s next bytes as a CHPE pointer, i.e. call an ordinary x86 image hybrid. The reader was mutation-tested rather than trusted green (three separate mutations each break tests, 4 in total) and checked differentially against an independently written from-spec reader across all six real binaries, which agree field for field; both genuine ARM64 images read present-and-zero, so a consumer keying on non-zero cannot misfire on them. **Nothing consumes the field and no behaviour changes** — the decode-rate floor is untouched, because a wrong CHPE reading that started rejecting genuine ARM64 would be worse than the current heuristic and cannot be tested here. **The positive case has never been observed**: there is no ARM64EC or ARM64X binary on this machine, so a real hybrid writing a non-zero pointer is read from the layout, not measured. 13 new tests, 7 of them adversarial (peek-a-bin-7p5t) (2026-08-12 04:34)

### Removed
- **`detectFunctions`' `prevWasUnconditional` heuristic, which was subsumed and would be measurably wrong to revive** — the x86 sweep loop kept `prevWasUnconditional` (set on `ret`/`retn`/`jmp`, reset across a decode gap by `prevEnd`) for one reader: `if (prevWasUnconditional && callTargets.has(insn.address)) addrSet.add(insn.address)`. The add can never be new, because the call branch adds every in-section target to `addrSet` on the line *before* adding it to `callTargets`, `addrSet` is never deleted from and `callTargets` has no other writer — so `callTargets ⊆ addrSet` by construction. Re-censused at **`11408ac`**: the guard fires **156/34/35/146** times on t32/t64/w64/w32 and adds a new address **0** times on all four, matching `peek-a-bin-x40u`'s `6f2ce28` figures. The alternative — giving it a reader that treats "call target after a terminator" as a *strong* start — is refuted by the same census: it names **15 of t32's 33 and 13 of w32's 32** starts `interiorBranchedOverStarts` withdraws, every one with exactly one direct caller and including 0x4058A6 / 0x40BEBA (w32 0x4050F4), the addresses the `strongStarts` docstring already names as its counterexample — a superset of the 9 and 7 it measures and refuses, because an MSVC `__finally` funclet *is* a call target at its parent's tail. 3 per 32-bit binary rest on a preceding `jmp`, the ambiguous evidence that function admits only for an *unreached* boundary, which a call target never is. No gate can see either choice, so the census is the instrument and it is recorded in the `callTargets` and `strongStarts` docstrings; the corpus is byte-identical (`diff -r` finds only the label line, `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four), 51/51 ARM64 gates green, all four x86 comment md5s unmoved (`peek-a-bin-7lue`) (2026-08-24 02:20)
- **`ssaopt.ts` no longer holds a flag definition live by hand, and `flagResult.ts` is deleted** — `protectedFlagDefs`' two loops (the `eflags` def of a Jcc block, `peek-a-bin-ua8`; the arithmetic setter re-derived from the instruction stream, `peek-a-bin-pu06`), the post-fixpoint `eflags` strip, `structure.ts`'s `conditionFromFlagResult`, `RESULT_ANSWERABLE_JCC` and the whole backward walk. Every one existed to stand in for a use dead-code elimination could not see, because the guard was not an IR reader; the `branch` arm of `countStmtUses` **is** that use, and `RESULT_ANSWERABLE_JCC` was a second statement of what `RegState.getCondition`'s `result` arm already decides. Measured before it was attempted rather than after: instrumented over the whole corpus with the scaffolding still in place, `conditionFromFlagResult` fired **0 times**. **Deleting it is output-neutral — byte-identical emitted C on all 1127 functions of all four binaries** (md5 per binary; `compare.mjs` reports `CHANGED 0`, `emitted C identical` on every function, verdict "no regression"), which is the proof that the hand-holding had become redundant and the reason it was taken as its own measured step. The two survivors, `isFlagTransparent` and `clobberedAfter`, moved into `flagModel.ts`, so there is now **one** copy of the x86 flag grammar rather than two hand-synced ones; `flagModel.test.ts`'s drift guard is inverted to fail if a *second* declaration appears anywhere under `src/disasm/decompile` or `corpus`. IRStmt dispatch sites 26 → 24, kind-reads 143 → 137, compiler-caught unchanged at 8 (`peek-a-bin-c33` stage 4, closing `peek-a-bin-wf7t`) (2026-08-20 19:35)

### Changed
- **`corpus/sweep.ts`'s polarity table gained a bit-test vocabulary** — `TAKEN` maps each jcc to the operator of the magnitude comparison its flags describe, which is a `cmp` model and the wrong vocabulary for a `bt`: CF is bit *n* of the operand, so `jb` means "that bit is set" and no `<`- or `>=`-shaped spelling of that exists. `BIT_TAKEN` gives `jb → !=` and `jae → ==` for a jcc whose **immediately preceding instruction** is a `bt`, decided from the machine text with no flag-transparency reasoning, so a `bt` one instruction further away keeps `TAKEN`'s expectation and is still reported MISMATCH. **A widening, not a weakening**: `NEG` maps `!=` and `==` to each other, so a bit-test guard at the wrong polarity is still INVERTED and one spelled as a magnitude comparison is still MISMATCH — negative-controlled by flipping the decompiler's `bittest` arm, which makes `npm run corpus` exit 1 with `inverted=8` on t64 and `inverted=5` on w64. Without it the gate reports 13 MISMATCH rows corpus-wide, every one of them provably correct output; contorting the emitted C into `0 < (x >> n & 1)` to satisfy the old table was considered and refused as gaming the oracle (peek-a-bin-frt8) (2026-08-21 15:20)
- **The metrics worker is handed the original `File` instead of a copy of its bytes** — after `peek-a-bin-7hg` moved checksum and entropy off the main thread, the one main-thread cost left in that path was `prepareBinaryArgs` slicing the argument: ~100 ms for a 253 MiB file, a large win for the entropy work but a **wash for the checksum alone** (138 ms computed inline against 148 ms to hand off). A `Blob` — and therefore the `File` the drop/browse handler already holds — is structured-cloneable *by reference*, so posting one is O(1) at any size and `metricsDispatch` reads the bytes itself with `Blob.arrayBuffer()`, on the worker thread. `FileMetricsArgs.buffer` becomes `source: ArrayBuffer | Blob`, `metricsDispatch` becomes `async` for that one reason (so its unknown-method branch now **rejects** rather than throwing synchronously — identical from `metrics.worker.ts`, which awaits inside its `try`), `FileLoader`'s `onFile` gains an optional third `File` argument, and `App.tsx` registers the handle beside the `bufferRef` assignment, only after the parse succeeded. **The copy path is not legacy and is the common case**: only drop/browse has a `File` — `loadRecentFile()` returns an `ArrayBuffer` out of IndexedDB and the demo binary arrives via `fetch().arrayBuffer()` — so two of the three load paths still copy, by construction rather than by accident, and a missing registration is simply the old behaviour. The registry is a `WeakMap<ArrayBuffer, Blob>`, so the `File` lives exactly as long as the buffer it describes and needs no teardown; the result cache stays keyed on the `ArrayBuffer`, so promise-sharing between the Headers and Sections tabs and eviction-on-rejection are unchanged. **What is measured is the equivalence, which is the actual correctness claim**: a `Blob`, a `File`, a multi-part Blob and an odd-length buffer all produce results `toEqual` the `ArrayBuffer` arm's over the same fixture, negative-controlled (making `bytesOf` ignore the Blob fails 8 tests), plus that `prepareBinaryArgs` passes a Blob through by identity with an empty transfer list and still copies a buffer sitting beside it. **What is NOT measured is the speed**: the O(1) post is a spec property plus 7hg's measurement of the copy it removes — the corpus binaries top out at ~180 KB, there is no 253 MiB image here, and nothing in this repo spawns a real worker or renders a component, so no `File` has ever crossed a real `postMessage`. The bytes were checked rather than assumed: hex patches are a read-time overlay (`state.hexPatches`, never written into `pe.buffer`; the patched download slices a copy), `checksumFile` and `parsePE` only read, and `pe.buffer` is the very object `FileReader` produced — the residual is an on-disk change after loading, where `Blob.size` is the snapshot state and cannot see it, but the File API requires the *read* to fail, which surfaces as a rejected metric every caller already handles (`peek-a-bin-ex2`) (2026-08-21 04:53)

- **The nesting variant of a switch arm is refused a second time, with the numbers recorded where the next reader will find them** — `armExit`'s docstring now carries what `structureFrom(target, switchStopAt, undefined, true)` inside `armBody` actually costs, measured at `39b1bb3` rather than argued. It reads better (gotos 3070 → 2983, labels 2517 → 2445 corpus-wide) and the 12 guards it takes out of the polarity audit's anchored set are **not** a loss — same guard text, `if`/`while`/`for` unmoved, 31 more guards anchorable than leave — but it closes its walk with the same unconditional `break` `armExit` exists to stop emitting: **30 of the 64 arms it walks end with a REACHABLE appended `break`** (20 t32, 10 w32), every one of them stopping on an already-visited block rather than the switch's stop set, four at `t32!sub_4045B1`'s `default` body. `corpus/armExits.ts` would see none of them, since the claimed-arm path stops going through `armExit` and `arms` falls 72 → 32 and 54 → 30 with `falseBreaks` still 0 (peek-a-bin-64gp) (2026-08-21 05:10)
- **Arity over-count is now a GATE at 0 in `npm run corpus`** — the upgrade `corpus/README.md` had recorded as a condition ("if the OVER count is ever driven to 0, make it a gate at 0") has been met and taken. No entry in `apitypes.ts` is variadic, so a call passing more arguments than the API declares passes one the machine never passed: `polarity inverted`'s character, not a baseline's, and the only thing that had ever separated them was that the count was not zero — 24 corpus-wide at `e22ba6e`, 16 after `peek-a-bin-f51x`, 6 after `peek-a-bin-6lmh`, 0 after `peek-a-bin-7r1l`. **A gate at 0 is not a threshold on an absolute** and so does not move when function detection does, which was the whole of the earlier refusal. **This changes CI**: a change that makes any emitted call pass more arguments than the table declares is now red rather than a number in a report. UNDER stays ungated — it is not zero and no threshold on it is justified — and rises in it are still judged in `compare.mjs`. The failing message names each row with its function, callee, both counts and the emitted line, because an over row is adjudicated against the real prototype and the audit cannot tell an invented argument from a wrong table entry. Validated by negative control: removing the one `readSinceWrite` line from `collectArgs64` makes `npm run corpus` exit 1 and name all six pre-`7r1l` rows (`peek-a-bin-7r1l`) (2026-08-20 18:55)

- **ARM64 code section decoded once per load instead of three times** — the linear sweep *is* the disassembly on A64, so `detectFunctions` (`bl` targets, switch dispatches), `hybridDisassemble` (the view) and `buildAllXrefs` (adrp pairs) each swept the whole `.text` for itself. They now share one decode inside the worker, through `WorkerState.arm64Sweep` (`Arm64SweepCache` in `src/disasm/arm64.ts`). Passing an `Instruction[]` between RPCs is deliberately *not* the fix — every element carries a `bytes` view, the case `src/workers/transfer.ts` exists to keep out of a message. Measured at `7082e66` by replaying a load's RPCs against the real dispatch: t64-arm.exe **4083 → 1361** `cs.disasm` calls, 7.32 → 2.44 MiB fed to Capstone, **407 → 166 ms** (median of 3); w64-arm.exe 3393 → 1131, 6.51 → 2.17 MiB, **328 → 129 ms**. The key is the section's **bytes**, compared byte-for-byte — both real ARM64 binaries base `.text` at 0x140001000, so an address-and-length key would be unsound on the only two files there are, and a content key needs no assumption about message order. Only the decode is cached: `disassembleArm64` is now `sweepArm64` + `decorateArm64Sweep`, and comments and the `.pdata` `source` marking are reapplied per caller (~5 ms on 27428 instructions), so every RPC's answer is unchanged — verified element-by-element against an uncached run on both binaries with real strings, a real IAT map and driver mode on. `sweepArm64` throws `Arm64DecodeRateError` before anything is stored, so the ARM64EC/ARM64X refusal cannot become a cached empty answer. The x86 path is untouched and the four x86 corpus binaries emit byte-identical C (`peek-a-bin-kis`) (2026-08-20 18:34)
- **One declaration of the structured-tree body traversal, and it is exhaustive** — `rewriteBodies` ("rebuild `stmt` with `f` applied to each of its nested statement lists") existed as a *verbatim* copy, docstring included, in both `structure.ts` and `cleanup.ts`, and `cleanup.ts` held two further specialisations of it under other names: `repairStmt` was it with `giveTrailingLabelsAStatement` and `cleanupStmt` was it with `cleanupPass`. Four hand-synced switches over `IRStmt` for one traversal — the shape `sections.ts`, `ripRelative.ts`, `funcInsns.ts` and `apiLists.ts` each exist to end. All four ended in `default:`, so a new statement kind carrying a nested body would have been returned unrecursed in every one of them, silently and with the typechecker saying nothing, and the copy you had not noticed is the one that bites. `bodiesOf`, the read half, moved with them. Both now live in `ir.ts` beside `walkExpr`/`walkStmts` and end in an exhaustive `const _exhaustive: never` — a **deliberate second decision, not a side effect of the de-duplication**: naming all twelve body-less kinds explicitly makes the next body-carrying kind a build error rather than a silent drop. Both directions were verified rather than asserted. Output-neutral: all **1127 emitted functions byte-identical** across t32/t64/w64/w32 by md5, `compare.mjs` reporting `CHANGED 0` of 3144 distinct jccs and "no regression" with every gate flat (842/842 gcc, 1495/1495 polarity, 994/994 offsetof fields, 0 throws, 0 stale version-0), both sides pinned to `e22ba6e`. And effective: a throwaway `IRStmt` kind added to the union fails the build at *both* new switches. The compiler-caught `IRStmt` dispatch count is now 8, measured by that probe — the docs had claimed 8 while these two did not exist, by crediting two `IRExpr`-only switches (peek-a-bin-svwt) (2026-08-20 15:55)
- **The view tabs are named and ordered in one place** — `AddressBar.tsx` spelled the nine display names a second time alongside `VIEW_TAB_LABELS` in `components/analysisNotice.ts`, and its 1–9 shortcut map spelled the order a third time alongside `VIEW_TABS` in `usePEFile.ts`, so a tab could be called one thing on its button and another in the notice telling you to open it, and the digit could disagree with the button at that position. The tab bar and `TAB_KEYS` are now both derived from the exported `VIEW_TABS`, taking labels from the map — which is `Record<ViewTab, string>` and fails the build on a missing tab, where the array would have silently dropped it from the bar. A test in `components/__tests__/analysisNotice.test.ts` pins the two against each other and a source guard fails if the literal labels come back (peek-a-bin-t40b) (2026-08-12 04:25)

### Fixed
- **A 32-bit self-move on x64 now states the zero-extension it performs** — `mov r8d, r8d` clears bits 63:32 of R8, and MSVC emits the idiom for exactly that; on PE32 `mov edi, edi` is the hot-patch pad and a genuine no-op. `liftBlock` emitted an ordinary `assign r8d = r8d` and **`copyPropagation` deleted it** — `isCopyStmt` admits any `assign` with `reg` on both sides, a versioned dest and neither being RSP, which `r8_2 = r8_1` satisfies — so every reader was rewritten to the *pre*-truncation version. Correctly, by that pass's own rule: nothing in `r8d = r8d` says the high half was cleared, so the truncation has to be in the expression. It is now `<canon64> = <canon64> & 0xFFFFFFFF`, the one spelling `foldExpr` already refuses to strip when the left operand is wider than the mask (`narrowEnoughForMask32`, peek-a-bin-6hw), gated on `is64`, 32-bit width and `canonReg !== "rsp"`. **The filed mechanism was wrong and named the wrong file** — `liftBlock`'s `mov` handler has no self-move test at all; the only one in `lifter.ts` is `firstCalleeSavedWrites`', which answers a different question and is untouched — and the filed population was 6 of 8. The two it missed are the strongest evidence: `mov eax, eax / sub rbp, rax` on each x64 binary emitted `rbp -= eax;`, the untruncated value under a 32-bit name, a wrong value in C that compiles. The other six are peek-a-bin-frt8's residue, where the self-move is the whole content of a `bt r12d/r13d, 0xc / jb` arm, so the arm lifted to nothing, `cleanup.ts` dropped the empty `if` and the region left the output with its `(0x8000 & r12d) == 0` test. Measured at base `6d5ae92`: `if` emitted **1934 → 1938 (t64)** and **1717 → 1719 (w64)**, statements lifted 7745 → 7750 and 6881 → 6884 (exactly the 5 and 3 sites), 5 of 279 and 3 of 275 functions changed, **t32/w32 byte-identical**. The restored pair folds into one `&&`, so the guards do **not** re-enter the polarity audit as predicted — `polarity guards audited` is exactly flat at 566/566 and 493/493, `guards w/o single compare` rises by the +4/+2 that are the new guards themselves, and `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` proves no pre-existing guard moved. Every gate flat (gcc 1127/1127, offsetof ratio 1.00, 0 inverted, 0 dropped, 0 callees lost, arity/staleGuards/staleReads/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/unencodableNames unmoved), **unrecovered values unmoved** at 91/21/17/72, loop shapes identical on all four (peek-a-bin-tez6) (2026-08-21 16:40)
- **A jump table whose entry 0 MSVC overlapped with the instruction in front of it is no longer decoded as code** — `unboundedTableExtent` requires the run of code-pointer words to *contain* the base the dispatch names, and at four sites in the corpus it does not, because the base's four bytes are the tail of a real instruction plus a `nop`: t32.exe 0x40b7f1 (base 0x40b804, overlapped by the 7-byte `jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + 0x40b884]` at 0x40b800) and 0x40b985, w32.exe 0x409491 and 0x409625. Entry 0 is unreachable at all four — the block is entered only when `test edi, 3` was non-zero, so the `and eax, 3` in front of the dispatch cannot produce 0 — and the assembler spent its slot on an instruction rather than four bytes of padding. So **12 bytes per site were gap-filled as instructions**, and the misaligned walk then ran off the end into the case body that follows: t32 0x40b813 `add byte ptr [ebx], ah` swallowed the `23` of `and edx, ecx` at 0x40b814 and the next five decodes were fiction laid over 21 bytes of real code, ending in `jmp 0x288402b` — an address outside the image. **The evidence for the fix is real rather than statistical: a byte that belongs to a decoded instruction cannot be a table entry**, so `overlappedTableExtent` starts the table at the first grid slot past that instruction. Three restrictions keep it as short as `unboundedTableExtent`, and each refuses something this corpus contains: the instruction must end *inside* the base slot (`base < end <= base + ptrSize`), which is the idiom and which refuses w32 0x409498 where the sweep covers the base with a 6-byte `add`; the run is counted **from the base**, so `MIN_UNBOUNDED_TABLE_ENTRIES` is applied to the table's own length and four entries means three readable words, which refuses t32 0x40b7f8 whose base is followed by nothing that resolves; and a base some recovered table already *dispatches to* is refused outright by the caller, which is what makes the negative-index shape (`sub ecx, 4` leaves ecx at 0xFFFFFFFC.., so the words read are *below* the base and are a table already recovered and spanned) refused as a property rather than by the two counts happening to disagree with it. So Shape 2 is refused three ways over, measured rather than argued. The evidence arrives by **deferral, not a second decode**: the overlapping instruction is at a *higher* address than the dispatch, so the linear `recent` window structurally cannot hold it, and `TableRead.deferredBase` hands the refusal back to the sweep, which settles it when it walks onto the instruction and drops it the moment it passes the base — address-monotone, a handful of live entries, no extents array and no extra Capstone call, where a forward decode probe would have re-decoded what the sweep is about to decode anyway and could disagree with it. The `and <index>, 3` present at all four sites is **corroboration from outside the rule** — it bounds the index to `[0, 3]`, exactly the four slots the overlap plus the run finds — and is deliberately not *used*: a mask is not a `cmp`, and it says nothing about which bytes are opcode, so on its own it would report a span covering four bytes of a real `jmp`. Measured base `6d5ae92` + the new audit, both sides pinned: **instructions 18032 → 18025 (t32) and 16611 → 16601 (w32)**, x64 **byte-identical** and every t64/w64 count unmoved; branches aimed outside the image **1 → 0** on each 32-bit binary; emitted C moved in exactly **1 function per 32-bit binary** (t32 `sub_40B780`, w32 `sub_409420`), where a region of fiction ending `eax = sub_288402B(); return eax;` — a call to a nonexistent function — becomes the CRT's real unrolled byte copy, read line by line against `objdump`, and three invented prelude declarations per binary disappear with it. `functions` 288/279/275/285, `jumpTables` 13/0/0/11, gcc 288/279/275/285 clean, `offsetof` 331/301/303/418 fields and 54/54/53/58 definitions all **ratio 1.00 and unmoved**, polarity 499/493/430 audited **0 inverted** with `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0` on all four, unrecovered values 91/21/17/72 unmoved, statements dropped 0, callees lost 0, loops 0 short, arity/staleGuards/staleReads/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/unencodableNames all flat, and **loop shapes identical**: `for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77. One `nop` per site leaves the view, which is the pre-existing all-padding gap rule working — each is a single 0x90 that was only ever decoded because the gap ran on into the table (peek-a-bin-xqxy) (2026-08-21 16:45)
- **A `push <imm>` / `pop <reg>` split across a branch is now a phi of immediates rather than a stale read** — `stackIdiom.ts`'s pairing is handed one block, so a `pop` whose `push` sat in a predecessor got no definition in SSA and every later read named the value from before the pop: `peek-a-bin-3axd`'s wrong-value defect one block further out, and invisible to every gate (gcc compiles it, the operator in a guard is right so polarity passes, it is not `__unrecovered_N`). The tempting rule — "every predecessor pushes the same immediate, so assign it at the `pop`" — **describes not one site in this corpus**: all five are MSVC selecting a *different* constant per arm of an `if`/`else if` chain and popping it once, so the value is a phi and the definition has to land in **each predecessor** with `buildSSA` building the phi. That is why it cannot live in `liftBlock`, which returns one block's statements: it is `pipeline.ts` **step 2b**, `crossBlockPopImmediates` + `liftCrossBlockPops` in `lifter.ts`, appending through **`pushBeforeTerminator`** because a predecessor already ends in an `IRBranch` and a plain `push` would land the definition after the guard that reads it. Defining the register at the push is EARLY, so four refusals make it sound: every predecessor must pair or none do (a partial answer leaves the phi's other operand naming the stale value, which reads as recovered while being wrong on one path, and a block the unwinder enters has no predecessor so the loop refuses it structurally); a pushing predecessor's only successor must be the `pop`'s block; its tail must not be a conditional jump; and the `pop` must be its block's **first** instruction, which makes the interval provably empty and is why no second machine-write grammar was written beside `firstCalleeSavedWrites`. The block-leader restriction **costs exactly 0, measured**: of the non-leader pops whose prefix touches neither the stack nor the register — 53 on t32, 50 on w32 — 0 would have paired. The definition carries the **pop's** address, matching the block-local form and letting `corpus/popReads.ts`'s `popsLifted` liveness check cover this rule too; the three corpus replicas of the lift loop each gained the call, and `stackIdiom.test.ts` now fails if a file calling `liftBlock(` does not also call `liftCrossBlockPops(` — a replica missing step 2b fails *quietly*, reporting rows the fix removed. Measured at base `6d5ae92`, both sides pinned: `popReads` **7 → 4 wrong on t32 and 6 → 4 on w32** over 5 → 2 and 4 → 2 pops with `popsLifted` 67 → 73 and 63 → 68, **x64 byte-identical** (an x86-only encoding, 0 sites), only 6 of 288 and 5 of 285 functions changed, **unrecovered values unmoved on all four** (91/21/17/72, tokens identical — this recovers a value rather than admitting a gap), `if`/`while`/`for` identical, `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0` and verdict "no regression", gcc 1127/1127, offsetof ratio 1.00 unmoved, polarity 0 inverted, staleReads/staleGuards/lostDefs/armExits/unencodableNames/arity all flat. The marker is **after** the inner call, not before it: there is no call between the pushes and it, so the obvious rule "stop the walk at an intervening call boundary" would never have fired. Such a call now gets no pushed arguments at all — deliberately an *admitted under-count* rather than a re-attribution to the outer call, which would be a guess in the invented-argument direction. The default is **deleted** rather than re-pointed, since a second absolute path is wrong on every machine but one; `preflight.ts` now searches `PEEK_CORPUS_DIR` (environment), then `PEEK_CORPUS_DIR` in a gitignored `.env` at the repo root, then `$XDG_DATA_HOME/peek-a-bin-corpus`, `~/.peek-a-bin-corpus` and `<repo>/corpus/binaries` — every candidate derived from `$XDG_DATA_HOME`, `$HOME` or the repo, never from anyone's home directory in particular. So `cmp eax, 5 / … / sub ecx, edx / jne` emitted `eax != 5` where the machine branches on `ecx - edx != 0`: right operator, wrong operands, and therefore invisible to the polarity gate. The walk now clears the flags on any instruction that is not `isFlagTransparent` — `flagResult.ts`'s own table, **exported rather than copied**, since a second copy of that grammar is exactly what that module exists to prevent — and clears them before each `cmp`/`test` too, so a compare whose operands cannot be parsed does not leave an older one's standing. The corpus occurrences are two, both MSVC's branchless unsigned size check (`cmp / sbb eax,eax / inc eax / jne`, t32 0x4078de and w32 0x4070e3, objdump-confirmed): the `inc` sets the ZF the `jne` reads, and the base emitted `eax != arg_0x10` against a compare three instructions dead. - **A guard can no longer name a compared operand a later instruction overwrote** — the block's statements are emitted *above* the `if`, so `cmp eax, 5 / mov eax, edx / je` printed `eax = edx;` and then `if (eax != 5)`, reading the new EAX where the machine compared the old one. **The argument was already in the repo, made for the other path**: `flagResultSetter`'s condition 6 has refused this since `peek-a-bin-b531`, with a docstring — *"`dec ecx / mov ecx, edx / jne` leaves ECX holding EDX where the guard would read it, so naming it would state a different test entirely"* — that is verbatim true of the `cmp` path. So the two paths in the same function disagreed about whether it mattered. That check is now **one predicate**, `clobberedAfter` in `flagResult.ts`, asked by both: condition 6 asks it of the *result* register, `extractCondition` asks it of the `cmp`'s *operands*, walking the recovered condition for the register names and derefs it is spelled with. It covers the memory case the bead called for (a compare against memory spoiled by an intervening store), is width-blind (`mov al, 1` spoils `eax`), and counts `push`/`pop` as writing RSP so an `[esp + N]` operand is not read across a stack adjustment. **56 wrong-operand guards on the page at `e22ba6e`** (29/5/3/21 t32/t64/w64/w32 including jitf's 2), all refused. - **A statement appended to another block can no longer land past its terminator** — `destroySSA` lowering a phi into a predecessor and `loopInvariantCodeMotion` hoisting into a preheader both `push`ed to the *end* of another block's statement list, which is correct only while no terminator exists in the IR. A branch statement makes "end of the statement list" and "end of the block's straight-line code" different places, and a plain `push` lands a definition *after* the branch that reads it — a read preceding its own definition, in the preheader (`ctx.idom.get(header)`), the likeliest block in a function to end in exactly such a branch. Both now go through `pushBeforeTerminator` (`ir.ts`). Nothing constructs a branch at either site today, so both still take the plain-`push` path and the corpus **cannot observe this at all** — which is precisely why it is pinned by unit test (`blockTerminator.test.ts`, 5 cases including repeated insertion) rather than by a measurement (2026-08-20 14:05)

### Changed
- **Two verification claims are narrowed to what is actually measured** — `CLAUDE.md` stated "a register is never named for a value it no longer holds" without qualification, and it is **false as written**. What the gate covers is every surviving *version-0* read whose register an *SSA-visible* dominating write has changed; its site filter attributes a phi's definition to the phi's own block while `destroySSA` materialises the copy in each *predecessor*, and a predecessor routinely dominates blocks the phi block does not — so the gate prints 0 over 12 provably wrong reads (`peek-a-bin-fppy`, fix filed rather than applied because it turns the gate red until `peek-a-bin-pzws` is fixed). Separately, `gcc -fsyntax-only` is **structurally blind** to a wrong register *name*: `preludeFor` declares every undeclared identifier as its own `long`, so `r13` and `r13d` compile as unrelated variables and the 842/842 ratio cannot move on that class — it stayed green over six functions that each read a name nothing assigned. Also corrects the switch-site count: the documented 64 counts `IRExpr` and `IRStmt` together; for `IRStmt` alone it is **91, of which only 8 are compiler-caught**. **Superseded on 2026-08-20**: both `peek-a-bin-fppy` and `peek-a-bin-pzws` are now fixed, so the first claim no longer needs that qualifier — see the two entries under Fixed. The `gcc` blindness and the switch-site count still stand (2026-08-20 14:05)

- **Version-0 register reads keep the value they name** — a read of a register's SSA entry value that a strictly dominating block had already overwritten was emitted as the bare register, which by then held something else; where the old repair did fire it was taken at the reading block's top, past the damage, so `rcx_0` named the wrong value under a name that looked recovered. `destroySSA` now takes the copy at the function's entry, the one point where the register provably holds version 0, and routes every stale read of that version to it. Measured over the four distlib binaries: wrong names 28/78/28/78 -> 0, spoiled copies 13/19/13/18 -> 0, with gcc, polarity, offsetof, callees-lost and statement-drops unchanged (2026-08-20 04:42)

- **A sub-width write to an x64 argument register counts as argument setup** — `mov ecx, 1 / call ExitProcess` emitted `ExitProcess()`, with no argument and no `ecx = 1` either: `collectArgs64` asks `RegState` how many *leading* fastcall registers the block wrote, `RegState` keys its defs by the literal operand text, so the probe for `"rcx"` missed the def under `"ecx"` and broke out at that position — and with no reader left in the IR, DCE then deleted the write. The exit code was simply gone from the output, in valid, well-typed C that no corpus audit could see (gcc is happy, polarity is unaffected, and the callee guard never looks at arguments). Fixed with a new `RegState.wroteAnyAlias`, which asks the width-blind question — *did this block touch RCX at all* — over the width-exact map, rather than canonicalising the map itself: a def keyed by `rcx` would record `mov cl, 2`'s one byte as if it were eight, and on x86-64 a byte write does not even clear the upper bits a 32-bit one does. Only *whether* an argument is emitted changes; what is emitted is still the plain `irReg(reg, 8)`, so peek-a-bin-urs's re-expansion of the computing expression cannot come back. Measured against the pinned `base-cee6f91` corpus: **156 of 279 t64 functions and 154 of 275 w64 functions changed emitted text**, zero-argument calls 927 → 560 (t64) and 892 → 538 (w64), and all ten `ExitProcess()` across the two binaries now carry their exit code. The external oracle is the Win32 signature: over the ~130 calls `apitypes.ts` declares, emitted arity that *exactly matches the declaration* went 64 → 90 (t64) and 70 → 96 (w64), under-count 60 → 34, and over-count did **not** move (3 pre-existing on each, none of them new). No regression on any audit — gcc 842/842, polarity 1487 → 1490 correct with 0 inverted and 0 mismatch, offsetof 975/975 across 169 definitions → 994/994 across 171, 0 callees lost, 0 throws, 0 dangling gotos, loop exits 0 short. t32 and w32 are **byte-identical**, as expected: `collectArgs32` reads pushes from the instruction stream and never consults `RegState`. One measured cost, small and in a pre-existing class: an 8-byte read of an argument register can flip `registerSpeller`'s width choice for a phi-lowered copy, so register families assigned under more than one spelling in the same function went 255 → 263 (t64) and 238 → 243 (w64), across the same set of functions as before. Three of the five new `pipeline.test.ts` fixtures verified to fail without the fix (peek-a-bin-qb2x) (2026-08-20 03:30)
- **A PE with no executable section is explained instead of spinning forever** — `App`'s analysis effect bails at `if (!textSection) return`, and that return sits *above* the first `SET_ANALYSIS_PHASE` it dispatches, so the phase stayed on `"extracting-strings"` — the last value `handleFile` set — with every other dispatch in the effect downstream of it. Nothing could move it, and `isAnalyzing` was a hand-written `phase !== "idle" && !== "ready" && !== "failed"` chain, so the status bar and the sidebar spun indefinitely and the user was told nothing. Reachable on ordinary input, not just malformed: a resource-only DLL — a satellite or MUI file — genuinely has no executable section. The bail-out now dispatches a new terminal phase `"no-code"`, and `analysisNotice()` gains a fourth kind, `"no-code-section"`: "This image has no executable section, so there is nothing to disassemble — a resource-only DLL … is the ordinary case. Nothing failed …", followed by the tabs that *are* populated, derived from `PARSER_DERIVED_TABS` so the prose cannot disagree with the buttons rendered beside it. `"failed"` was deliberately not reused — the parse succeeded and nothing went wrong — and the new kind ranks directly below `"unsupported-arch"`, since an ARM32 resource-only DLL should be told about its machine type, the fact that is true of every such file rather than only this one. The disassembly panel takes the same explanatory arm as the architecture refusal instead of falling through to "Address 0x… is not within any section". The spinner half is fixed structurally: `ANALYSIS_IN_PROGRESS` in `usePEFile.ts` is a `Record<AnalysisPhase, boolean>` that both the status bar and the sidebar now read, so a phase added later fails the build rather than silently defaulting to "still analysing". **Unverified**: nothing renders a component in this repo, so the reducer branch, the pure decision, the terminal-phase record and the wiring of all four surfaces are tested, and no banner, panel or spinner has been observed. 14 new tests, 11 of which fail without the change (peek-a-bin-bo3b) (2026-08-20 03:05)
- **An indirect tail `jmp` says a transfer happens there, instead of emitting nothing** — a successorless `jmp` through a register is deliberately not lifted as a call, because `resolveNamedTarget` can put no name on the target and an invented callee is worse than an admitted gap. But saying nothing is a different claim from saying "a call happens here that I could not name", and it was the first: `t32!sub_402C5A` (MSVC's `DecodePointer` dispatch) emitted C that decodes a pointer and then does nothing whatever with it, the `jmp eax` through the decoded pointer being simply absent. Such a site now emits `/* unlifted: indirect jmp through eax */`, reusing the existing `raw`/unlifted mechanism — no new IR kind, no synthesized call. Only a **bare register** operand is reported: the other shape that ends a successorless block unnameably is `jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + 0x40b900]`, an unrecovered jump table, which is a gap in table recovery rather than an indirect call and stays silent (a *recovered* table never reaches this code — its case targets are block leaders, so the dispatch block has successors and a `switch` is emitted). Measured over the corpus at `cee6f91`: 115 successorless `jmp` sites, 18 of them emitting nothing, split exactly 4 register-shaped (t32 0x402c70/0x404480, w32 0x402ec4/0x4046e0 — all 32-bit, 0 on either x64 binary) against 14 table dispatches. Corpus compare against a pinned base: those **4 functions and no others** changed emitted text, gcc-clean 842/842 held (288/288, 279/279, 275/275, 285/285 per binary), offsetof 975/975 across 169 definitions, polarity 1487/1487 with 0 inverted and 0 guards changed by jcc address, 0 callees lost, 0 throws. The two `DecodePointer` functions gained the `else` arm the guard always had (peek-a-bin-xerm) (2026-08-20 03:10)
- **A phi operand whose version the register no longer holds is repaired instead of emitted as a stale read** — the machine executed `xor edi, edi` and the emitted C said `edi = ebx`, a copy of a *live* register where the instruction wrote zero: valid, well-typed C stating something the machine does not do. Not the `xor`, and not a value-numbering error — GVN forwards `edi_14 = 0` to an equal `ebx_1` whose definition dominates, which is correct *in SSA*. It goes wrong because `destroySSA` ends SSA by stripping versions rather than splitting live ranges, and EBX is rewritten in between, so `ebx_1` and `ebx_9` collapse to one name and the lowered phi copy reads the reload. `splitStaleReads` already existed for exactly this and its docstring already named GVN as the cause — its scan simply never visited a phi operand, which was the one place GVN had put it. It now treats a phi operand as a read and asks the staleness question at the predecessor's exit, where `destroySSA` materialises the copy, so **any** operand carrying a version the predecessor no longer holds is repaired regardless of which pass put it there. The same-canonical-register flavour, previously skipped as a self-copy and emitting nothing at all, is fixed with it. RSP is excluded because `push`/`pop`/`call` are unmodelled and the repair would relocate a read of it — measured, not assumed: without the guard one function gained ten `esp = rsp_N` lines. 248 of 1127 functions changed, every change additive. No regression: guards changed by originating jcc address **0 on all four binaries**, polarity 0 inverted with correct 1471 → 1487, gcc 842/842, offsetof 975/975 across 169 definitions, 0 callees lost, 0 throws, 0 dangling gotos. Fixture in `pipeline.test.ts` verified to fail without the fix (peek-a-bin-21ey) (2026-08-12 18:03)
- **A switch bounded by a register is recovered, and its table is no longer decoded as code** — a 32-bit switch table lives in `.text` immediately after the function that dispatches through it and is uncovered by construction (recursive descent never walks into it), so phase-2 gap fill decoded its case addresses as instructions: six phantom conditional jumps out of the 33 bytes at t32 `0x4086a3`, three more from the same shape in w32, all aiming past the end of the function they were attributed to. Those nine were the entire remaining residue of peek-a-bin-g7yp. Fixed at the cause rather than the symptom — detection now handles a bound compared against a *register* (`push N / pop ecx / cmp eax, ecx` and `mov ecx, N / cmp eax, ecx`), and recovering the switch marks the table's bytes as data via a new `DetectResult.jumpTableSpans` threaded through to `hybridDisassemble`, so the phantom instructions are never generated rather than filtered afterwards. The refusals are the load-bearing half and are tested individually: it declines when the stack moved between the push and the pop, when a call sits between the constant and the compare, and when the register holds a copy of another register, because a wrongly recovered table marks real code as data. Verified against the real corpus: all seven phantom addresses at t32 `0x4086a4..0x4086c1` are gone, and t64 holds at 279 functions, unmoved as intended. The standing regression set was run afterwards against pinned worktrees and found **no regression on any of 8 audits, all ratios 1.00**: polarity 0 inverted / 0 mismatch with t64 and w64 guard-for-guard identical, gcc-clean 842/842, struct offsetof 975/975 fields across 169 definitions, 0 dangling gotos, 0 callees lost, 0 throws, loop-exit coverage 0 short. Four functions gained a `switch` with 8 cases where none was emitted before, which is the recovery working. Two emitted-text quality costs are tracked separately: frame-slot naming is locally lost in those functions (`var_420` → `*(int32_t*)(ebp_1 - 0x420)`), and 19 small blocks base emitted are absent. An earlier note here said the regression set was unrun and that t32's function count moved 293 → 288; both are superseded — the count was unchanged by this commit at 288, having moved at `95229d3` (peek-a-bin-y1di, peek-a-bin-mk42, peek-a-bin-gtlf) (2026-08-12 05:20)
- **A branch whose flags come from arithmetic no longer loses its condition to dead-code elimination** — `dec ecx / jnz` sets ZF from the decrement, and the structurer spells that guard `ecx != 0` by naming the destination register. But the lifter emits no `eflags` assign for `dec`, so the only reader of `ecx` is the flags, the flags are not in the IR, and `deadCodeElimination` deleted the arithmetic before the structurer ever looked — 127 blocks across the three reference binaries, every one emitting `__unrecovered_N`. DCE already held one definition live by hand for this exact reason (the last `eflags` assign of a Jcc block, peek-a-bin-ua8); it now does the same for the destination of the block's last flag-setting instruction. Unrecovered guards went **374/6147 → 251/6149** and blocks recovered by the flag-result path **325 → 447 of 601**, with the result-gone class **127 → 3** (the survivors are defs folding inlined into a use, where nothing is lost). The predicate deciding which definition to protect is **shared** with the structurer as `flagResult.ts`'s `flagResultSetter` rather than copied into `ssaopt.ts`, so protection cannot drift wider than the use it serves; it reads the *instruction stream* rather than the in-flight IR, because an overwriting `mov` that is itself dead disappears on DCE's first iteration and would otherwise make protection depend on which pass iteration asked. Regressions flat: every pre-existing guard byte-identical by jcc address (identical=1472, verdictChanged=0, onlyInA=0), polarity 1505 checked with 0 inverted, 0 dangling gotos, 0 structurer drops, 0 callees lost, 0 throws, gcc-clean 842/842, struct offsetof 975/975 fields. Emitted text +0.06–0.09%. This is scaffolding around one design flaw — branch conditions recovered from instructions rather than from the IR — and peek-a-bin-c33 deletes `flagResult.ts` and both call sites when it lands (peek-a-bin-pu06) (2026-08-12 05:06)
- **The hex entropy strip is redrawn when the display's pixel ratio or the pane's width changes** — the draw effect read `window.devicePixelRatio` once into its closure and depended on `[showEntropy, entropyBlocks]`, so nothing re-ran it when the ratio changed. Dragging the window to a display of a different density, or a browser zoom (Chrome folds zoom into `devicePixelRatio`), left the strip sized for the old ratio and upscaled by the browser — exactly the blurriness the HiDPI sizing removed. There is no event for `devicePixelRatio` and a `ResizeObserver` cannot see it either, since it reports CSS pixels and a density change moves none of them; the one signal available is a media query naming the *current* ratio, which stops matching when the ratio changes and is therefore single-use, so the listener re-arms from the new ratio each time it fires (`dprMediaQuery` in `src/utils/entropy.ts`, which sanitises the ratio because `(resolution: NaNdppx)` does not throw — `matchMedia` returns a list that never matches and never fires, making a dead listener indistinguishable from a working one). The same effect also missed every resize too small to cross `entropyBlocksForWidth`'s 64-block quantum: the CSS width moved, the stored width deliberately did not, and the backing store kept its old size while the browser stretched it. The canvas now keeps itself painted — a `ResizeObserver` on the element plus the ratio listener, neither going through state, so a resize drag repaints without re-rendering the hex view — and both width and ratio are re-read on every draw. The "has the block budget actually moved" rule came out of the callback into `nextStripWidth`, where the two cases an equality test gets wrong are stated and tested: an unmeasured `0` shares its budget with a very narrow strip and must always be replaced, and a non-finite measurement would reach `entropyBlockSizeFor` as a NaN block size, i.e. an empty strip reporting no error anywhere. **Nothing renders a component in this repo**, so the listener lifecycle, the observer and the repaint are reasoned and typechecked, not observed; what the 15 new tests pin is the query string, its agreement with the ratio the geometry would draw at, and the width decision (peek-a-bin-oqp) (2026-08-12 04:17)
- **The decompiler's constant folder no longer truncates 64-bit values to 32 bits on the identity and unary paths** — every JavaScript bitwise operator coerces its operands to *int32* first, and `fold.ts` had three places left that relied on one. `x & 0xFFFFFFFF → x` and `x | 0xFFFFFFFF → 0xFFFFFFFF` were applied with no width test on the left operand: correct at 32 bits, but on a 64-bit operand the first is a real truncation of the high half and dropping it emits a value keeping bits the instruction cleared, and the second discards the high half of `x` that the OR leaves alone. Both now consult `knownWidth()`, which reads the width off a register, deref, cast or binary and deliberately *ignores* `IRConst.size` — that records the image's mode, 8 for every immediate in a 64-bit binary, not the width the instruction wrote, so trusting it would call `eax & 0xffffffff` a 64-bit operation. The `-1` spellings of both identities are all-ones at every width and stay unconditional. Unary `~` had the same int32 truncation (`~0x100000000` is -1, not -0x100000001) and is now redone in BigInt as `x ^ -1` whenever the operand does not survive `ToInt32`, which is precisely when the two answers differ. Finally, `+`, `-` and `*` are exact double arithmetic on the way in but can leave the exactly-representable range on the way out, so the switch now declines to fold a result past 2^53 instead of printing a rounded constant.
