The copy is written as `esi_1 = arg; esi = esi_1;` rather than appended, because branch conditions are built from `RegState` and are not in the statement list `foldBlock` counts uses over: appending made the definition look single-use and left guards naming a variable nothing assigns (119 such reads over the three binaries; this form adds none). Split names follow the reads, so 32-bit output gets `eax_1`, not an `rax_1` naming a register its disassembly never mentions (t32: 229 → 10). Measured over t32/t64/w64: 0 distinct callees lost, 0 functions throwing, 0 dangling gotos, polarity 294/466/403 correct and 0 inverted, gcc-clean 996/1001, struct layouts 149/149 (up from 145, all correct), +153/+199/+183 emitted lines (peek-a-bin-bld) (2026-08-11 23:05)

- **The MCP server builds string, import and data xrefs and a call graph** — `src/mcp/disasm.ts`'s `buildXrefs` had no caller anywhere in the repo: `FileSession.loadFile` built only `buildXrefMap` (per-instruction refs), so on every architecture an MCP client saw no string references, no import references, no data references and no call graph, while the browser had all four. `loadFile` now calls it and stores the maps; `get_xrefs` reports `stringRefs`/`importRefs`/`dataRefs`/`calls`/`calledBy` alongside what it already returned, plus the string value or the `lib!func` behind the address; a new `get_call_graph` tool and `pe://{fileId}/callgraph` resource expose the graph; `pe://{fileId}/strings` and `/imports` carry per-entry `xrefCount`/`xrefs`. Cost, measured through `loadFile` on the five distlib images (medians of 5, quiet machine): 72/74/57 ms on t32/t64/w64 against 389/297/264 ms loads. ARM64 pays 19/16 ms rather than 175/140 ms because `buildXrefs` now takes the caller's already-decoded instructions — there is no worker boundary on this path, so passing them is free, which is exactly what `workers/transfer.ts` forbids on the browser side. Function, instruction and xref counts are byte-identical to computing the maps standalone (peek-a-bin-0d0, peek-a-bin-kis) (2026-08-11 21:25)
- **`detectAnomalies` no longer walks the whole file on the main thread** — it called `validateChecksum` plus its own per-section entropy walk synchronously inside `handleFile`, measured at ~2.0 s of frozen main thread on a 201 MiB synthetic PE (the bead's quieter measurement was 910 ms). It now takes the metrics the metrics worker already computes for the Headers and Sections tabs — cached per `ArrayBuffer`, so whichever consumer asks first pays and the rest share it — leaving **0.04 ms** on the main thread plus the one-time argument copy. Files at or below `MAX_SYNC_FILE_METRIC_BYTES` (1 MiB, i.e. every ordinary binary) still compute inline, so no spinner appears where there was none. A reply for a file that is no longer loaded is dropped by identity comparison, the same guard `hooks/asyncMetricState.ts` uses; if the worker fails, every check that needs no whole-file walk still runs and an `info` anomaly records that the other two did not (peek-a-bin-vrl) (2026-08-11 21:25)
- **A malformed section table no longer fails the whole load** — `analysis/anomalies.ts` carried its own copy of the entropy walk, and the copy had drifted from `utils/entropy.ts`'s in three ways. A `pointerToRawData` past EOF, a negative offset or a negative length threw `RangeError` out of it; in `App.tsx` that call sits inside the `try` around `parsePE`, so a crafted section table failed the load and reported itself as a parse error, and in the MCP server it rejected `load_pe`. The copy is gone. Its *other* difference was the correct one and was kept: a section that starts inside the file and overruns the end is scored over the bytes that are actually there, where the shared function scored it 0 — which read as "empty" and dropped the high-entropy warning for exactly the truncated packed section the check exists to catch (peek-a-bin-vrl) (2026-08-11 21:25)
- **The hex entropy strip stops drawing off the edge of its own canvas** — the block count was a fixed cap of 4096 while the draw clamped each block to a 2 px bar, so on a ~1000 px strip every block past the 500th was computed and then drawn outside the canvas, invisible; meanwhile the click and hover handlers mapped x to a block using the *unclamped* width, so the bar under the cursor was not the bar being reported. The count now follows the strip's measured width (`entropyBlocksForWidth`, one block per 2 CSS px, quantized to 64 so a resize drag changes nothing until it crosses a whole step — no debounce, no timer, no stale window), and the draw uses the exact quotient the handlers use. A 1 MiB section: 4096 blocks in 40 ms and 4096 `fillRect`s becomes 512 blocks in 10 ms and 512 rects; 4 MiB: 61 ms → 22 ms. Above ~64 MiB the histogram dominates and the compute is unchanged — the win there is the draw and the correctness (peek-a-bin-5cr) (2026-08-11 21:25)
- **The whole analysis chain no longer runs twice per file** — `SET_STRINGS` replaces `state.peFile` with a new object, which is the dependency of the detection effect, so when the extracted strings landed the chain re-ran end to end: a second `detectFunctions` over the same bytes, a second `SET_FUNCTIONS`, a second `buildAllXrefs`, and `analysisPhase` bouncing from "ready" back to "detecting-functions". It is now keyed on the file's buffer, which is what actually identifies the image. The first xref build also reads the newest string map through a render-assigned ref, so the rebuild that late strings would trigger is skipped when it would use the same map — identity comparison, so it cannot skip across files. Per load that removes one `detectFunctions` (279-668 ms on the distlib images) and one to two `buildAllXrefs` (57-175 ms each) (peek-a-bin-kis) (2026-08-11 21:25)

- **A switch case whose block another switch already took says where it goes** — MSVC emits one jump table per copy-direction/alignment path of `memcpy` and lands them all on the same few tail blocks (t32's `sub_40B780`: nine tables, two distinct target sets), and a block is emitted once, so the first switch structured took every body and the rest emitted `case VAL_0x1: break;` — true about the case value and silent about what the case does, indistinguishable from a case that really is empty. Such an arm now emits `goto loc_…` to the label the block was emitted under: no second copy of the statements, and the reader is told where the case goes. Guarded by whether that label actually exists, since the short-circuit fold consumes blocks without emitting anything for them, and a `goto` naming one of those would not compile. Empty-looking case arms over the three distlib binaries: 7 → 2, and both survivors are cases whose block really did fold to nothing. gcc-clean count, dangling gotos, statement drops and struct layouts all unchanged (peek-a-bin-dp6) (2026-08-11 21:20)

- **Arithmetic on a recovered address is emitted as the byte arithmetic the instruction performed** — a machine `add`/`sub` counts bytes; C's `+`/`-` counts pointee objects once an operand has a pointer type, so `p->field_0x10 + 8` on a `struct_1 *` field compiled and meant 0x80 bytes on, and `PVOID - struct_4 *` (a CRT `FILE`'s `_ptr - _base`) did not compile at all — the last 5 gcc errors over t32/t64/w64. The pointer operands of `+`/`-` now carry a `(uintptr_t)` cast; the field declarations keep the types their evidence gave them, so which slot was read as an address is still visible. Bitwise operators deliberately get no cast: a pointer masked with a constant (peek-a-bin-h89) is a contradiction between two claims rather than a spelling problem, and emit has no evidence about which claim is wrong. 1001/1001 functions over the three distlib binaries now compile under `gcc -std=gnu89 -fsyntax-only`, up from 996; 15 casts in 8 functions (peek-a-bin-d8t, peek-a-bin-q30) (2026-08-11 21:10)
- **A sub-register read names storage the emitted function writes** — registers reach the output as undeclared free variables, one per *name*, so `while (dx != 0)` around a body whose only assignment is to `edx` was a loop that as C could never terminate (t64's `wcslen`). An 8- or 16-bit register read that the function never assigns is now spelled as an explicit narrowing of the narrowest wider alias it does assign — `(uint16_t)edx` — with the width taken from the register name and the *signedness* from the operator, since `(uint8_t)edx >= 0` would be constantly true where `js` on DL tests bit 7. A name the body does assign is left exactly as it is, and `eax`-width names are never respelled because emit is not told whether it is looking at 32- or 64-bit code. 691 narrowings in 154 of 1001 functions (peek-a-bin-uxm) (2026-08-11 21:10)
- **A driver's dispatch switch names its control codes again** — narrowing IOCTL decoding to a control-code argument removed 1475 false annotations from user-mode binaries and took the real kernel-driver case with it, where the codes are `case` labels on `irpSp->Parameters.DeviceIoControl.IoControlCode` rather than arguments. `TypeContext.isDriver`, set by `inferTypes` from the import table it is already handed (`importsKernelModule`, one of `detectDriver`'s own reasons), gates a switch whose labels are *all* plausible control codes and of which there are at least two. None of the three test binaries is a driver, so this is pinned by a constructed test only (peek-a-bin-i7v) (2026-08-11 21:10)

### Added

- **`.rdata` reaches function detection, so x64 switches are visible** — `src/disasm/dataWindows.ts` builds the readable data spans of a loaded image (the shared `isDataSection` predicate, minus discardable sections and `.rsrc`) and `FileSession.loadFile` and the browser's analysis chain now pass them to `detectFunctions` as `options.dataWindows`. On a hand-built x64 PE whose third function dispatches through a table in `.rdata`, the whole pipeline goes from 2 recovered jump tables to 3 and emits the `switch` with its three case bodies instead of a bare bounds check. They cross the worker boundary **flattened** — one top-level buffer plus plain-number spans, packed by `packDataWindows` and rebuilt by `unpackDataWindows` — because `workers/transfer.ts` transfers top-level binary arguments only, and every window is a view onto the whole loaded file whose structured clone would copy the entire file once per window. Cost on the distlib binaries: 15-44 KiB of windows against a 54-110 KiB code section, ~0.05 ms to build and pack (peek-a-bin-g17) (2026-08-11 16:45)
- **ARM64 xrefs are no longer discarded** — `src/workers/dispatch.ts` and `src/mcp/disasm.ts` route `buildAllXrefs` for an ARM64 image to `buildArm64Xrefs` instead of returning four empty arrays. t64-arm.exe: 81 string refs over 65 strings, 194 import refs over 86 IAT slots, 1259 call edges from 373 callers, 378 data refs over 138 targets; w64-arm.exe: 87 / 70, 203 / 92, 1086 from 331, 365 / 140 — all previously zero. The A64 reader takes decoded instructions, so the sweep is redone from the bytes the call already carries (155 ms for t64-arm.exe's 110 KiB / 27428 instructions), exactly as the x86 branch re-decodes them inside `buildAllXrefs`; shipping an `Instruction[]` would put a per-instruction `bytes` view in every message, which is what `transfer.ts` exists to keep out. The x86 route is untouched and its xref counts are byte-identical (peek-a-bin-aq5) (2026-08-11 16:45)
- **`detectFunctions` can be given data windows outside the code section** — `options.dataWindows` is a list of `{ base, bytes }` spans keyed by virtual address, searched after the code window when a jump table is read. It exists because x64 compilers put switch tables in `.rdata`, which is outside the byte window detection is handed, so the x64 RVA-table support below can only reach a table that happens to sit inside `.text` until a caller supplies those bytes. The callers now do — see the entry above (peek-a-bin-g17) (2026-08-11 16:45)
- **ARM64 control flow, jump arrows and address xrefs** — `src/disasm/arm64Operands.ts` is the single A64 branch/address grammar (`b`, `b.<cc>`, `bl`, `br`/`blr` incl. the PAuth forms, `cbz`/`cbnz`, `tbz`/`tbnz`, `ret`, and the `adrp`+`add`/`ldr` address idiom), following the `ripRelative.ts` precedent rather than a fourth hand-rolled copy. `buildCFG`, `layoutCFG`, `parseBranchTarget` and the `JumpArrows` guard now read it: t64-arm.exe goes from 659 blocks / **0 edges** to 6641 blocks / 9287 edges (409 of 539 functions multi-block, was 85), w64-arm.exe from 611 / 0 to 5810 / 8096. Matching is by exact mnemonic — `brk` is not a `br` — and the x86 arms are untouched, so t32/t64/w64 produce byte-identical edge lists. `src/disasm/arm64Xref.ts` adds `buildArm64Xrefs` on top of the pair reader: 81 string refs, 194 import refs and 1259 call-graph edges on t64-arm.exe where there were none (peek-a-bin-8bj, peek-a-bin-erb) (2026-08-11 15:45)

- **Real-binary verification pass** — three MSVC-compiled PEs (PE32+, PE32 and ARM64) driven through the tool headlessly for the first time, 1475 functions; every suite until now was synthetic. The PE parser matches an independently written from-spec reader on sections, imports, exports, imphash, resources, checksum and `.pdata` for all three files (pefile is not installed here, so the reference is hand-written rather than pefile). The condition-polarity fix holds on real compiler output: 450 emitted `if` guards, zero inverted, across `je`/`jne`/`js`/`jns`/`jge`/`jl`/`ja`/`jae`/`jb`/`jbe`. Control-flow structuring does not: `detectLoops` fabricates a loop for every if-without-else triangle, so ~86% of reported loops do not exist, and non-taken branch bodies are dropped from the emitted C. The measured performance envelope (disassembly ~1.2 s and ~68 MiB of heap per MiB of `.text`, linear to 4 MiB; `parsePE` ~0.3 ms on a 100 KB PE) is recorded in `docs/architecture.md`, along with the caveat that large-image figures come from a synthetic PE — no real PE over 273 KB exists on this machine (2026-08-11 05:50)
- **Nested struct detection** — a field whose loaded value is then used as a struct base is declared as a pointer to that struct (`struct_1*`) rather than a bare integer, and chains resolve in one pass. The typedef collection follows those references transitively, so an emitted block can never name a struct it does not also declare. Attribution is flow-insensitive in the same way the base alias map is: if a register holds values loaded from two different fields over the function, the entry is dropped rather than guessed, because a missed nesting costs a `uint32_t` where a `struct_1*` was due while a wrong one declares a field to point at an object it never points at. A type already established from a callee signature wins over the nesting. A self-referencing object (a linked list) emits one typedef, not an infinite expansion. This replaces the `findNestedStructUse` stub that always returned `null` and whose three-round driver loop was dead code (2026-08-10 22:51)
- **Full Content Security Policy** — the policy is defined once in `build/csp.ts` and consumed by both deployment paths: a build-only Vite plugin (`apply: "build"`) injects it as a `<meta>` tag into `dist/index.html` for GitHub Pages, which cannot set response headers, and `nginx.conf` carries the same policy as a real header for Docker. `build/csp.test.ts` fails if the two drift. It cannot be committed into `index.html` because that file is also the dev entry point and Vite injects an inline React Refresh preamble there. `frame-ancestors` is deliberately omitted from the meta variant — browsers ignore it in a meta tag — but retained in the header. **The policy has not yet been exercised in a browser** (2026-08-10 14:19)
- **Engine and parser test suites** — the modules that had no tests now have them, and writing them surfaced ten real bugs (see Fixed). New suites for `functionDetect`, `lifter`, `structure`, `structs`, `promote`, `cfgpatterns`, `metadata`, `authenticode`, `resources`, `driver`, `appReducer`, the annotation undo/redo stack, `parseViewTab`, row search, `useMcpSync`, `recentFiles`, `entropy`, `fuzzyMatch` and `clampPopup` (2026-08-10 14:58)
- **`src/pe/ordinalTables.ts`** — ordinal→name tables for `ws2_32.dll` (196 entries), `wsock32.dll` (75) and `oleaut32.dll` (425), transcribed from pefile's `ordlookup` and verified entry-by-entry against it. Previously a ~10-entry subset, which meant imphash disagreed with every other tool for ordinal imports (2026-08-10 14:51)
- **LLM retry, backoff and rate limiting** — `src/llm/retry.ts`: 4 attempts, exponential backoff with equal jitter, 20s per-sleep and 60s total-elapsed caps, `Retry-After` honoured in both RFC 9110 forms. Retries 408/429/5xx/network, never 401/403. A `RequestLimiter` caps concurrency at 2 with 250ms spacing. Backoff sleeps are abort-aware and reject immediately rather than running the timer down. Retry is refused once a token has reached the UI (`LLMCommittedError`), so a partially streamed answer is never silently replaced (2026-08-10 14:30)
- **AI scan state machine** — `AppState.aiScan` carries `phase`/`scanned`/`failed`/`total`/`error`, distinguishing "not run", "scanning", "complete and clean", "complete but partial" and "failed". Previously a failed scan and a clean scan were indistinguishable, because both leave the findings list empty (2026-08-10 14:40)
- **Zod validation of LLM responses** — batch-rename and scan payloads are schema-validated and return a `{ok, value} | {ok, error}` result instead of being parsed inside a `catch {}` that swallowed failures. One `unwrapJSON` helper replaces two duplicated fence regexes that only matched `json`/`jso` info strings (2026-08-10 14:30)
- **`src/llm/models.ts`** — single registry for model IDs, provider defaults and per-task token budgets, replacing IDs duplicated across three files (2026-08-10 14:30)
- **`src/workers/dispatch.ts`** — the worker RPC dispatch extracted from `disasm.worker.ts` so the unknown-method path is testable at runtime; the compile-time `never` exhaustiveness guard moved with it. `disasm.worker.ts` cannot be imported under vitest because it touches `self`/`indexedDB` and loads Capstone WASM at module scope (2026-08-10 14:44)
- **`src/mcp/paths.ts`** — `parseAddr` and `resolveExportPath` extracted so their tests call them directly instead of driving a full `export_analysis` invocation. `src/mcp/__tests__/importGraph.test.ts` now *enforces* that `tools.ts` and `resources.ts` only type-import `./session`, keeping Capstone WASM out of the MCP test import graph (2026-08-10 14:26)
- **Keyboard shortcut drift guard** — `src/components/__tests__/keyboardShortcuts.test.ts` compares `docs/keyboard.md` against `SHORTCUT_GROUPS` in both directions. Deliberately spelling-tolerant (`Alt+←/→` matches `Alt+Left`+`Alt+Right`), with deliberate doc-only entries in an allowlist carrying a stated reason each (2026-08-10 14:26)
- **Biome lint + format** — `npm run lint` / `npm run format` / `npm run check`; `biome.json` configured with the a11y group and other large-backlog rules at warn so the baseline is visible without blocking CI (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **CI build, lint and audit gates** — CI now runs `lint`, `typecheck`, `test` and `build` on every PR (`build` previously ran only on release tags, so Vite/PWA breaks shipped silently); separate `audit` job at `--audit-level=high`; dependabot config for npm and GitHub Actions (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Malformed-PE regression suite** — `src/pe/__tests__/malformed.test.ts`, 8 tests covering truncated headers, unbounded section/data-directory counts, unmapped import thunks, a 0xFFFFFFFF export-name count, and a poisoned DER length; each carries an explicit timeout so a regression fails rather than hangs (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Test coverage reporting** — `npm run test:coverage`; v8 provider configured in `vitest.config.ts` (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Untrusted-input validation** — `validateAnnotations()` guards the two paths that reached the reducer unchecked: `localStorage` (editable by the user or any script on the origin) and the MCP WebSocket bridge (remote input). `parseViewTab()` narrows the `#tab=` URL parameter, which was cast straight to `ViewTab` — `#tab=lol` previously put the app in an unrenderable state. 13 tests in `src/utils/__tests__/exportSchema.test.ts` (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **`typecheck`, `test:coverage`, `mcp:setup` scripts** — replacing the hand-typed `npx tsc --noEmit` documented in three places (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Ghidra connection test** — "Test Connection" button in Settings → Ghidra tab verifies server reachability and displays server version and Ghidra version (2026-03-27 14:00)
- **MCP auto-discovery** — `.mcp.json` at project root enables Claude Code to automatically discover and use the MCP server without manual configuration (2026-03-27 12:00)
- **MCP setup CLI** — `npx tsx src/mcp/index.ts setup <client>` configures AI clients (claude-code, opencode, continue.dev) with `--dry-run` and `--list` flags; extensible registry pattern in `src/mcp/clients.ts` (2026-03-27 12:00)
- **AI Chat panel** — multi-turn streaming conversation with binary context; right sidebar panel toggled via Ctrl+Shift+A or toolbar button; PE metadata + active function pseudocode auto-attached as system context; `[RENAME:0xADDR:name]` markers in AI responses render as inline "Apply" rename buttons; per-file message persistence in localStorage (capped at 50 messages); local useReducer state to avoid app-wide re-renders during streaming (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **AI batch auto-rename** — "Rename" toolbar button or command palette "AI: Batch Rename Functions" decompiles all unnamed functions (no user rename, not thunk, size > 16 bytes) via worker, batches pseudocode to LLM in groups of 6, parses JSON rename suggestions; review modal with current/suggested name, confidence score (color-coded), reasoning, and accept/reject toggles; bulk actions (Accept All, Accept High Confidence, Reject All); accepted renames dispatched as RENAME_FUNCTION with undo support (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **AI analysis report** — "Report" toolbar button or command palette generates comprehensive Markdown report; builds ~12K token context (PE headers, notable imports, exports, anomalies, driver info, decompiled key functions, interesting strings); streams to full-page modal with live MarkdownRenderer; cached per file in localStorage with "Regenerate" button; downloadable as .md file (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **AI vulnerability scanner** — right-click function → "Scan for vulnerabilities" in both linear and graph mode context menus; "Scan" toolbar button or command palette "AI: Scan Suspicious Functions" bulk-scans functions calling dangerous APIs (VirtualAlloc, WriteProcessMemory, CreateRemoteThread, etc.); findings displayed in new "AI Security Findings" section in Anomalies tab with severity badges, clickable function names, collapsible descriptions and remediation text (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **Shared LLM infrastructure** — `streamChat()` multi-turn streaming function extracted from `streamEnhance()` with shared SSE pump; 4 new system prompts (chat, batch rename, report, vuln scan) in `src/llm/prompt.ts`; `ChatMessage`, `BatchRenameResult`, `AIScanFinding` types in `src/llm/types.ts` (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **Markdown renderer** — shared `MarkdownRenderer` component using `marked` library for chat messages and report display; full CSS styling for headers, code blocks, tables, lists, blockquotes; theme-aware variables (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **AI commands in command palette** — 4 new searchable commands: "AI: Open Chat", "AI: Batch Rename Functions", "AI: Generate Analysis Report", "AI: Scan Suspicious Functions" (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **AI toolbar buttons** — Chat, Rename, Report, and Scan buttons in the address bar toolbar for quick access to all AI features (2026-03-11 19:33)
- **Multiple AI provider profiles** — create, switch, and delete up to 10 named LLM profiles (each with its own provider, API key, model, base URL, and enhance source); auto-migrates legacy single-settings on first load; profile selector dropdown and name editor in Settings → AI tab; quick-switch popover badge in StatusBar for fast profile switching; `peek-a-bin:llm-profiles` localStorage key with zero breaking changes to existing consumers (2026-03-11 16:18)
- **CI workflow** — new `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `tsc --noEmit` and `npm test` on every push/PR to main (2026-03-11 16:18)
- **Gitignore hardening** — added patterns for security-sensitive files (`.pem`, `.key`, `.cert`, `.p12`, `.pfx`, `.jks`) and database files (`.sqlite`, `.db`); added `.env.example` with documented MCP WebSocket port config (2026-03-11 16:18)
- **MCP → Browser live sync** — WebSocket bridge pushes annotation changes (comments, renames, bookmarks) from MCP server to browser in real-time; auto-reconnect with 3s backoff; green "MCP" status dot in StatusBar when connected; configurable port via `PEEK_A_BIN_WS_PORT` env var (default 19283) (2026-03-08 22:51)
- **MCP annotation tools** — add_comment, rename_function, add_bookmark (toggle), list_comments tools for AI-driven annotation; renames overlay into decompile funcMap; decompile_function now returns lineMap for line→address mapping (2026-03-08 09:32)
- **MCP export/import tools** — export_analysis produces ExportSchemaV1 JSON (optionally writes to file); import_analysis reads ExportSchemaV1 JSON and merges annotations into session state (2026-03-08 09:32)
- **MCP server** — Model Context Protocol server for programmatic PE analysis; stdio transport; multi-file sessions; tools: load_pe, list_files, list_functions, decompile_function, disassemble_function, get_xrefs, detect_anomalies; resources: pe://{fileId}/{headers,sections,imports,exports,strings,functions,anomalies,driver}; shared module extraction from worker (functionDetect.ts); run via `npm run mcp` (2026-03-07 18:06)
- **Pseudocode comments** — annotate decompiled code with user comments; inline green `// comment` display on all lines mapping to the same address; right-click context menu (Add/Edit comment, Copy address); `;` hotkey to add comment on highlighted line; inline textarea editor (Enter=save, Shift+Enter=newline, Escape=cancel, empty=delete); shared editing state with disassembly view; comments auto-persist via existing annotation system (2026-03-07 14:17)

- **Theme system** — 4 built-in color themes (Dark, Light, IDA Pro, Terminal) with full CSS variable token system; all ~15 component files migrated from hardcoded Tailwind colors to theme-aware CSS classes; import/export custom themes as JSON; theme picker in Settings → Theme tab; mnemonic, operand, and decompiler syntax colors all themeable (2026-03-07 13:10)
- **CSS grid column alignment** — instruction and data rows use CSS grid with `ch`-based column widths that scale with font size; 32-bit (10ch) and 64-bit (18ch) address columns; toggleable bytes column with toolbar button (2026-03-07 13:10)
- **Context menu enhancements** — "Follow target" item for call/jmp instructions, "Show xrefs (N)" item with xref count; unified menu component for linear and graph modes (removes duplicated code); keyboard shortcut hints on right side; backdrop blur visual polish (2026-03-07 13:10)
- **Scroll-synced split view** — scrolling disassembly auto-scrolls decompile panel to matching code region; separate `scrollSyncAddr` state avoids feedback loops with `SET_ADDRESS`; throttled (100ms) scroll handler; sync toggle button in decompile panel header; persists to localStorage (2026-03-07 13:10)
- **Decompiler output improvements** — cast elimination (double-cast always collapses, cast-on-const folded, same-size cast stripped); De-Morgan's law for `!(a && b)` / `!(a || b)`; comparison canonicalization (const on right); recursive guard clause flattening for deeply nested if/return/else chains; for-loop detection scans all body blocks for increment; do-while with leading break converted to while; string constant propagation into emitted call args; type-based variable naming (HANDLE→hFile, NTSTATUS→status, HRESULT→hr, PVOID→pBuffer, BOOL→bResult) (2026-03-07 12:33)
- **Regex search UI hint** — `?` help icon next to search bar shows tooltip explaining `/regex/` and `/regex/i` syntax (2026-03-07 00:07)
- **Search results grouping** — search matches grouped by function with match counts; clickable function names to navigate; shown when matches span 2+ functions (2026-03-07 00:07)
- **Operand hover tooltips** — hovering clickable operand addresses shows contextual info: import names (`kernel32.dll!CreateFileW`), string previews, function names, section offsets; 200ms delay, auto-dismiss (2026-03-07 00:07)
- **Export analysis report** — "Report" button in sidebar generates comprehensive Markdown report with file summary, anomalies, driver info, functions, imports, exports, strings, and annotations; downloadable as `.md` file (2026-03-07 00:07)
- **Enum type inference** — switch statements with 3+ cases auto-infer enum types (`enum_N`); enum member names (`VAL_0x1`) emitted in case labels and constant references; `meetTypes` supports enum lattice merging; 6 new tests (2026-03-07 00:07)
- **Loop-aware SSA optimizations** — natural loop detection via back-edge analysis; Loop-Invariant Code Motion (LICM) hoists invariant assignments to preheader; induction variable recognition tags phi nodes with step metadata; 3 new tests (2026-03-07 00:07)
- **Exception handling IR** — `IRTry` statement type for `__try/__except` blocks; UNWIND_INFO parsing from `.pdata` extracts exception handler RVAs; exception regions wrapped during structuring; `__try/__except(filter)` emission; all walkers updated (ir, fold, emit, cleanup, promote, structs, typeInfer); 3 new tests (2026-03-07 00:07)
- **Global Value Numbering** — SSA-based GVN pass in `ssaopt.ts` eliminates redundant common subexpressions; commutative op normalization (`a+b == b+a`); conservative handling of calls, derefs, and unknowns (always unique); runs after const prop, before DCE in the optimize loop; 7 new tests (2026-03-06 23:28)
- **Decompiler expression quality** — 7 new fold rules (div/mod, comparison folding, ternary simplification, sign-extend `(x<<24)>>24` → `(int8_t)x`, strength reduction `x*2` → `x<<1`, double-cast removal, negation absorption `!(x==y)` → `x!=y`); `IRArrayAccess` node with `base[index]` emit instead of `*(type*)(base + idx * scale)`; increment/decrement `x++`/`x--` instead of `x = x + 1`; redundant cast suppression via TypeContext; array rewrite in struct synthesis pass (2026-03-06 22:30)
- **Control flow improvements** — `IRContinue` statement; continue detection in loops (goto-to-header → continue); guard clause flattening (`if(cond){return} else{rest}` → `if(cond){return} rest`); redundant goto/empty-block elimination; better loop classification (body-top break → while(!cond)); chained short-circuit `a && b && c` up to 8 blocks; post-structuring `cleanupStructured` pass (2026-03-06 22:30)
- **Expanded type system** — HANDLE, NTSTATUS, HRESULT as distinct DecompType kinds; type-aware idioms (`x == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE`, `NT_SUCCESS(x)`, `SUCCEEDED(x)`, `FAILED(x)`); ~130 Win32/NT API signatures across memory, string, file I/O, process/thread, sync, exception, crypto, COM, NT/Zw, network, device I/O categories; auto stack-frame synthesis when no stack frame analysis available (2026-03-06 22:30)
- **Fold rule unit tests** — 20 tests covering all new fold rules in `fold.test.ts` (2026-03-06 22:30)
- **Struct synthesis engine** — full struct type system for decompiler: `IRFieldAccess` IR node, `StructRegistry` cross-function state with fingerprint-based deduplication and subset merging, address decomposition (`base + idx*scale + offset`), automatic struct candidate detection from 2+ offset accesses on same base, field type inference (signedness from comparisons, pointer from derefs, float from XMM), alias-aware base grouping, call-site parameter linking for cross-function struct propagation, typedef emission (`typedef struct { ... } struct_N;`), `->fieldName` syntax in emitted pseudocode; replaces old cosmetic `collectStructBases` heuristic (2026-03-06 20:39)
- **Font-size responsive CFG blocks** — graph view block dimensions and text sizes now scale with the font size setting; `CFG_LAYOUT` converted to `getCfgLayout(fontSize)`, hardcoded pixel font sizes replaced with em-relative units (2026-03-06 18:48)
- **Tabbed settings modal** — settings reorganized into three tabs: AI (provider, key, model, enhance source, base URL), Ghidra (server toggle, URL, API key), Display (font size) (2026-03-06 18:17)
- **SSA-based decompiler** — full Static Single Assignment pass with phi nodes inserted between lift and structure phases; Cooper-Harvey-Kennedy dominator algorithm, pruned phi insertion with liveness, per-register versioning and renaming; SSA optimization passes (simplify phis, copy propagation, constant propagation, dead code elimination); SSA destruction with phi-to-copy lowering; cross-block value propagation now produces cleaner pseudocode with fewer redundant assignments (2026-03-06 17:59)
- **Type inference engine** — forward + backward type propagation over decompiled IR; lattice types (int with signedness, float, ptr, bool, void); signed/unsigned inference from Jcc comparisons, cast annotations, and deref patterns; API-aware typing from ~50 Win32/C function signatures (VirtualAlloc, CreateFile, GetProcAddress, memcpy, etc.) (2026-03-06 17:59)
- **Wider instruction lifting** — sign extensions (cdq, cqo, cdqe, cwde, cbw, cwd), div/idiv (quotient + remainder), single-operand mul (high-part eliminated by SSA DCE), xchg (SSA-correct swap without temp), rep movsb/stosb → memcpy/memset, basic FPU (fld/fst/fstp/fadd/fsub/fmul/fdiv on st0), SSE scalar ops (movss/addss/subss/mulss/divss/comiss on xmm registers) (2026-03-06 17:59)
- **Short-circuit && / || detection** — consecutive conditional blocks sharing a common branch target are now collapsed into compound boolean expressions instead of nested if-else (2026-03-06 17:59)
- **Multi-exit loop break detection** — conditional branches inside loop bodies targeting outside the loop are now emitted as `if (cond) break;` instead of gotos (2026-03-06 17:59)
- **De Morgan negation for && / ||** — condition negation now applies De Morgan's law: `!(a && b)` → `!a || !b` (2026-03-06 17:59)
- **for-loop emission** — `IRFor` statement type with init/condition/update/body emitted as `for (init; cond; update) { body }` (2026-03-06 17:59)
- **Decompile panel sub-tabs** — decompile panel now has three pill tabs: **Low Level** (existing built-in decompiler), **High Level** (Ghidra server, or a "not available" placeholder when none is configured), and **AI** (enhance/explain using best available source); active tab remembered across function navigation; per-tab per-function caching; decompile state extracted into `useDecompileTabs` hook (2026-08-10 19:37)
- **Ghidra decompilation server** — companion `ghidra-server/` with Dockerfile, FastAPI REST endpoints (`/ping`, `/binary`, `/decompile`), pyhidra integration; optional bearer token auth; binary uploaded once and cached by SHA-256
- **Decompilation server settings** — new "Decompilation Server" section in Settings modal with enable checkbox, server URL, and optional API key; stored in `peek-a-bin:decompile-server` localStorage key
- **Data section view** — non-executable sections (`.rdata`, `.data`, etc.) now render as structured data directives instead of garbage disassembly: `db` with string literals (green, ASCII/UTF-16LE), `dd`/`dq` with clickable pointer targets and resolved labels (IAT imports, functions, strings), `dup` for padding runs, and raw `db` hex with ASCII preview; jump arrows, graph toggle, and decompiler are hidden for data sections; search and range copy fully supported
- **Font size setting** — configurable monospace font size (10–16px) via slider in Settings modal; applied globally to disassembly, hex view, pseudocode, and CFG blocks via CSS custom property; persisted to localStorage
- **Keyboard shortcuts panel update** — added missing shortcuts: `Space` (graph toggle), `;` (comment), `N` (rename), `X`/`R`/`I`/`D` (panel toggles), `Alt+H` (recent addresses); new Graph category with block navigation, Tab, Enter, and zoom-to-fit shortcuts
- **Explain with AI** — new button in the Pseudocode panel header sends decompiled code to the LLM with an explanation prompt and streams the result back as `//` comments prepended to the pseudocode; mutually exclusive with Enhance (starting one cancels the other)
- **Recent files with instant re-open** — recently opened PE files are stored in IndexedDB (up to 5 entries, 50 MB cap) and shown on the file loader screen with file size, relative timestamp, and annotation summary; click to re-open instantly without re-browsing; remove button clears both IndexedDB and localStorage entries
- **Callers/callees sidebar panel** — collapsible "Call Graph" panel in the sidebar (between Bookmarks and Functions) shows which functions call the active function and what it calls; built from call graph data extracted during xref analysis; click entries to navigate with call stack push
- **PE anomaly detection** — automatic detection of 10 suspicious PE characteristics grouped by severity: critical (entry point in writable section, WX sections), warning (unusual entry point, packer section names, TLS callbacks, checksum mismatch, high code entropy), info (ASLR/DEP disabled, overlay data); shown as colored dismissible banners at the top of the Headers view
- **Data cross-references** — references to `.data`, `.rdata`, and `.bss` addresses (globals, vtables, etc.) are now tracked globally during xref analysis; Hex view shows purple xref count badges on rows containing referenced addresses with click-to-popup listing all referencing instructions
- **Escape navigates back in graph view** — pressing `Escape` in graph view pops the call stack breadcrumb or navigates back in history (same as the back arrow button), works globally without requiring focus on the graph container
- **Leading-whitespace string stripping** — `.rdata` strings starting with whitespace/control characters (`\t`, `\n`, `\r`) are now detected by skipping leading whitespace bytes and extracting from the first printable character, for both ASCII and UTF-16LE scanners
- **Inline graph view** — CFG is now an IDA Pro-style inline view togglable with `Space`, replacing the old modal overlay. Stays in graph mode across function changes; viewMode persisted to localStorage
- **Full interaction parity in graph blocks** — clicking, context menus, keyboard navigation, comments (`;`), register highlighting, operand navigation, and bookmarks all work identically inside graph blocks
- **Graph keyboard navigation** — `Arrow Up/Down` navigate within and across blocks, `Tab` cycles successor blocks, `Enter` follows branch targets, `0` zooms to fit the entire graph
- **Collapsible blocks** — click a block header to collapse/expand it
- **Graph minimap** — minimap transforms to show block rectangles with viewport indicator; click to pan the graph
- **Graph overview in sidebar** — IDA-style graph overview panel at the bottom of the sidebar showing all blocks, edges, and a viewport rectangle; click or drag to pan the main graph view; collapsible with state persisted to localStorage
- **Zoom toward cursor** — mouse wheel zoom centers on the cursor position instead of the origin
- **Copy comment** — context menu option to copy instruction/user comments to clipboard (both linear and graph mode)
- **Collapsible sections panel** — sidebar sections panel can now be collapsed/expanded with state persisted to localStorage
- **Trackpad swipe prevention** — graph view blocks browser back/forward navigation triggered by horizontal two-finger swipe gestures
- **Kernel driver detection** — automatically identifies `.sys` drivers by checking for NATIVE subsystem, WDM_DRIVER DllCharacteristics flag, and imports from kernel modules (ntoskrnl.exe, hal.dll, ndis.sys, fltmgr.sys, etc.) (`src/analysis/driver.ts`)
- **Driver banner and badge** — dismissible amber banner between address bar and tab content showing driver type, WDM status, and kernel API count; small amber badge in the status bar
- **Suspicious kernel API flagging** — database of 50+ kernel APIs across 7 risk categories (Process/Thread, Callback/Hook, Memory, Registry, Filesystem, Network, Object) with color-coded inline tags in the Imports view
- **IOCTL code decoder** — decodes Windows I/O control codes into device type, access, function number, and transfer method; annotates IOCTL constants inline in both disassembly and decompiler output
- **IRP dispatch table detection** — pattern-matches `mov [reg+offset], handler` instructions in DriverEntry to identify MajorFunction handler assignments (IRP_MJ_CREATE through IRP_MJ_PNP); auto-renames handler functions
- **Authenticode parsing** — parses WIN_CERTIFICATE / PKCS#7 SignedData structures with a minimal DER walker (~200 lines, no ASN.1 library) to extract signer subject CN, issuer CN, and validity dates (`src/pe/authenticode.ts`)
- **Digital Signature section in Headers view** — collapsible section showing signed/unsigned badge, subject, issuer, validity period, signature size, and certificate type

### Changed

- **`correctness/useExhaustiveDependencies` is an error, so a missing React dependency can fail CI** — it was at `warn`, and `npm run lint` is `biome lint src`, which exits 0 on warnings, so the whole stale-closure class was invisible to every gate; with no React renderer in this repo, that rule is the closest thing to a runtime check that exists for it. The ratchet became urgent the moment `useDisassemblySearch`'s return was memoised, which turned `handleKeyDown`'s 37 dependency entries from inert decoration into load-bearing state. Cost measured before deciding and after landing: **0 findings either side** across 230 files, with the warning total unchanged at 71 — which is the tell that `biome.json` is still being honoured at all — and both directions verified with a deliberately removed dependency, `biome lint src` exiting 1 at `error` where it exited 0 at `warn`. `build/lintConfig.test.ts` is a new guard on that severity and on the landmine next to it: `biome.json` must stay strict JSON, because a single `//` comment silently voids the whole config and Biome falls back to defaults (peek-a-bin-7ki) (2026-08-12 01:40)
- **Whole-file metrics moved off the main thread** — checksum validation, per-section entropy and the hex entropy strip ran inside `useMemo`s, and a `useMemo` cannot yield: on a synthetic 253 MiB PE that froze the UI for ~140 ms (Headers), ~770 ms (Sections) and ~6.5 s (entropy strip, which was computed on every Hex tab open whether or not the strip was ever toggled on). They now run in a new stateless `metrics.worker.ts`, kept separate from the disasm worker because that one services whole-image disassembly serially and a checksum queued behind it would wait minutes. Inputs under a measured threshold (256 KiB for the strip, 1 MiB for the file metrics) stay synchronous, so ordinary binaries see no loading state and spawn no worker. The strip's block count is now capped at 4096 — a 250 MiB section used to produce 1,024,000 blocks and draw each as its own `fillRect` onto ~1000 px of canvas. Main-thread cost on the 253 MiB image, interleaved medians: Headers+Sections 910 ms → 148 ms, entropy strip 6569 ms → 101 ms; the checksum on its own is a wash, because what is left is one memcpy of the argument (2026-08-11 07:35)
- **Six hand-rolled modal overlays collapsed into one `Modal` component** — `src/components/Modal.tsx` owns the `fixed inset-0 z-50` wrapper, the overlay dim, centring, dialog semantics and dismissal for `BatchRenameModal`, `GoToAddressModal`, `SettingsModal`, `CommandPalette`, `KeyboardShortcuts` and `AIReportPanel`. The copies had diverged on all five: three tints, two placements, and three different Escape mechanisms (a window listener, an input `onKeyDown`, and nothing). Dismissal is now a per-dialog option rather than an accident — Settings stays deliberately non-dismissible, and the batch-rename and report dialogs keep their existing "buttons only" behaviour. `modalScaffold.ts` holds the class composition and focus arithmetic as pure functions so they can be tested without a renderer (2026-08-10 22:22)

- **`DisassemblyView.tsx` split from 2326 lines to 1598** — the virtualized row renderers moved to `DisassemblyRows.tsx` (`SeparatorRow`, `DataRow`, `LabelRow`, `InsnRow`), the eleven context-menu actions to `useInsnContextMenu.ts` with its presentation in `InsnContextMenu.tsx`, and the toolbar, filter chips, export menu and search banners to `DisassemblyToolbar.tsx`. Strictly mechanical: no renamed variables, no dependency-array corrections, no logic changes — the component has **no test coverage at all** (there is no React renderer in this repo), so anything beyond a move would have been unverifiable. Extracted hooks sit at the same position in the call sequence the originals occupied, preserving hook order. The 17 new `useExhaustiveDependencies` warnings are a lint-scope artifact rather than a behaviour change: dependency arrays were copied verbatim, but `setCtxMenu` is now a parameter rather than a component-local setter, and Biome cannot prove a prop is stable (2026-08-10 22:00)
- **`[rip ± 0x..]` displacement parsing collapsed from nine hand-rolled copies to one module** — spread across `functionDetect.ts`, `operands.ts` and `decompile/lifter.ts`. They agreed on the arithmetic (`insn.address + insn.size`, since RIP holds the address of the *next* instruction) but not on the pattern: two of the nine matched `rip` case-insensitively and seven did not. `src/disasm/ripRelative.ts` is now the single home for both the regex and the address computation, standardised on the case-insensitive form — the safe direction, since no site can now miss an operand it previously caught (2026-08-10 21:47)
- **Struct merging is evidence-based where call-site provenance exists** — the signal was already computed and discarded: `propagateCallSites` recorded `linkParam(targetAddr, argIdx, structId)` and `getParamStruct` had no callers, so `paramLinks` was write-only. A base that occupies the same parameter slot as another *is* the same object, which is evidence rather than the shape coincidence the subset path relies on. A provenance-backed merge therefore ignores `MIN_SUBSET_MERGE_FIELDS`, restoring the two-field completion that guard had to give up — but it still cannot override a boundary conflict, since contradictory layouts mean one reading is wrong. Two maps are kept apart deliberately (what a *caller* passed in vs the *callee's* own reading) so a merge always has the callee's corroboration: a passthrough helper taking a `void*` never links its callers to each other. Identity is published in both directions, so caller-then-callee and callee-then-caller give the same result (2026-08-10 20:17)
- **`findCodeSection()` replaces the code-section predicate written out at seven call sites** — `s.name === ".text" || (s.characteristics & 0x20000000)` appeared verbatim in `App.tsx` (twice), `pe/parser.ts`, `mcp/session.ts` and three AI hooks, while `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE` was *defined twice* (`pe/constants.ts`, `analysis/anomalies.ts`) and referenced by none of them. Now one helper in `src/pe/sections.ts` and one constant. The helper is documented as **first-match-wins over the section table**, not ".text if present, otherwise the first executable section" — a packed binary whose first section is an unnamed executable stub returns the stub even when a later `.text` exists. Every replaced call site already behaved that way, so it is preserved rather than quietly "fixed" (2026-08-10 20:12)
- **One `decompileForLLM()` replaces the ~100-line decompile-a-function routine duplicated across `useBatchRename`, `useVulnScanner` and `useAIReport`** — the worker calls and bounds arithmetic were identical; the only genuine divergence was the per-caller line cap, which stays a parameter because the three features have different token budgets (2026-08-10 20:12)
- **`DisplayRow` has one declaration again** — `JumpArrows` and `DisassemblyMinimap` each kept a private narrowed copy that had to be hand-synced, a hazard CLAUDE.md warned about. A narrowed structural clone still accepts the canonical rows at the call site, so the copies drifted silently instead of failing the build (2026-08-10 20:12)
- **`parseBranchTarget` deduplicated, preserving a real behavioural difference** — the `JumpArrows` copy resolved jumps only; the `shared.tsx` one also resolves `call` immediates. Collapsing them naively would have made the jump-arrow overlay sprout arrows for recursive and intra-function calls, so the call site now carries an explicit jumps-only guard. The old copy's `mnemonic === "jmp" || (mnemonic.startsWith("j") && mnemonic !== "jmp")` reduces to plain `startsWith("j")`, which made the "Also handle jmp" block below it unreachable; a test pins the input that used to reach it (2026-08-10 20:12)
- **RVA→file-offset resolution no longer rescans the section table per lookup** — `rvaToFileOffset` is a linear scan called once per import thunk and twice per `.pdata` RUNTIME_FUNCTION, so a large driver ran it hundreds of thousands of times. `buildSectionIndex()` is built once per parse and `rvaToFileOffsetIndexed()` binary-searches it. The subtlety: binary search answers "which section holds this RVA", while the scan answers "the *first* section in table order that holds it", and those agree only when sections are disjoint. An out-of-order table is therefore sorted, but an **overlapping** one falls back to the scan rather than silently resolving to a different section's bytes — as does a table with a NaN address or size. Short tables also stay on the scan. Pinned by a 10k-random-table differential against the reference scan, covering both disjoint and overlapping families (2026-08-10 19:55)
- **`validateChecksum` no longer walks the file two bytes at a time through a `DataView`** — it ran ~25 million iterations on the main thread for a 50 MB image, folding carries on every word. It now accumulates over a `Uint16Array` in four lanes and folds once at the end; the accumulator cannot lose precision until roughly a 274 GB file. Endianness is checked rather than assumed, since a `Uint16Array` reads native-endian and a wrong checksum is silent. The original's quirk of summing its own checksum field when `e_lfanew` is odd is preserved deliberately — the value this function writes back has to verify against itself (2026-08-10 19:55)
- **`src/decompile/` split up to end the name collision with `src/disasm/decompile/`** — the 175-LOC directory was UI tab state plus a remote HTTP client, not a decompiler, which made it easy to confuse with the 6000-LOC in-browser decompiler next door. `ghidraClient.ts` → `src/ghidra/client.ts` (named for the service it talks to, matching the top-level `ghidra-server/` it is the client for), `types.ts` → `src/hooks/decompileTabsState.ts` (the pure reducer behind `useDecompileTabs`, kept as its own leaf module so it stays importable without pulling in `disasmClient` → Capstone WASM). Pure rename; no behaviour change (2026-08-10 19:37)

- **Accessibility pass** — 301 Biome a11y findings cleared to zero. The codebase previously contained zero `aria-*` and zero `role` attributes; 18 component files now carry them. Five of the seven a11y rules are ratcheted from `warn` to `error` in `biome.json` so they cannot regress. Where a fix was not correct — a disassembly row containing its own buttons cannot itself become a button, and `role="button"` would be invalid ARIA for the same reason — the instance carries a `biome-ignore` with the reasoning rather than being silently suppressed; proper keyboard selection there needs a roving-tabindex grid model, tracked separately (2026-08-10 14:59)
- **PWA precache reduced from 4850 KiB to 2695 KiB** — removing the duplicate WASM (see Fixed), excluding the 435 KB `icon-512.png` (read by the OS at install and splash time, which happens online), and adding `manifest.json`, which `globPatterns` had omitted (2026-08-10 14:19)
- **Per-task LLM token budgets** — `max_tokens` was hardcoded twice for Anthropic and omitted entirely for OpenAI, so a long report could be truncated on one provider and unbounded on the other. Now keyed by task for both (2026-08-10 14:30)
- **Model IDs updated and centralised** — `claude-sonnet-4-20250514` was a full generation stale. IDs now live only in `src/llm/models.ts`, and the Settings model field gained a `datalist` of current IDs while staying free-text, so a model newer than the build can still be entered. `LEGACY_DEFAULT_MODELS` + `isDefaultModel()` let the provider switcher recognise a profile still holding a superseded default and refresh it, rather than mistaking a stale default for a deliberate user choice and carrying a Claude ID over to the OpenAI provider (2026-08-10 18:15)
- **CHANGELOG restructured into released sections** — ~150 bullets sat under a single `[Unreleased]` with `### Changed` repeated 5 times, `### Fixed` 3 times, `### Removed` twice and a non-standard `### Previously Added`, despite two shipped tags. Now cut at the tag boundaries into `[0.0.2-alpha]` (content verified against `git show v0.0.2-alpha:CHANGELOG.md`) and `[0.0.1-alpha]` (which predates the file), with each subsection appearing once per version. All 166 bullets preserved verbatim; entries misfiled under `Fixed`/`Removed` were moved to the section matching their content (2026-08-10 13:52)
- **Documentation accuracy pass** — corrected counts and claims that had drifted from the code across `CLAUDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `docs/`: `AppAction` 48→50 types, `AppState` 33+→38 fields, `IRExpr` 13→12 kinds, `DecompType` 11→12 kinds (the `enum` kind was undocumented), theme color tokens 51→36, and the CI description (which omitted lint, build and audit). The `wrapExceptionRegions` pipeline stage was missing from `CLAUDE.md`'s pipeline order (2026-08-10 13:52)
- **IR walker checklists rewritten** — `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/decompiler.md` listed 6 files for `IRExpr` and 4 for `IRStmt`; there are ~30 switch sites. Both now split them into the seven now guarded by `never` exhaustiveness checks, those with a silent `default:`, those with neither, and the three if-chains that are not switches at all (2026-08-10 13:52)
- **Docs update mapping consolidated** — the "update docs/ when you change X" table was duplicated in `CLAUDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `docs/README.md`; `docs/README.md` is now canonical and the other two point at it (2026-08-10 13:52)
- **`AGENTS.md` trimmed** — 128 lines of which ~75 were Beads boilerplate repeated three times, with one block byte-identical to `CLAUDE.md`. Reduced to its unique non-interactive-shell guidance plus a pointer at `CLAUDE.md`; both tool-managed Beads blocks left byte-for-byte intact (2026-08-10 13:52)
- **Package identity** — renamed from the scaffolding name `web-disassembly` to `peek-a-bin`; added `description`, `license`, `repository`, `homepage` and `engines: node >=20` (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **`noUnusedLocals` / `noUnusedParameters` enabled** in `tsconfig.json` (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Dependency vulnerabilities: 23 → 1** — including the critical Vitest advisory and the direct `ws` uninitialized-memory-disclosure. The remaining moderate (`@hono/node-server` path traversal) is pinned transitively by the latest MCP SDK with no upstream fix and is unreachable here — the server uses stdio + ws, not Hono serve-static (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Documentation restructuring** — split monolithic README into `docs/` folder with 8 topic-specific guides (keyboard, theming, ghidra-server, mcp-server, ai-features, architecture, decompiler, deployment); added CONTRIBUTING.md, SECURITY.md, and docs/README.md hub; README slimmed to project overview with doc links (2026-03-27 16:00)
- **Ghidra 12.0.4** — Docker image updated from Ghidra 11.3.2 to 12.0.4; ping endpoint now returns `ghidraVersion` (2026-03-27 14:00)
- **Browser deps moved to devDependencies** — react, react-dom, marked, @dagrejs/dagre, @tanstack/react-virtual moved to devDependencies; only MCP runtime deps (SDK, capstone-wasm, ws, tsx, zod) remain in dependencies (2026-03-27 12:00)
- **Tag-based deployments** — deploy workflow now triggers on `v*.*.*` tags instead of every push to main; adds type-check and test steps before build (2026-03-11 16:18)
- **Performance: worker decompile maps** — iatMap, stringMap, and jumpTables are now stored in the worker and reused across decompile calls instead of being re-serialized every invocation (2026-03-07 16:33)
- **Performance: deduplicate buildIATLookup** — IAT lookup map computed once in App.tsx and stored in AppState; DisassemblyView and useDisassemblyRows read from state instead of recomputing (2026-03-07 16:33)
- **Performance: memoize GraphOverviewContext** — context value stabilized with useMemo to prevent unnecessary consumer re-renders (2026-03-07 16:33)
- **Performance: memoize App.tsx callbacks/style** — 4 modal onClose handlers wrapped in useCallback, font-size style object wrapped in useMemo (2026-03-07 16:33)
- **Performance: memoize ImportsView filtering** — filtered imports, totalFunctions, and filteredFuncCount wrapped in useMemo (2026-03-07 16:33)
- **Performance: hoist SUSPICIOUS_MNEMONICS** — moved from useMemo inside component to module-level constant (2026-03-07 16:33)
- **Shortcut legend** — `;` description now clarifies it works in both disassembly and pseudocode views (2026-03-07 14:58)
- **Minimap hidden in graph mode** — minimap and Map button only shown in linear view mode (2026-03-07 14:17)
- **Decompiler pipeline** — new pipeline: buildCFG → liftBlock → buildSSA → ssaOptimize → destroySSA → foldBlock → structureCFG → cleanupStructured → inferTypes → promoteVars → synthesizeStructs → emitFunction; cleanup pass inserted after structuring; TypeContext threaded to emitter for cast suppression and type idioms (2026-03-06 22:30)
- **Unconstrained decompile panel resize** — removed 800px max width cap and lowered minimum to 100px; panel can now grow as wide as viewport allows (2026-03-06 18:17)
- **Graph re-center on decompile open** — opening the decompile panel in graph mode now re-centers the CFG on the current block after layout adjusts (2026-03-06 18:17)
- **Back-navigation preserves full view state** — pressing `Escape` to go back now restores viewMode (linear/graph), graph pan position, and zoom level, not just the address; works across function jumps (call stack), section changes, and graph↔linear transitions
- **Compact graph spacing** — reduced vertical spacing (80→40), horizontal spacing (50→30), edge separation (20→10), and instruction line height (16→14px) for denser, more compact graph blocks
- CFG layout constants updated for better readability: wider blocks (320px), taller rows (16px), more spacing between blocks
- Extracted shared disassembly utilities (`ColoredOperand`, `mnemonicClass`, `tokenizeOperand`, `parseBranchTarget`, `REG_NAMES`) into `src/components/shared.tsx` for reuse between linear and graph views
- Toolbar "CFG" button replaced with a "Graph" toggle button that reflects active state

### Fixed

- **An emitted struct now lays its fields out at the offsets its field names record** — the field *names* carry the recovered offsets (`field_0x18`), but the typedef block emitted the fields back to back and let C place them, so `struct_0 { uint32_t field_0x0; uint16_t field_0x18; }` put `field_0x18` at 4 and every `p->field_0x18` in the body read bytes the access never touched. Compiled cleanly, said something the machine does not do: **131 of the 149 struct definitions** emitted over t32/t64/w64 were wrong this way. `emit.ts` now computes the layout — explicit `_pad_0xNN` members plus `#pragma pack(push, 1)` around the definitions, neither of which works without the other, since padding alone cannot express an unaligned offset. Measured by compiling and running an `offsetof` probe over every emitted struct: **18/149 definitions and 318/852 fields before, 145/145 and 819/819 after**. Four kinds of field cannot be placed by any padding — one overlapping a field already placed, a negative offset, one past 0x8000 (C89's guaranteed object size), and one whose recovered width has no spelling — and they are reported in the struct body instead of being declared somewhere convenient, with their accesses spelled as the bytes they touch (`*(uint16_t*)((uint8_t*)rbx + 2)`); a struct where nothing can be placed is left incomplete, which is the forward declaration the emitter already uses for a shape it does not have. A declared type is kept unless it would move the *next* recorded offset, where the field is declared at the width the access had and the inferred type moves to a comment. gcc-clean functions 995→996/1001, condition polarity byte-identical, 0 throws, 0 dangling gotos (peek-a-bin-ey0) (2026-08-11 17:05)
- **An index whose stride is not the width read there no longer makes a field an array** — `isArray` was a sticky OR set by any indexed access, while the field's size and type came from the widest access of any kind, so a field could be declared with 8-byte elements over a recovered stride of 4 — an object neither reading found. The array claim now requires the stride and the recovered width to agree, and where they do not the width wins, being a direct measurement of one access. The emitter's array declaration also spells its element from the stride rather than from the field size, so the two can no longer disagree even if a cross-function merge widens the field (peek-a-bin-hyv) (2026-08-11 17:05)
- **A field the code masks, ors or shifts is no longer inferred to be a pointer** — `struct_4::field_0x18` in t64 was declared `struct_4 *` and then `|= 0x20`, which does not compile and is not what the field is: all six of its uses in that binary are flag arithmetic on a CRT `FILE::_flag`. The pointer came from *another* function that used the loaded value as a base, and reached this one because StructFields are shared live registry state. Bitwise use of a field's value is now recorded on the field itself and works in both directions — a mask seen first refuses the promotion, a mask seen later takes it back — because which function a session decompiles first is not evidence about the field. `+` and `-` are excluded: they are ordinary pointer arithmetic. Clears the last 2 `invalid operands to binary |` errors over the three binaries (peek-a-bin-h89) (2026-08-11 17:05)
- **ARM64 branches are no longer reported as data references** — `buildTypedXrefMap` classified xrefs with an x86-only test: an A64 branch writes its destination as `#0x…`, which matches neither the `^0x…$` direct form nor `resolveRipTarget`, but does satisfy the loose hex scan's `mn !== "call" && mn !== "jmp" && !mn.startsWith("j")` guard. So every branch target became a `type: "data"` xref — **all 7263 xrefs in t64-arm.exe and all 6409 in w64-arm.exe**, stated confidently and wrongly to the xref panel and to the MCP `get_xrefs` tool. It now reads `classifyArm64Branch` from `src/disasm/arm64Operands.ts` (the existing, tested A64 grammar — not a second one) and `continue`s, so no A64 branch reaches that scan: t64-arm.exe becomes 1725 call / 1200 jmp / 3331 branch / 1007 data, w64-arm.exe 1501 / 1018 / 2918 / 972. The set of (target, from) pairs is unchanged — only the type moved — and `adrp`/`adr`/literal-pool loads are still data. No `arch` parameter is threaded: the mnemonic sets are disjoint, and the one shared spelling (`ret`) is left on the x86 path, where both architectures already yield nothing. t32/t64/w64 xref lists are byte-identical, per-xref (peek-a-bin-a0d, peek-a-bin-3c1) (2026-08-11 16:26)

- **The 2^32-bit MD5 length case no longer fails the suite on a busy machine** — `metadata.test.ts`'s boundary case hashes 2^29 bytes, which is the smallest message that reaches the length carry, and costs 11.5 s quiet. Its timeout was 30 s, i.e. vitest's default written out longhand, so it raised nothing; under load the case took ~57 s and took the whole run down with it. The bound is now 180 s, sized to catch a hang rather than to police throughput. The input, the differential against `node:crypto` and the boundary itself are unchanged (peek-a-bin-8hi) (2026-08-11 16:26)

- **The disassembler no longer stops decoding without saying so** — `cs.disasm` was being handed 0x10000-byte windows, which is within 1 KiB of a cliff. capstone-wasm's linear memory is a fixed 16 MiB that *cannot grow* (its `emscripten_resize_heap` aborts immediately), the input is copied onto a ~65.6 KiB WASM stack by `stackAlloc`, and `cs_disasm` allocates one contiguous `cs_insn[]` — ~240 B each — for the whole window. Measured against the shipped `capstone.wasm`: 65536-byte windows work and 66560-byte ones throw `table index is out of bounds`; 22528 instructions in one call work and 23552 abort with `Aborted(OOM)`. Either kills the module permanently, and the failure is silent, because `disasm` *throws* when it decodes nothing and every scan loop read a throw as "this byte is not code, skip one". Measured: `hybridDisassemble` over a real `.text` grown to 0.5/1/2/4 MiB returned **12.5%/7.2%/4.6%/3.2%** of its instructions, threw nothing, and left the decoder unable to decode a single `nop` afterwards. `src/disasm/capstoneWindow.ts` now owns every decode: 0x2000-byte windows capped at 2048 instructions per call — 12% of the stack and 9% of the heap ceiling, down from 93% and 75% — plus a liveness probe after a run of failed decodes, so an exhausted decoder reaches the caller as a `CapstoneUnavailableError` (hence `analysisPhase: "failed"`) rather than as a short list that looks complete. The same size sweep now returns 100% at every size through 32 MiB. Function and instruction counts on t32/t64/w64/w32/t64-arm/w64-arm are byte-identical, and a `.pdata` range — attacker-controlled, previously decoded in one unbounded call — is windowed like everything else (peek-a-bin-6a5) (2026-08-11 16:35)

- **A loop no longer claims to repeat on a test the machine does not use to repeat** — three defects in `structure.ts`'s loop-condition choice, all of the peek-a-bin-h9v class (valid C stating something the machine does not do), all silent to every stage-level test. (1) A conditional inside a loop body whose *both* arms are targets the walk had already claimed — the loop header and the way out — produced two empty arms, and `pushConditional` deleted the guard entirely: the loop's own back-edge test vanished, leaving `while (eax != 0)` for a `WriteFile` retry the machine repeats while `eax != 0 && r15d > esi` (peek-a-bin-jlo), and `for (eax = 0; ecx != tbl[eax]; eax++)` for a table search bounded by `eax < 0x16`. An arm the walk refuses to follow is now the `goto` that says where control really goes. (2) A header conditional whose two arms are *both inside* the body decides something within an iteration, not whether there is another one; taken as the loop's test it fabricated a guard, as often inverted as merely wrong — `while (edi >= ecx)` for a loop the machine runs while `edx < esi`. Such loops are bottom-tested and are now structured as such, with the header's branch kept as the `if` it is. (3) A header that does work as well as testing cannot be a `while`: `while (c) { H; B }` tests before running `H`, and the machine runs `H` first and tests what it just produced — so the emitted loop tested the *previous* `WriteFile`'s result before making the first call, and guarded a `while ((c = *p) != 0)` scan on a stale register. `H` empty stays `while (c)`, `B` empty becomes `do { H } while (c)`, and neither becomes `while (1) { H; if (!c) break; B }` — deliberately the ugly spelling, because a loop condition that cannot be stated without also stating when `H` runs is one no reader can check against the disassembly. Across t32/t64/w64: 34 wrong guards → **0**, with the loop count per binary unchanged (160/189/181 — only the spelling moved), statement drops 14/30/30 → 4/8/8, dangling gotos still 0, gcc-clean still 995/1001 (peek-a-bin-jlo, peek-a-bin-bhh) (2026-08-11 16:12)
- **x86-64 jump tables are detected** — the detector required the dispatching instruction to name its table (`jmp dword ptr [eax*4 + 0x40941c]`) and read absolute pointers from it, which is the 32-bit shape and only that. x64 code is position-independent, so the address arrives through a `lea`, the entry through a scaled load, and the two are added: `lea base, [rip + N] / mov(sxd) off, dword ptr [base + idx*4 (+ disp)] / add off, base / jmp off`. Entries are **signed 32-bit displacements from the lea's target**, not addresses — read as absolute pointers they resolve into the first page of the image and are silently discarded. Taking the table as `lea` target + load displacement and the case target as `lea` target + entry covers both spellings at once: MSVC points the `lea` at `__ImageBase` and carries the table's RVA in the load, GCC/clang point it at the table and leave the displacement zero. The walk backwards from the jump gives up whenever a register it is chasing is written by anything else (a `call` included), the bounds check that states the case count has to name the index register *and* precede the load (the load's destination is routinely the index register itself), the count is capped at 512, and every entry has to resolve into the code window — a table has no length field, so an unbounded read invents case targets out of whatever follows it. The dense two-table (`movzx`) form is recognised and deliberately refused: entry *i* is not case *i* there, and wrong case labels are worse than no switch (peek-a-bin-ydh) (2026-08-11 15:55)
- **MCP decompiled ARM64 instead of declining** — `decompile_function` called `decompileFunction`/`analyzeStackFrame`/`inferSignature` without consulting `AnalyzedFile.arch`, so an MCP client asking for an ARM64 function got a short, confident, wrong C body (mostly `/* unlifted: … */`, every branch dropped, a fabricated `__unrecovered_N` per register, a closing `return rax`) with nothing marking it as invented. The browser worker had refused since ARM64 landed; the tool now returns the same `unsupportedOnArch` message, before the address is even resolved. `load_pe`, `list_files` and `pe://{id}/headers` also report `arch` next to `is64` — an ARM64 image is PE32+, so `is64` alone reads as "x64" (peek-a-bin-9b1) (2026-08-11 15:45)

- **A logical shift right is emitted as C, at the width the machine shifted** — `shr` lifts to the IR op `>>>`, JavaScript's unsigned shift, and it reached the output verbatim: 66 syntax errors over three real binaries, the largest remaining category, in functions with nothing else wrong. C spells a logical shift as `>>` on an unsigned operand, so the translation needs a width — and both obvious repairs are dishonest rather than merely imprecise. A bare `>>` is an *arithmetic* shift and shifts the sign bit in where the machine shifts zeros; a fixed `(uintptr_t)` cast picks a width instead of finding one, and is wrong whenever a 32-bit `shr`'s operand sits sign-extended in a wider emitted variable. `IRBinary` carries no width, but every operand node does — `IRReg.size` (the lifter keeps sub-register names, so `eax` is 4 and `r8` is 8), `IRVar.size`, and the access `size` on derefs, field and array accesses — so `operandWidth()` reads it, through casts, unary, ternary and nested arithmetic, and the shift is emitted as `(uintN_t)x >> n` at exactly that width. No IR change was needed. Where no width can be established, or a constant count contradicts the one found (which would also be undefined behaviour in C), the value is reported through the existing `__unrecovered_N` mechanism instead of guessed — that fallback fired zero times over 1001 functions (2026-08-11 15:58)
- **A synthesised enum is declared, and its member names stay where they mean something** — type inference invents an enum for a switch with three or more cases, and nothing ever emitted the `enum` block: `case VAL_0x0:` reached the output as an undeclared identifier, which is not an integer constant expression, so the switch did not compile. Worse, `emitExpr` scanned *every* enum in the function for one holding each constant's value, so unrelated literals came out as enum members — t32 `sub_40B780` emitted `edi & VAL_0x3` and `x != VAL_0x0` for a mask and a zero test that have nothing to do with its switches, each reading as an undeclared name rather than as its value. The block is now emitted for exactly the enums the body named a member of, with explicit values, and a member name is used only where the enum-typed value is in hand: a `case` label, and `==`/`!=` against a value of that type (2026-08-11 15:58)
- **A shortened string literal states the prefix it recovered** — a recovered string over 40 characters was truncated with `...` appended *inside* the quotes, so the emitted literal's value ended in three dots — a value the binary does not contain, in a form a reader cannot distinguish from a string that really does end that way. The marker now goes outside the literal, where C treats it as whitespace: `"C:\Users\Public\Documents\Very\Long\P" /* + 12 more characters */` (2026-08-11 15:58)
- **A scalar access to a field the layout calls an array means that access** — struct synthesis marks an offset as an array as soon as one access reaches it through an index, and direct accesses to the same offset remain. `p->array_0x0` is then not the value at that offset: in C an array name in an expression is its own address, so a read silently yielded a pointer where the machine loaded data, and a write did not compile at all ("assignment to expression with array type"). The access is now spelled as what it is under the declaration the same file emits — `p->array_0x0[0]` when it is exactly one element wide, and the bytes it really touches when it is not — without inventing an element index. Whether the offset is an array at all is unchanged and still an open contradiction in struct synthesis (peek-a-bin-hyv) (2026-08-11 15:58)
- **The decompiler calls a function once when the machine code calls it once** — `liftBlock` resolved every register operand through `RegState`, i.e. to the symbolic expression that register was last given, while still emitting the assignment that produced it. A `test eax, eax` after a call therefore re-expanded the whole call: one CALL instruction became three calls in the emitted C. Measured on t64/t32/w64: lines calling the same function twice 121/188/120 → 0/0/0, and `eflags` lines 138/326/135 → 5/4/5 (the survivors are flag definitions whose source contains a call, which must not be deleted). The same substitution meant a register *leaf* stood for the block-entry value while `ssa.ts` renames it to the most recent definition, so `sub rax,rcx / sar rax,1 / dec rax` emitted `((rax - rcx - rcx >> 1) - rcx >> 1) - 1` — RCX subtracted three times. `parseOperand` no longer takes a `RegState` at all; propagation is SSA's, `ssaopt`'s and `foldBlock`'s job, which they do with the version information that makes it sound (2026-08-11 15:35)
- **`ret` returns the accumulator, not the value a register used to hold** — the lifter emitted whatever expression `RegState` had recorded for RAX, which is bound to the registers it names as they were when it was recorded and is not invalidated when one of them is written again. `mov rax, rbx / mov rbx, [rsp+0x30] / ret` returned the *restored* RBX — emitted as `var_30`, the saved-register slot — and the real return value, now unread, was deleted as dead along with whatever fed it (2026-08-11 15:35)
- **Loop bodies keep the instruction that updates the loop condition** — the branch condition is not an IR value: `structure.ts` takes it from `RegState.getCondition`, which recorded the `cmp`/`test` operands from the instruction. So the registers a condition names had no IR consumer, and dead-code elimination correctly — given what it could see — deleted the `eflags` definition and then the load feeding it. `wcslen` emitted `while (dx != 0) { rax += 2; }`, an infinite loop. `ssaOptimize` now holds the flag definition of any Jcc-terminated block live through the fixpoint and drops it afterwards, so nothing leaks into the text. Recovers ~570 statements per binary on t64 (2026-08-11 15:35)
- **A read of the stack pointer is not moved to another program point** — `push`, `pop` and the return address a `call` pushes are not lifted, so RSP changes with nothing in the IR saying it did. Inlining `mov ebp, esp` across an unmodelled `push` printed `*(int32_t*)(esp + 8)` where the instruction said `[ebp + 8]` — a base register the instruction never named, one push off the value it did name. Both `ssaopt.ts`'s copy propagation and `foldBlock`'s single-use inlining now refuse it (2026-08-11 15:35)
- **A definition read twice is not deleted after the first read** — `foldBlock`'s single-use scan stopped at the first `call_stmt`, which ended the *use count* as well as the hazard scan. A definition read once before a call and once after looked single-use: it was substituted into the first read and dropped, leaving the second read naming a register nothing assigns. The call is now a boundary for whether the value may *move*, not for how many times it is read (2026-08-11 15:35)
- **`xchg`, `div` and `mul` write their two destinations in an order that reads the right inputs** — all three lift to two statements from one instruction, and SSA renames a statement's uses before it versions its definition. `xchg rax, rbx` as `rax = rbx; rbx = rax` left both registers holding RBX; `div` emitting EAX before EDX made the remainder read the quotient; `mul` emitting the low half first made the high half square the product. `xchg` now goes through a temporary that copy propagation removes, and `div`/`mul` write the half the other statement does not read first (2026-08-11 15:35)
- **ARM64 images disassemble instead of showing 419 empty functions** — Capstone was opened only in x86 modes and every call site picked between them with `is64`, which is the PE32+ optional-header magic and not the machine type; an ARM64 PE is PE32+, so it parsed, listed its `.pdata` function boundaries, and decoded to *zero instructions*. `coffHeader.machine` now selects the decoder (`src/disasm/arch.ts`), and ARM64 gets its own sweep (`src/disasm/arm64.ts`) rather than another branch inside the x86 detector: A64 is fixed-width, so a linear sweep at 4-byte alignment is not a heuristic, it is the decoding, and recursive descent — which exists to resolve x86's ambiguous instruction boundaries — has nothing to do. Measured on `t64-arm.exe`: 0 → 27428 instructions, all 419 `.pdata` starts decode, 0 misaligned; `w64-arm.exe` 0 → 24393 with 381/381. Function starts come from evidence only — `.pdata` extents (exact sizes), exports, the entry point, unwind handlers, and direct `bl` targets outside every extent, which are the leaf functions ARM64 unwind data may omit (120 of them on `t64-arm.exe`, and not one `bl` lands *inside* an extent, which is a good check on the sweep's alignment). No prologue byte scan. The x86 path is untouched: instruction streams and function lists for `t64`/`t32`/`w64` are byte-identical (2026-08-11 14:55)
- **What cannot be done on ARM64 now says so instead of guessing** — the decompiler, xref building and IRP-dispatch detection are x86 instruction grammars, and fed A64 they do not fail, they emit confident nonsense: the IR lifter produced a body of `/* unlifted: stp x19, x20, ... */` lines with every branch silently dropped, a fabricated local per ARM64 register and a closing `return rax`.
