Decompiling an ARM64 function now rejects with "Decompilation is not supported for ARM64 images", which the panel displays; `buildAllXrefs` and `detectIRPDispatches` return nothing rather than inventing references. Instructions inside a `.pdata` extent are marked as vouched-for and the rest as gap fill, so the view dims what the sweep guessed. The MCP tool layer has since been given the same guard — see the entry above (peek-a-bin-9b1) (2026-08-11 15:45)
- **Jump-table case bodies are seeded into disassembly, so `case 0` keeps its body** — `hybridDisassemble`'s recursive descent stops dead at an indirect `jmp`, and its only seeds were function starts, so case bodies fell to phase 2's linear gap fill. Where MSVC puts the table immediately before its first case body — the ordinary x86 layout — that sweep started *on the table*, decoded its entries as instructions and walked off the end misaligned, swallowing the head of case 0. On `t32.exe` two of sixteen distinct targets (`0x40b900`, `0x40ba9c`) were not instruction starts, and both switches emitted `case 0: break;` followed by a garbage `esi->field_0x5F |= bl` at a misaligned label. The targets are a by-product of `detectFunctions` that every caller already holds, and are now added to the seed list; 16/16 targets are instruction starts and both case bodies emit. `t64`/`w64` detect no jump tables and are byte-identical (2026-08-11 14:55)
- **Emitted C now says what it recovered in a form C accepts: 648 → 962 of 1001 real functions compile clean** — measured with gcc `-std=gnu89 -fsyntax-only` over every function of three MSVC binaries, with a prelude declaring only what the decompiler deliberately never declares (registers, imported APIs, `__try`). Four spelling defects, all in `emit.ts`, none of them a change to what was recovered: a field access on a register emitted `rcx->field_0x18` with nothing declaring `rcx` (1656 errors); string literals escaped `"` and nothing else, so `<launcher_dir>\` escaped its own closing quote (8 unterminated literals, 2 stray backslashes); an unlifted instruction emitted MSVC's `__asm { ... };` and an unrecovered condition emitted `if (!/* je */)` (together the bulk of 1886 syntax errors); and an indirect call emitted `(*esi)(...)`, dereferencing an integer (314). Condition polarity is byte-identical per guard, dangling `goto`s stay at zero, and no function throws (2026-08-11 14:45)
- **A field access names the struct it reads, by casting at the point of use** — `((struct_0 *)rcx)->field_0x18`, not a `struct_0 *rcx;` declaration at the top of the function. The declaration is the obvious repair and it is wrong: 59 of the 151 functions that use a register as a struct base also write byte offsets off that same register, and a struct-pointer declaration makes C scale those by `sizeof(struct_0)` — code that still compiles and now describes a different address. The cast claims only what the recovery supports, this access reads this address as a `struct_N`, and is suppressed where a declaration in scope already says so (2026-08-11 14:45)
- **A value the decompiler failed to recover is named rather than printed as a comment** — `if (!/* je */)` was a statement that states nothing while looking like it states something (423 of them across three binaries). An `IRUnknown` now emits `__unrecovered_1 /* je */`, declared uninitialised as `intptr_t __unrecovered_1; /* not recovered: je */` — which is what an indeterminate machine value is, keeps the C valid, and makes a compiler's own `-Wuninitialized` point at exactly the places the recovery gave up. Each occurrence is numbered separately, because two conditions that both came from a `je` are two facts. An instruction with no C form emits `/* unlifted: rep movsd ... */;` verbatim rather than `__asm__("...")`, which gcc syntax-checks and then cannot assemble (2026-08-11 14:45)
- **String literals are escaped completely** — backslash, quote, `\a \b \t \n \v \f \r`, three-digit octal for control and high bytes (C's hex escapes are greedy and would absorb a following digit), UTF-8 octal above `0xFF`, and the second `?` of a `??` pair for trigraphs (2026-08-11 14:45)
- **A `do`/`while` body was a flat re-listing of its blocks, so branches inside it ran unconditionally** — the sibling of the `for` defect below, in the other arm of `structureLoop`. A loop whose header does not end in a two-way conditional (a bottom-tested loop, whose header ends in `jmp`) fell through to a fallback that concatenated every body block's lifted statements in block-id order and called that the body: both arms of an `if` inside the loop came out as consecutive unconditional code. Nothing is dropped, which is exactly why no statement-identity instrument saw it — the result was valid C stating something the machine does not do, the class of defect this project treats as its worst. The body is now walked by `structureFrom` like any other region, stopping at the header (the back edge) and at every block outside the loop a body block can reach; anything that walk does not reach is appended under its own pinned label, so the region is still complete. On t32/t64/w64 the emitted `if` count rises 1256→1286, 1577→1594 and 1375→1392, and nested `while`/`for` loops that the flat body had hidden appear for the first time (t64 `while` 166→174). Statement loss by object identity stays **0** on all three, dangling gotos stay 0, and per-guard condition polarity is unchanged (0 guards OK→bad; the two new non-OK are `while` headers of loops that were previously not emitted as loops at all, tracked as peek-a-bin-jlo). `sub_4031A4`'s critical-section init loop goes from nine unconditional statements to the four nested guards the machine actually has (peek-a-bin-b37) (2026-08-11 14:45)
- **A recovered `switch` states what it switches on** — `structureSwitch` looked for the subject only in a predecessor ending in `ja`/`jae`, so the inverted-sense bounds check MSVC emits for `memcpy`-style dispatch (`cmp ecx, 8` / `jb table`, where the in-range path is the branch *taken*) left the subject unrecovered and emitted `switch (/* switch_expr */)` — a switch with no subject, three of the four switches on t32. The bounds check is also not the subject in general: in `sub_40B780` the guard is `cmp ecx, 8` while the table is `jmp [edx*4 + 0x40b8f0]`, and `edx` is `ecx & 3`, a different value with a different range. The register the table is indexed by is now the primary reading — it selects the case by construction, so case *i* runs exactly when it holds *i* — with the bounds check kept as corroboration: when the two name the same register the comparison's spelling wins (and brings the default target with it), and when they disagree the index register is what is emitted. The inverted form yields no default, because its fallthrough is the out-of-range code, which is not an arm of the switch. t32's three unrecovered subjects become `switch (ecx)`, `switch (edx)`, `switch (edx)`, each verified case-by-case against the raw table. The recognition gate no longer also requires more than two distinct successor blocks, which silently rejected a table whose entries share targets (peek-a-bin-rev) (2026-08-11 14:45)
- **Every `goto` now has its label, including when the target block emitted nothing** — `emit.ts` could only anchor a label by matching the target address against the addresses of emitted lines, which finds nothing when the block's first instruction folded away or the block lifted to no statement at all: 405 of 553 gotos across the three distlib binaries. `structure.ts` knows where a block goes whether or not it produced a statement, so it now emits an `IRLabel` in front of every block it walks and sweeps away, at the end, the ones no `goto` targets — a label a reader would have to look up costs more than it gives. On t32/t64/w64: dangling gotos 392 → **0**, labels 279 → 646, `// no label: nothing was emitted for this address` annotations 392 → 0, and gcc's "label used but not defined" 337 → 25 — all 25 survivors being gcc's error recovery on `if (!/* je */ && …)`, an unrelated defect tracked as peek-a-bin-q4n, with no `loc_` among them. Labels nothing jumps to are unchanged at 147 (they mark the leftover regions the walk never reached), so the fix adds no noise (peek-a-bin-uzi) (2026-08-11 07:45)
- **A `for` loop's body was a flat re-listing of its blocks, so branches inside it ran unconditionally** — `detectForLoop` returns the body blocks' lifted statements concatenated in block-id order, and `structureCFG` used that verbatim in place of the body it had already structured. Every `if` inside the loop was flattened into consecutive unconditional statements, the header's own statements were left out entirely, and blocks belonging to the loop that had already been emitted elsewhere came back a second time (two t32 functions were four times longer than the code they describe: 29 copies of one statement in `sub_4045B1` alone). The structured body is used instead and only the recognition comes from `detectForLoop`. Statement loss measured by object identity across entry-reachable blocks falls to **0** on all three binaries (from 111/3/111), and the emitted statement-to-instruction ratio rises on t64 (0.660 → 0.673) and w64 (0.616 → 0.629) while falling on t32 (0.468 → 0.430) as the duplication goes away. Hoisting the update into the `for` header is now conditional on it being the last statement of the body and on the body holding no `continue` (which runs a `for`'s update but not the machine's back edge), and the initialiser moves into the header rather than being copied there — `x = f()` run twice is a different program. Loops failing a guard are emitted as the `while` they were built from (peek-a-bin-42l) (2026-08-11 07:45)
- **Struct synthesis turned the stack frame into an object** — two offsets off the frame pointer are two stack slots, not two fields, but `synthesizeStructs` grouped them like any other base: t32's `sub_4012EE`, a textbook `push ebp / mov ebp, esp` function, declared `struct_0 { PVOID field_neg_0x4; PVOID field_0x8; }` out of one local and argument 0. A frame base is now excluded, on either of two pieces of evidence that the frame pointer *is* one — a parameter stack.ts named `arg_<N>`, which it only does after verifying the prologue, or the `rbp = rsp` / `lea rbp, [rsp+N]` assignment itself where it survives to this pass. The stack pointer is excluded unconditionally: it is never an object. RBP is *not* excluded generally, because under frame-pointer omission it usually holds an object pointer and `[rbp+0x10]` genuinely is a field access — the two cases differ only by the prologue, and `pipeline.test.ts` pins both directions with identical bodies. Across t32/t64/w64: `esp->` 1157→0, `ebp->` 153→0, `rsp->` 280→0, `rbp->` 18→10 (the 10 survivors are real objects held in RBP, e.g. `mov rbp, rcx`), structs synthesised 185→115, and gcc's "invalid type argument of `->`" count on the emitted C drops 3491→1708. Emitted body statements are unchanged (24354 either way) — only fabricated declarations went away (peek-a-bin-a6n) (2026-08-11 07:42)
- **Stack slots in the first ten bytes of the frame were invisible to `analyzeStackFrame`** — Capstone prints a displacement as `0x`-prefixed hex only from 0xA up and as a bare digit below it, and the operand patterns matched only the `0x` form. On x86 that is exactly argument 0 (`[ebp + 8]`) and the first locals (`[ebp - 4]`, `[ebp - 8]`): t32's `sub_4012EE`, whose only parameter is reached through `push dword ptr [ebp + 8]`, was emitted as taking no arguments at all, and the unrecognised slots stayed raw derefs all the way to the output. Across the three binaries, functions with positional `arg_N` parameters go 88→161 and recovered stack locals 1902→2211 (peek-a-bin-a6n) (2026-08-11 07:42)
- **Jump-table case targets were registered as function starts, truncating the function containing the switch** — `detectFunctions` added every case target to the set of function *entry points*, and function sizes are derived as the gap to the next entry in that same set. So a switch's case bodies were split off into their own bogus "functions" and the function holding the `jmp [table]` ended at its first case. On t32.exe all 44 targets of all 10 tables fell outside their containing function; `sub_40B780` spanned `[0x40b780, 0x40b900)` while its targets were `0x40b900/08/14/28`. Downstream, `buildCFG`'s range guard then rejected every target as a block leader, the indirect-jmp block got **zero** successors, and `structureSwitch` was dead code on real input — no `switch` had ever been emitted for a real binary. A case target is now treated as a case label: kept out of the function set, and (like the `.pdata` suppression) allowed to outrank a byte-pattern guess at the same address, since case bodies routinely follow alignment padding. Evidence about an entry point still wins — a target that is also a call target, export, entry point, unwind handler or `.pdata` begin stays a function. t32 goes from 463 functions and 0 `switch` statements to 447 and 4 (20 case labels, 2 functions), removing exactly the 16 case-target pseudo-functions and widening exactly 2 real ones; t64/w64 have no jump tables and are unchanged (peek-a-bin-jy4) (2026-08-11 07:35)
- **A computation was inlined past a redefinition of one of its own inputs** — `foldBlock`'s single-use inlining scanned forward for the statement that reads the destination register and stopped only at a write to *that* register or at a call; it never checked the inlined expression's own operands. After `destroySSA` the version numbers are gone, so two SSA values of one register share a name and nothing downstream can distinguish them. On t64 `sub_1400010E8` this emitted `r9++` before the `cmove rbx, r9` that reads it — valid C stating the conditional move saw the incremented value. The scan now stops when a statement it would move past writes an input, and also when a memory load would be moved below a store (the addresses may alias and nothing in the IR can prove otherwise) (peek-a-bin-juu) (2026-08-11 07:02)
- **Global value numbering reused a value computed only in a sibling branch** — the available-expression table was flat while the walk was a dominator-tree preorder, so a definition stayed visible after its subtree was left. Where both arms of an `if` computed the same expression, the second arm's register was rewritten to the first's, the phi went trivial, and the assignment vanished from that arm — the emitted C then read the incoming value on a path where the machine code assigns it. The table is now pushed and popped per dominator-tree node. 39 functions on t64 change, all of the form `rcx = rax` (a register zeroed in a branch that did not run) becoming `rcx = 0` (2026-08-11 07:02)
- **`hasSideEffects` classified a cast-wrapped call as pure** — it was maintained separately in `fold.ts` and `ssaopt.ts` and both copies omitted the `cast` case, so `rbx = (int64_t)GetLastError()` read as side-effect-free; SSA dead-code elimination would delete the statement, and the call with it, whenever `rbx` had no remaining uses. There is now one definition, exported from `fold.ts`, written as a switch with a `never` exhaustiveness check so a new `IRExpr` kind cannot be classified by omission (2026-08-11 07:02)

- **ARM64 `.pdata` was decoded with the x64 schema** — `parsePdata` was gated on the PE32+ magic, and an ARM64 image is PE32+, so its 8-byte `{BeginAddress, UnwindData}` entries were read as 12-byte x64 `RUNTIME_FUNCTION`s. That is not a lost-flags bug: the stride desynchronises the table. On `t64-arm.exe` it reported 140 entries where there are 419, every one of them ending outside `.text` (the "end address" was really an `.xdata` RVA), 139 of 140 overlapping the previous entry, 91 not even 4-byte aligned, and all 140 carrying `handlerFlags` — with 138 `handlerAddress`es — invented out of ARM64 instruction bytes. The schema now follows `coffHeader.machine`: ARM64 (and the ARM64EC/ARM64X hybrids) decode packed unwind words and full `.xdata` records, including the extension word, the single-epilog form and the real handler RVA; everything else is read as x64 exactly as before. Verified against an independently written from-spec ARM64 reader — 419/419 and 381/381 entries agreeing on begin, end, unwind RVA and handler on the two ARM64 binaries — and against capstone's ARM64 decoder, which finds 90% of `.text` covered by ranges that are ascending, non-overlapping, 4-aligned and mostly ending in a return or branch (peek-a-bin-kwc) (2026-08-11 06:56)
- **x64 function detection reported the same function two or three times over** — the prologue-byte scan and the alignment-padding heuristic fired *inside* functions whose extent `.pdata` already gives exactly, adding a second entry 20-30 bytes into a function and 1-byte `push rbx` "functions" that decompiled to `void f() { }`. Where `.pdata` exists it is the linker's own record, so a pattern match strictly inside one of its functions is now dropped, while every other kind of evidence — a call or jump-table target, an export, the entry point, an unwind handler — is kept even inside a range. On `t64.exe`: 511 functions → 279, 232 overlapping pairs → 0, 93 fully-contained functions → 0, 40 functions of ≤4 bytes → 3, 53 empty decompiled bodies → 16; all 232 removed lay strictly inside a `.pdata` function, none was a `.pdata` start, and none had a name. Same shape on `w64.exe` (501 → 275) and a 17-section mingw binary (234 → 128). `t32.exe`, which ships no `.pdata`, is byte-for-byte unchanged at 463 functions and 18140 instructions (peek-a-bin-p3i) (2026-08-11 06:56)
- **Every emitted `goto` dangled: no label was ever written** — `structure.ts` emits `goto loc_<addr>` for a jump to an already-emitted block and nothing produced the matching label, so the pseudocode was not compilable C and the reader could not follow the jump (every one of the gotos in the three real binaries). `emitFunction` now places the label on the first emitted line carrying that address, wherever that line ended up — including inside a branch body, which is where these jumps actually go — and only for addresses something jumps to, so nothing is littered with labels. On a frozen snapshot of the three binaries (1017 functions, 456 gotos): 127 labels placed, 313 gotos still without one, and `gcc -std=gnu89 -fsyntax-only` over every emitted function reports 271 "label used but not defined" where it reported 398. The remainder is not an emitter bug and is deliberately not papered over: for 295 of them the target block produced no emitted line at all and for 110 only its first instruction did, so there is no honest anchor, and those gotos carry a `// no label: nothing was emitted for this address` comment instead of a fabricated destination (peek-a-bin-alc; the rest tracked as peek-a-bin-uzi) (2026-08-11 06:52)
- **A struct with an array field did not compile** — an `isArray` field was declared `uint16_t array_neg_0x2[];` wherever it fell in the layout, which is invalid C anywhere but last ("flexible array member not at end of struct", 16 such errors over the three binaries) and a worse reading than the layout supports. Fields are now emitted in offset order and an array field with another field after it is sized from the gap between them (`array_0x0[2]`), which is the extent the layout actually bounds; only a genuinely trailing field stays open-ended, because that is what a flexible array member means, and a sole field — which cannot be one — is declared as a single element and says so. Every flexible-array-member error in the corpus is gone (peek-a-bin-b2x) (2026-08-11 06:52)
- **Every worker RPC structured-cloned the entire loaded file** — byte arguments are views onto the whole file buffer (`new Uint8Array(buffer, section.pointerToRawData, section.sizeOfRawData)` and subarrays of it), and structured clone of a view serialises its whole `[[ViewedArrayBuffer]]`, so a 4 KiB single-function `disassemble` copied all 253 MiB. `disasmClient.send()` now routes args through `prepareBinaryArgs` (`src/workers/transfer.ts`), which replaces each top-level binary argument with a private `slice()` of exactly that view's window and transfers it. Measured with node's `structuredClone` (the algorithm `postMessage` runs), 253 MiB file / 200 MiB `.text`: `detectFunctions` 435→165 ms, one 4 KiB function 407→~0 ms, a whole file load's five calls 1868→706 ms. Slicing wins even when the copy is the same size (186→96 ms on a whole-buffer argument) because a clone costs about two passes where a slice costs one memcpy plus O(1) ownership transfer. It only ever transfers buffers it allocated itself — the main thread keeps reading the file buffer through `bufferRef`, `pe.buffer`, HexView and entropy, so transferring a caller's buffer would detach the loaded file. The walk is top-level only: an `Instruction[]` carries one tiny `bytes` buffer per element and transferring 500k of those measured 80.6 s against 1.6 s to clone them (2026-08-11 06:45)
- **A file that failed to parse stayed pinned in memory** — `bufferRef.current` was assigned before `parsePE()`, so dropping a 275 MB non-PE retained its whole buffer until some later load happened to replace it. Assigned after a successful parse now, and cleared on failure (2026-08-11 06:45)
- **`detectLoops` fabricated a loop for every `if` without an `else`** — back edges were approximated from BFS layers off the entry block ("the successor is on my layer or above"), which is true of the merge block of every triangle: the branch reaches the merge in one edge and so does the fallthrough body, so both sit on layer 1 and the body→merge edge looked like a back edge. A diamond is immune (its merge lands on layer 2), which is why every hand-written fixture passed. On three real MSVC binaries, 5883 of the 6308 loop headers reported did not exist (t64 2052/2375 false, t32 1618/1813, w64 1813/2120) and 492 of 1475 functions were given loops when they contained none; the worst case *deleted a guard*, turning a conditional store into an unconditional one. `detectLoops` now reuses the dominance-based `detectNaturalLoops` that already lived in `decompile/ssa.ts` for the SSA optimiser, so the tree holds one notion of "loop" instead of two that disagree. False headers drop to 2/3/2, none genuine are lost, and nesting depth is now computed from loop-body containment rather than from address-range overlap. The disassembly view's loop markers come from the same function and improve with it (2026-08-11 06:20)
- **A guard was dropped when its `if` had no `else`** — `structureCFG` treats "one arm ends in `ret`" as an early-return pattern, but a triangle's branch target *is* the shared tail, so that arm structured as an empty `then` body and the guard was discarded, leaving the other arm's statements unconditional. Both early-return shortcuts now require the arm not to be the convergence point. Convergence itself is now the immediate post-dominator (dominators of the reversed CFG, rooted at a virtual exit) instead of the first block a BFS from one arm happened to reach inside the other's reachable set, which for two `if`s in a row picked the *second* merge, nested the second `if` inside the first and left a `goto` into the middle of it. Measured across the three binaries: emitted statements per instruction 0.46/0.45/0.48 → 0.62/0.52/0.58, fabricated `while`s 1277/828/1228 → 179/147/169, and strict guard-polarity checking finds 1309 of 1336 emitted guards correct with 4 inverted, against 659 of 745 with 59 inverted before (2026-08-11 06:20)
- **`__try` was never emitted** — `wrapExceptionRegions` compared `func.address`, a VA, against `RuntimeFunction.beginAddress`, an RVA straight out of `.pdata`; on t64.exe all 240 entries match `beginAddress + imageBase` and none match the raw RVA, so no function was ever wrapped despite 50 handlers being present. The image base is not plumbed as far as the pipeline, so it is recovered from the pair — a VA and its RVA differ by the image base, which the PE spec requires to be 64K-aligned — and an ambiguous match is discarded rather than guessed at, since a `__try` attributed to the wrong function is worse than a missing one. The handler address in the emitted comment is rebased to a VA to match every other address in the pane. t64 now wraps 50 functions and w64 46 (2026-08-11 06:20)
- **A tail call was emitted as `do { } while (1);`** — t64's `sub_140002680` ends `add rsp, 0x28 / jmp 0x140002208` and came out as an infinite loop wrapping the whole function. It was the fabricated-loop bug: the `jmp` leaves the function and creates no edge at all, so nothing was looping. Fixed by the same change; the tail call itself is still not emitted as a call (2026-08-11 06:20)
- **IOCTL comments on constants that are not IOCTLs** — the emitter decoded every constant it printed, and `isPlausibleIOCTL` is a shape test that a large share of ordinary 32-bit values pass: any device type in `0x01..0x45` or `>= 0x8000`, any function code, any method. In pip's user-mode launcher stubs that put 817 `/* IOCTL: ... */` comments into t32 (40% of its functions), 351 into t64 and 307 into w64 — `0x412284`, a data address, decoded as BLUETOOTH, and `0xFFFFFFFF` decoded as a custom device — naming devices and function codes that do not exist. Decoding now requires the call site as well as the shape: the constant has to be the argument that carries a control code (`DeviceIoControl` arg 1, `Nt`/`ZwDeviceIoControlFile` and `Nt`/`ZwFsControlFile` arg 5, `IoBuildDeviceIoControlRequest` arg 0, the WDF pair arg 2 — `ioctlCodeArgIndex` in `src/analysis/driver.ts`, which sees through `__imp_` and `@N` decoration). All 1475 bogus comments are gone and a real `DeviceIoControl(h, 0x222000, ...)` still decodes. The disassembly-view annotation in `functionDetect.ts` already had a `driverMode` gate and is unchanged (2026-08-11 06:10)
- **Emitted typedefs did not compile** — a struct field can point at a struct declared later in the block (t64's `sub_14000228C` declared `struct_7* field_0x10;` inside struct_6, above struct_7), and the anonymous `typedef struct { ... } struct_N;` form has no way to forward-declare. Every struct name is now forward-declared above the definitions, which are written in tag form (`struct struct_6 { ... };`). Forward declarations rather than a topological sort because synthesis produces cycles — a self-referencing linked-list node has no valid order — and because `IRFunction.typedefs` only snapshots the structs this function touched, so a field can reference one that is never defined in the block at all. Compiling the extracted struct blocks from all three binaries with `gcc -std=c99 -fsyntax-only`: 70 "unknown type name" errors before, 0 after (2026-08-11 06:10)
- **Hook dependency arrays completed across the components and hooks layer** — all 94 `useExhaustiveDependencies` findings cleared. Most were scope artefacts of the hook extractions: a value a callback used to close over becomes a parameter, and Biome can prove a `useState` setter or a `useRef` is stable but cannot prove a prop is, so the identical code became a warning after moving. Those additions are free by construction. Three were not artefacts and were genuine latent stale closures: `handleKeyDown` read `highlightedReg` without listing it, `triggerAI` cached AI output under the previous function's address after a navigation, and the section-disassembly effect keyed on `hexPatches.size` so a patch overwriting an already-patched offset never triggered a re-disassemble. `funcMap` was a dead entry in `handleKeyDown`'s array — referenced nowhere in the body — and the parameter went with it. Nine arrays that deliberately omit a value to stop an effect chasing its own output (the CFG auto-pan/auto-center effects write the `pan`/`zoom` they read) keep the omission and carry a single-line `biome-ignore` with the reason. Nothing renders a component in this repo, so this is verified by typecheck, lint, the non-rendering suite, a production build and reading (2026-08-11 06:05)
- **Command palette results were up to 60 buttons in the tab order** — every result row rendered as a real `<button>`, so Tab from the search field walked the entire result list instead of reaching the next control, and the modal focus trap dutifully cycled through all of it. It was always a listbox: the arrow keys move a selection, Enter activates it, and focus never leaves the input. The rows are now `role="option"` with `aria-selected` inside a `role="listbox"`, and the input is a `combobox` naming the active row via `aria-activedescendant` — which also fixes the screen-reader side, where the selection highlight was purely visual and was never announced at all (2026-08-11 05:02)

- **The Stack Frame panel showed every parameter at a negative offset** — `InstructionDetail` rendered `-0x{offset}` for every entry, but `StackVar.offset` is the operand value as written and is documented "always positive", with the direction carried separately. That list holds locals below the frame pointer *and* parameters above it, so a parameter at `[rbp+0x10]` displayed as `-0x10`, as did any `[rsp+0x10]` local. `StackVar` now exposes a signed `signedOffset` and the panel renders the sign from it (2026-08-10 22:45)
- **Struct field types were overwritten by guesses instead of refined** — two heuristics in `synthesizeStructs` fired unconditionally: any pointer-sized field whose value was passed to any function became `PVOID`, and storing a loaded value into a field made that field a pointer. Both clobbered better-founded information, so a field already identified correctly was silently downgraded to "pointer to nothing". They now refine through `meetTypes()` — the same lattice `typeInfer.ts` uses, rather than a second inconsistent notion of "more specific" — and consult the ~130 real signatures in `apitypes.ts` first: a field passed to `Sleep` is a `uint32_t`, one passed to `CloseHandle` is a `HANDLE`. The pointer guess survives only where nothing better is known, i.e. an unknown callee. A field-to-field copy of a scalar no longer becomes a pointer, since a value having come from memory says nothing about whether it is an address. Demonstrated in `pipeline.test.ts` against the emitted `typedef struct` block rather than an IR node (2026-08-10 22:25)
- **Modal dialogs took no account of what Escape would throw away** — Escape and backdrop click are now on wherever they are safe, and off where the keystroke destroys something: the batch-rename error dialog gained both, the batch-rename progress dialog and review table keep neither (`dismiss` is `BATCH_RENAME_DISMISS`, which drops a whole decompile-plus-LLM run and every accept/reject the user has clicked), and `AIReportPanel` takes them only once the report has finished — a finished report is in `localStorage` and costs nothing to close, a streaming one is not. Settings remains deliberately non-dismissible. The rule is one pure function, `accidentalDismissAllowed`, rather than a per-dialog boolean nobody can audit (2026-08-10 22:50)
- **Nothing stopped the page scrolling behind an open dialog** — `Modal` now locks `<body>` for as long as any dialog is up: a depth counter, not a flag, so the first of two dialogs to close does not unlock the page underneath the second; the inline `overflow`/`padding-right` captured on the *first* lock only and restored verbatim, so a nested lock cannot save the already-locked values and freeze the page for ever; and the vanishing scrollbar's width added back as padding so the viewport does not widen and shift the page sideways. In this app it is currently a no-op — `body` is already `overflow: hidden; height: 100vh` from CSS and the full-viewport wrapper swallows the wheel before it reaches the inner `overflow-auto` panes — so it is insurance against a stylesheet change rather than a fix for a visible bug (2026-08-10 22:50)
- **Dialog names could drift from the headings above them** — five of the six dialogs rendered an `h2`/`h3` and separately passed the same words as an `aria-label`, so a screen reader could announce a name no sighted user could see. `Modal` takes `labelledBy` (an id on the dialog's own heading) or `label` (a string) but never both, enforced by the prop type. `GoToAddressModal`, `KeyboardShortcuts`, `SettingsModal`, `AIReportPanel` and all three `BatchRenameModal` states now point at their headings; `CommandPalette` keeps a string, having no heading to point at (2026-08-10 22:50)
- **Modal dialogs did not trap focus** — none of the six did, so a keyboard or screen-reader user who Tabbed inside an open dialog walked straight out of it into the page behind, with the dim still covering everything and no way to tell where focus had gone. `Modal` now moves focus into the dialog on open, wraps Tab and Shift+Tab at the ends of its focusable list, and returns focus to the triggering control on close instead of dropping it on `<body>`. Only the ends of the list are intercepted; movement inside it is left to the browser, which is what keeps radio groups behaving. The keyboard-shortcuts panel contains no controls at all, so the dialog box itself takes focus. `BatchRenameModal` keys its three states so the trap re-arms when the progress dialog is replaced by the review table — otherwise the focused Cancel button is unmounted underneath the trap. `BatchRenameModal` and `AIReportPanel` also gained the `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal` / label they were missing entirely (2026-08-10 22:22)

- **The High Level tab showed "not available" forever after a Ghidra server was enabled** — the placeholder was written into the result cache as though it were a successful decompilation, so once a user saw it, configuring a server changed nothing: the stale placeholder was served from cache for every function already visited. Two independent fixes, because one alone would rot. `writeHighCache` now refuses to store an `engine: "none"` entry at all — a failure is not a result. And each entry records the backend identity that produced it (`decompileServerKey`), compared at **read** time, so enabling, disabling, re-pointing the URL or changing the API key all invalidate without any settings path needing to remember to call an invalidator (2026-08-10 22:15)
- **D opened the decompile panel but could not close it** — `handleKeyDown` closed over `handleDecompileToggle`, which is declared ~340 lines later and depends on `showDecompile`, `instructions`, `decompile` and `viewMode`, none of which appear in `handleKeyDown`'s 19-entry dependency array. Pressing D set `showDecompile` true, but `handleKeyDown` was never re-memoized, so it kept the stale closure where `showDecompile` was still false and re-ran the open branch. The panel only became closable once something else in that dependency array changed — moving the cursor, for instance. The toolbar Decompile button was unaffected, since it receives the current callback directly. Fixed with a ref assigned during render; adding the callback to the dependency array is impossible (it is a `const` declared later, so the array would hit its temporal dead zone) and hoisting it would drag `useDecompileTabs` above that point, reordering hooks in a component with no test coverage (2026-08-10 22:06)
- **Stack parameter names encoded the order a function touched its arguments, not their position** — `stack.ts` numbered slots with a counter incremented per *observed* slot, so a function that never reads its first argument called its second one `arg_0`. Misleading in the emitted pseudocode, where `arg_0` reasonably reads as "the first argument", and unusable as an index by anything else — it had blocked stack-argument struct provenance entirely. N is now derived from the slot offset. **The numbering is gated on a verified frame-pointer prologue** (`push rbp` / `mov rbp, rsp`, seeing through hot-patch padding): under frame-pointer omission RBP is more often a callee-saved object pointer than a frame pointer, so `[rbp+0x10]` is frequently a struct field access rather than a parameter, and numbering those would manufacture false struct merges. A slot in an unverified frame is named after its offset (`arg_0x10`) instead, which the provenance path deliberately does not match. On x64 the first slot is `[rbp+0x10]`, the ABI home slot for the argument passed in RCX, so the fifth argument lands at `[rbp+0x30]` past the shadow space (2026-08-10 22:03)
- **Every `if`, `if/else` and `while` in the decompiler printed the negation of its real condition** — `structureCFG` built the general conditional as `if (negate(condition)) { branchTarget } else { fallthrough }`, but `condition` is the condition under which the conditional jump is *taken*, and `branchTarget` is the block reached when it is taken. So the guard was inverted while the bodies stayed in place: a `je` on `ecx == 0` emitted `if (ecx != 0)` around the block that actually runs when `ecx` **is** zero. Loop headers had the same inversion. Silent — the output was always plausible C, just describing the opposite of the binary. Three things independently confirm the taken-polarity reading: `RegState.negate`'s own docstring ("for structuring: if-not-taken path"), the `cmov` lifter feeding `getCondition` straight into `ternary(cond, src, dest)`, and the short-circuit path *in the same function*, which already emitted `if (scCond) { trueTarget } else { falseTarget }` un-negated. Every jcc family in `condMap` is taken-polarity, so the inversion was uniform rather than family-specific (2026-08-10 19:50)

- **Struct declarations changed retroactively after they were emitted** — `synthesizeStructs` handed a function's `typedefs` straight out of the registry, and a later function's inference pass then mutated those same objects in place. A function's struct declaration therefore depended on what was decompiled *after* it. Escaping definitions are now snapshots (`cloneStructDef`); registry-internal objects stay shared, because that in-place mutation is how one function inherits a field type another discovered. `mergeFields` also copies incoming fields instead of adopting the caller's array (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **Unrelated structs were conflated across functions** — the only evidence for "same type" was the set of `offset:size` pairs, so any two-field shape nesting inside a larger one merged session-wide; `{0:8, 8:8}` describes a large fraction of all structs. The subset merge path now requires 3+ shared fields and rejects layouts whose extents overlap. Exact fingerprint matches still merge unconditionally. **Cost, deliberate:** a two-field partial view no longer completes from another function's larger view — two `struct_N` declarations instead of one wrongly shared (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **Array-access rewriting was unreachable on its own** — `synthesizeStructs` returned early whenever no base had 2+ distinct offsets, so `base + i*4` became `IRArrayAccess` only in functions that happened to contain a struct elsewhere. Indexed accesses now rewrite regardless (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **`base - offset` was never recognised as a struct access** — only `+` chains decomposed. A top-level subtraction of a constant now folds to a negative offset; subtracting a register still returns null. Negative offsets are named `field_neg_0x8`, since `field_0x-8` is not a valid C identifier and the name is emitted verbatim (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **Accesses were credited to the wrong struct base** — the alias map is flow-insensitive and let the last copy in the function win, so reads made through a register while it aliased one base were attributed to whatever it was reassigned to later. A name assigned from two different sources, or overwritten by anything that is not a plain copy, is now dropped from the map instead (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **Import names were parsed as call-target addresses** — `parseInt(target, 16)` stops at the first non-hex character, so `CloseHandle` became `0xC` and `ExitProcess` `0xE`, recording parameter links against addresses that collided with each other and with real functions. Only `sub_<hex>` is treated as an address. Impact was latent — `getParamStruct` has no callers yet — but the map was being filled with nonsense (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **Signedness evidence inside `try` bodies and `for` conditions was ignored** — the signed-comparison walker recursed into every compound statement except `try`, and never looked at a `for` statement's condition (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **Struct accesses nested in an assignment destination were dropped** — the access-pattern collector walked an assignment's destination only when it was a bare `deref`, missing accesses nested inside a `field_access`, `array_access` or arithmetic destination (2026-08-10 18:42)

- **Resource parser could be made to exhaust the heap** — `MAX_DEPTH` did not bound the work. Each directory declares its own entry count in two uint16s, so one can claim 131070 children, and each child can point at a *distinct* subdirectory offset that the `visited` set cannot collapse. A 256 KB crafted `.rsrc` exhausted a 4 GB heap in under a minute; in a browser that is a dead tab, on a tool whose purpose is opening untrusted binaries. Now bounded by a global entry budget, surfaced as `ResourceTree.truncated`. Resource strings are also clamped, since `String.fromCharCode(...chars)` with a 65535-element spread is at or over some engines' argument limit (2026-08-10 14:51)
- **imphash was wrong for every ordinal-imported ws2_32 binary** — `ORDINAL_DLL_MAP` recorded ordinal 9 as `getpeername`; it is `htons` (`getpeername` is ordinal 5). The resulting imphash disagreed with pefile and every corpus built on it, so it matched nothing — which is imphash's only purpose. Nothing cross-checks a hash at runtime, so this would not have surfaced on its own (2026-08-10 14:51)
- **`parseRichHeader` crashed the Header tab** — any file ending in the four bytes `Rich` was accepted as a marker without checking the 4-byte XOR key behind it existed, throwing a `RangeError`. `HeaderView` calls it during render, so the whole tab went blank (2026-08-10 14:51)
- **"Go to address" scrolled to the wrong row** — `binarySearchRows` treated a separator row as "too high" and discarded the entire right half of the search space. Separators sit between every pair of functions, so any realistic listing has one on the search path (2026-08-10 14:56)
- **Every reconstructed 32-bit call had its arguments backwards** — `collectArgs32` walked backwards from the call and then reversed the result, but walking backwards already yields argument order: cdecl pushes right-to-left, so the push nearest the call is argument 1 (2026-08-10 14:47)
- **`isRegister()` matched every operand** — it tested `regSize(s) > 0`, and `regSize()` falls back to `4` for unrecognised names, so the predicate was true for every string and immediates were lifted as registers. Replaced with a real `isKnownRegister()` membership test (2026-08-10 14:47)
- **Function parameter counts were unreliable** — `signatures.ts` matched register names by substring and checked reads before writes, so the standard `xor rcx, rcx` zeroing idiom counted as a parameter, and `mov eax, edx` yielded 0 params while `mov eax, r8d` yielded 3 (`'r8d'.includes('r8')` is true, `'edx'.includes('rdx')` is not). Replaced with explicit per-instruction read/write effect sets over whole-word tokens, width-aware so an 8/16-bit write no longer counts as killing the parent register. `paramCount` feeds the LLM prompts and MCP output, so wrong arity propagated into AI analysis (2026-08-10 14:16)
- **Stale constants folded across calls** — `invalidateCallerSaved()` deleted only the 64-bit register names, but the lifter keys definitions by literal operand text (`ecx`, `r8d`, `cl`), so `mov ecx, 0x1234 / call f / mov edx, ecx` kept the stale constant. In 32-bit mode nothing was invalidated at all, since the return register is literally `eax` (2026-08-10 14:16)
- **Exported ordinals were off by the Ordinal Base** — the Export Directory's Base field at offset 16 was never read, so every ordinal displayed was one lower than dumpbin and pefile report. `parseExports` now walks the export *address* table rather than the name-pointer table, which also makes ordinal-only exports visible (common in real DLLs) and lets forwarder exports be detected rather than reported as code addresses (2026-08-10 14:18)
- **Vulnerability scans reported failures as clean results** — a malformed LLM response was parsed inside a `catch {}` and became an empty finding list, rendering identically to a scan that found nothing. On a security tool this reads as a clean bill of health. Scan outcome is now an explicit state machine that distinguishes failed, partial and genuinely-clean runs (2026-08-10 14:40)
- **A single 429 permanently disabled scanning** — a rejected `streamChat` propagated out of the bulk-scan loop uncaught, killing the run *and* leaving `scanningRef.current` true forever, so every subsequent scan was blocked until reload (2026-08-10 14:30)
- **The bulk scan cancelled itself** — `scanSuspicious` set `abortRef`, then called `scanFunction`, which overwrote it, aborting the run's own controller on the first iteration. All four AI hooks now abort the previous controller before replacing it, rather than orphaning in-flight requests (2026-08-10 14:30)
- **A malformed `Retry-After` meant "retry immediately"** — `Date.parse("-5")` succeeds, so a negative header value parsed as a date and produced a zero delay against an already-struggling server (2026-08-10 14:30)
- **Batch-rename modal reported the wrong phase** — `status === "decompiling" ? "decompiling" : "running"` can only re-select the value it just tested, so `"running"` was unreachable and the modal claimed "Decompiling functions…" for the entire LLM phase (2026-08-10 14:56)
- **PWA precached the Capstone WASM twice** — 1.7 MiB of the 4.7 MiB precache was a byte-identical duplicate. The cause was the `copy-capstone-wasm` plugin itself: Rollup already rewrites `new URL("capstone.wasm", import.meta.url)` to its hashed asset, so the manual copy was pure duplication. Removed, and replaced with a guard that fails the build if more than one WASM asset is emitted or if the emitted one is unreferenced (2026-08-10 14:19)
- **Negated `jcc` mnemonics had blank tooltips** — `jnle`, `jnl`, `jnge`, `jng`, `jnbe`, `jnb`, `jnc`, `jnae`, `jc`, `jna`, `jpe` and `jpo` are treated as branches by `buildCFG` but had no `MNEMONIC_HINTS` entry. A test now asserts every conditional jump has one (2026-08-10 14:12)
- **`[rip+disp]` operands could report a spurious second xref** — the RIP-relative case computed its target, then the generic hex scan re-consumed the same displacement. Unreachable at realistic image bases, where the displacement is filtered out as below the base (2026-08-10 14:12)
- **DER timestamps rendered as `NaN-ab-cd`** — `parseUTCTime`/`parseGeneralizedTime` checked only string length, not that the characters were digits, on attacker-controlled Authenticode data (2026-08-10 14:51)
- **`parseImports` could read past the import directory** — the descriptor walk was bounded by the null descriptor and EOF but not by the directory's own declared size (2026-08-10 14:18)
- **PE parser crashed on unmapped import thunks** — an `OriginalFirstThunk` RVA outside every section resolved to `-1` and was used unguarded, throwing an uncaught `RangeError` that failed the entire file load (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **PE parser froze on a hostile export table** — the name-pointer walk was bounded only by `numberOfNames`, read straight off the file as a uint32; `0xFFFFFFFF` spun ~4.3 billion times, measured at ~20 seconds of blocked main thread. Now clamped to what the buffer can hold, and out-of-range indices `break` instead of `continue` (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Authenticode DER parser killed the worker on a crafted certificate** — `contentLen = (contentLen << 8) | byte` is signed-32 in JS, so a 4-byte length of `0x80000000` became `-2147483648`, driving `readDERChildren` backwards through the buffer. Length accumulation no longer uses signed shifts, and out-of-range lengths are rejected (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Unbounded header counts** — `numberOfSections` (uint16) and `numberOfRvaAndSizes` (uint32) are now clamped to what the buffer can hold, and to the 16-entry spec maximum for data directories (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Truncated files threw bare `RangeError`s** — short buffers and PEs whose COFF header runs past EOF now report a usable parse error (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Unsanitized markdown rendering** — `MarkdownRenderer` passed `marked` output straight to `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. That content is LLM output which routinely echoes strings extracted from the analysed binary, so attacker-controlled text from an untrusted PE reached raw HTML injection on an origin holding plaintext LLM API keys. Now sanitized with DOMPurify (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Conditional hook call** — `useContainingFunc` called `useSortedFuncs()` behind a `??`, a Rules-of-Hooks violation that breaks under React Compiler. The redundant `sortedFuncs` parameter was removed (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Infinite loop in dominance-frontier and loop detection** — `computeDomFrontier` and the `dominates()` helper in `detectNaturalLoops` walk an `idom` chain unbounded. `computeDomFrontier` takes `idom` as a public parameter, so a cyclic or non-entry-rooted map spins the worker thread at 100% CPU forever. Both walks are now bounded, and `computeDomFrontier`'s `df.get(runner)!` no longer crashes when `runner` is not a block (2026-08-10 13:29)
- **Worker RPC could hang forever** — `send()` had no timeout, so a wedged worker left every pending *and future* request unsettled with no recovery. Added a 5-minute watchdog (flat rather than per-method: the worker services messages serially, so a cheap call can legitimately queue behind a whole-image disassembly), plus `onmessageerror` handling and cleanup of the pending-request entry and its timer (2026-08-10 13:29)
- **Unknown worker methods resolved with `undefined`** — the dispatch `switch` had no `default`, so a caller's promise silently resolved rather than rejecting. It now throws through the existing error path, and a `never` binding makes an unhandled method a compile error (2026-08-10 13:29)
- **`phi` statements emitted nothing** — `emitStmt` had no `phi` case, so a phi surviving SSA destruction vanished silently from the output. It now emits a visible `/* unresolved phi: … */` comment rather than a guessed value, since a surviving phi indicates incomplete SSA destruction (2026-08-10 13:29)
- **SSA renaming dropped struct and array accesses** — `renameExpr`, `stripVersionsExpr` and `stmtUses`'s walker did not recurse into `field_access` / `array_access`, so versions were left unrenamed and liveness under-reported. Latent because `structs.ts` runs after `destroySSA`; a pipeline reorder would have made it live. `never` exhaustiveness checks added to 8 IR switches so the omission class becomes a compile error (2026-08-10 13:29)
- **Stack slot key collision** — `[rbp-0x10]` (local) and `[rsp+0x10]` (param slot) shared one `varMap` entry keyed on the raw offset, merging their sizes and access counts. Now keyed per base. `promote.ts` keyed its lookups on the bare offset too, so both slots still promoted to a single variable in emitted C — fixed there as well (2026-08-10 13:29)
- **`Ctrl+Shift+A` never opened the AI chat** — it toggled a `chatOpen` state in `App` that nothing read; real visibility lives in `DisassemblyView`. It now dispatches `peek-a-bin:open-chat`, as the toolbar button already did (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **Panel resizes persisted the pre-drag width** — `ResizeHandle` registers its mousemove/mouseup listeners once per drag, so `onResizeEnd` closed over the callback as of *mousedown*. Callers pass inline arrows reading width from their render scope, so every resize wrote the old value to localStorage. Callbacks now route through refs (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **Two rival `?` overlays opened at once** — `DisassemblyView` rendered a second, independently hardcoded shortcuts panel. Both were window-level `keydown` listeners, and `stopPropagation()` does not stop other listeners on the same target. The duplicate is deleted and `SHORTCUT_GROUPS` in `KeyboardShortcuts.tsx` is now the single source of truth (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **Cancelling an LLM stream showed a spurious "Network error"** — `streamSSE` never saw the `AbortSignal`, so the aborted reader's rejection was reported as an error after the caller had already settled. Also removed a `cancelAnimationFrame(0)` that could never cancel anything (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **Anthropic requests ignored the configured base URL** — `buildUrl` hardcoded `api.anthropic.com`, so Anthropic-compatible gateways could not be used, and the per-profile default was the nonsensical `https://api.openai.com`. The default is corrected and custom gateways are honoured; the old OpenAI value is still treated as "use the default" so saved profiles keep working (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **A failed analysis left the UI spinning forever** — the `configure → detectFunctions → disassemble → buildAllXrefs` chain had no rejection handler, pinning `analysisPhase` on its last value. Added a `failed` phase surfaced in the status bar, plus handlers on the string-extraction and xref-rebuild chains (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **Stale-closure dependency** — the typed-xref effect compared against a `typedXrefMap` captured from an earlier render while depending only on `instructions`; it now compares against `prev` in the state updater (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **11 hooks called after an early return** — `HeaderView` (7), `ExportsView` (2), `ResourcesView` (1) and `SectionTable` (1) ran `useMemo`/`useState`/`useCallback`/`useVirtualizer` below an `if (!pe) return null` guard, so the hook count changed between renders. Hooks are now hoisted above the guard with null-tolerant bodies, and `useHookAtTopLevel` is enforced at error level (2026-08-10 13:00)
- **Keyboard docs corrected** — `docs/keyboard.md` documented all four panel keys wrong (`X` as Xrefs, `R` as Resources, `I` as Imports, `D` as Detail; actually call panel, xref panel, instruction detail and decompiler), listed tabs as `1`–`8` plus a separate `9` when the binding is `1`–`9`, and conflated `G` (focus address input) with `Ctrl+G` (go-to dialog) (2026-08-10 13:13)
- **Hardening without a demonstrated bug (recorded for honesty)** — `intersect()` in `ssa.ts` and the `emitFunction` module-state save/restore were both changed defensively, but neither fixed observable behaviour. `idom`'s values are always also its keys, so the `intersect()` walk cannot dereference `undefined` (~1M randomised CFGs, including irreducible and stale-RPO inputs, produced no hang); and `emitFunction` reassigns both module globals unconditionally on entry, so a leaked value is always overwritten before it can be read. Their tests are termination and invariant guards, not bug repros, and say so in comments (2026-08-10 13:29)
- **Ghidra NotFoundException on binary import** — Ghidra 12 throws `NotFoundException` (not `IOException`) when `openProject()` fails, breaking pyhidra's fallback to `createProject()`; server now catches `NotFoundException` and falls back to direct Java API calls (`GhidraProject.createProject` + `importProgram` + `analyze`), bypassing pyhidra's broken exception handling; stale project directories also pre-cleaned unconditionally; `WindowsResourceReferenceAnalyzer` disabled in fallback path to prevent NPE from uninitialized OSGi BundleHost, and `analyze()` wrapped in try/except so any analyzer crash is non-fatal (2026-03-27 15:30)
- **`;` hotkey in DecompileView** — `preventDefault` now only fires when a matching address is found; added `stopPropagation` to prevent duplicate comment editor in wrong view (2026-03-07 14:58)
- **`?` duplicate shortcut pane** — added `stopPropagation` to prevent event from bubbling and toggling the overlay twice (2026-03-07 14:58)
- **Graph mode hotkey focus** — `cfgContainerRef` now auto-focuses when entering graph mode and on mousedown clicks, so `;`, `?`, `B`, `N`, `I`, `R` etc. work reliably (2026-03-07 14:58)
- **Ghidra Docker image** — Dockerfile now downloads and installs Ghidra 11.3.2 with JDK 21; forces `linux/amd64` platform (Ghidra has no `linux_arm_64` decompiler binary); fixes container crash on startup due to missing `GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR`; native decompiler binaries `chmod +x` after unzip; `DecompInterface.openProgram()` return value checked; server error details now surfaced in client UI (2026-03-06 15:40)
- **Settings modal no longer dismissible by accident** — removed backdrop click and Escape key handlers; modal now only closes via Save or Cancel buttons

### Removed

- **`src/decompile/wasmDecompiler.ts` stub** — `decompileWithWasm` ignored all three of its parameters and unconditionally returned a fixed comment string, so `useDecompileTabs` awaited a promise that could not fail inside a `try`/`catch` that could not fire. The message it returned is now a named constant at the point of use, so the High Level tab shows exactly what it showed before — the same two-line placeholder body plus the existing "(not available)" engine indicator — with no fake async in the path (2026-08-10 19:37)
- **Dead nested-struct resolution loop** — `findNestedStructUse` unconditionally returned `null`, so the three-round nested-struct loop in `synthesizeStructs` never did anything. Removed rather than left in place implying a capability that does not exist; real nested-struct detection is tracked separately (2026-08-10 18:42)
- **Dead code** — ~60 unused imports, variables, parameters and functions, including `useDecompileTabs`'s `highlightLines` (always returned an empty Set) and `handleLineClick` (a no-op), plus `CFGView`'s `onNavBack` prop, which was declared and destructured but never invoked — making the 20-line handler `DisassemblyView` passed to it unreachable (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Broken `bin` entry** — `package.json` declared `peek-a-bin-mcp` pointing at a raw `.ts` file whose `#!/usr/bin/env npx tsx` shebang cannot work on Linux, in a `private: true` package that can never be published. All client configs invoke the server via `npx tsx` explicitly (2026-08-10 05:19)
- **Blocks button** — removed block tinting toggle and alternating block backgrounds from disassembly toolbar (2026-03-07 14:17)
- **Compact button** — removed compact density mode toggle; fixed row height at 20px; removed `.density-compact` CSS (2026-03-07 14:17)
- CFG modal overlay — replaced by the inline graph view

## [0.0.2-alpha] - 2026-03-04

### Added

- **Resource Directory parsing** — full recursive tree walk of PE resource directories with support for named and ID-based entries, depth-limited traversal, and circular reference protection (`src/pe/resources.ts`)
- **ResourcesView component** — tree view UI grouped by resource type (Icon, Version Info, Manifest, etc.) with expand/collapse, size/RVA display, and per-entry download button
- **Version Info extraction** — parses VS_VERSIONINFO structures to display FileVersion, ProductName, CompanyName, and other StringFileInfo key-value pairs
- **Icon reconstruction** — reconstructs valid `.ico` files from RT_GROUP_ICON + RT_ICON resource entries with inline preview
- **Manifest display** — renders RT_MANIFEST XML content in a scrollable `<pre>` block
- **Resources tab** — accessible via keyboard shortcut `8`, added to tab bar and routing
- **Keyboard Shortcuts panel** — press `?` to toggle a modal overlay showing all 12 keyboard shortcuts grouped by category (Navigation, Annotations, Search, Clipboard, Disassembly)
- **Unified analysis export** — export now includes hex patches as `[offset, value]` tuples and detected functions alongside bookmarks, renames, and comments (v1 schema with `version` field)
- **Unified analysis import** — imports v1 schema files with full hex patch and annotation merging; maintains backward compatibility with legacy annotation-only JSON files
- **Unit tests** — 27 tests covering PE32/PE64 parsing, DOS/PE signature validation, COFF fields, imageBase, section headers, `rvaToFileOffset`, truncated buffers, and `.pdata` exception directory parsing
- **Test fixture builder** — `buildMinimalPE32()` and `buildMinimalPE64()` construct valid PE buffers programmatically for testing without binary fixture files
- **PWA / offline support** — service worker via `vite-plugin-pwa` precaches all assets including `capstone.wasm` (~2 MB) for full offline functionality; app is installable as a standalone PWA
- **PWA manifest and icons** — `manifest.json` with 192px and 512px icons, theme color `#111827`

### Changed

- Export filename changed from `{fileName}.json` to `{fileName}-analysis.json`
- Export format now uses versioned schema (`version: 1`) for forward compatibility
- Page title in `index.html` updated from "Web Disassembly Viewer" to "Peek-a-Bin"

## [0.0.1-alpha] - 2026-03-03

Initial tagged alpha. `CHANGELOG.md` was first added after this tag, so no itemised entries were ever recorded for it; run `git log v0.0.1-alpha` for the commit history it covers.
